# Production parity drive

Date: 2026-07-30
Mode: Build under `/goal` + `loop-build`
Branch: `task/20260730-TSK-HUM-rvp9-production-parity-with-pinned-libvp`
Worktree: `/Users/md/worktrees/rvp9-2/20260730-TSK-HUM-rvp9-production-parity-with-pinned-libvp`

## Objective

Bring the scoped one-pass realtime VP9 encoder matrix to reproducible production
parity with pinned libvpx v1.14.1. Final gates are correctness, aggregate PSNR
BD-rate no worse than +0.5%, no important unexplained clip regression above
+2%, corpus geomean encode time no worse than 1.05x vpxenc, and the absolute
720p30/1080p30/1080p60 latency budgets.

## Canonical open stage

`PLAN.md` leaves Stage 4a open. Its immediate exits are:

- cpu-used 7, realtime, one-pass VBR, lag 0, tune-content default;
- 720p30 median no worse than 33 ms;
- BD-rate within +2% of matched vpxenc;
- clean decode under rvp9-decoder, vpxdec, and ffmpeg;
- documented deliberate divergences.

Later Stage 4b–4e and final cpu-used 6–8 parity remain downstream. Do not treat
the historical Foreman-only or cropped-testsrc evidence as final corpus parity.

- Numerical BD-rate `-3.99%`, but only two rvp9 points overlap the libvpx

- Matched-bitrate PSNR deficit: mean +0.99 dB.
- Cropped 1280x704 realtime median: 25.02 ms.
- rvp9-decoder and vpxdec were byte-exact; ffmpeg was unavailable in that run.

The later Slack1Over8 split-admission product work added strict evidence gates,
but its journal records no product PASS because the Windows worktree lacked
Foreman, approved libvpx tools, and ffmpeg.

## Rejected hypotheses to preserve

- Global integer-pel interpolation-filter shortcut: speed win, Foreman BD-rate
  regression.
- Track2 block-size diamond start and coefficient sign-bit cost: did not transfer
  to the current tree.
- High-precision MV, forced max TX, RECT disable, loop-filter disable, broad
  NEWMV masks, GF target boost, keyframe/GF extra spend, and raw q-only scaling:
  none produced a reproducible Pareto improvement.
- Do not use numerical BD-rate from non-overlapping or badly shaped curves as
  completion evidence.

## Unit 1 — native pinned libvpx build

Initialized `third_party/libvpx` at the pinned gitlink and confirmed tag
`v1.14.1`.

Fixed three repository build-script defects:

- Darwin arm64 now selects libvpx's newest macOS arm64 target,
  `arm64-darwin23-gcc`; the unversioned `arm64-darwin-gcc` target is iOS and
  requires an unavailable iPhoneOS SDK.
- The script no longer passes `--disable-tools`, which removes the `vpxenc` and
  `vpxdec` targets it needs.
- The script builds `target=libs libvpx.a` before requesting `vpxenc` and
  `vpxdec` from `target=examples`; top-level named-tool goals are incorrectly
  forwarded to libvpx sub-makes that do not define them.

Observed clean script result:

- configure target: `arm64-darwin23-gcc`
- configure flags:
  `--enable-vp9 --disable-vp8 --enable-vp9-highbitdepth --enable-pic
  --disable-docs --disable-unit-tests`
- `build/libvpx/vpxenc`: Mach-O arm64,
  SHA-256 `1bcd1492c1fc6319820862a685d9ff35f7a460570ad8b0704ce36e1b0d2ebe66`
- `build/libvpx/vpxdec`: Mach-O arm64,
  SHA-256 `dde363e5dbec2abb2298690039566ea7fe3b3fbe0ed0d2b608cb7e11aa075f65`
- both binaries print their help successfully.

Foreman was fetched from the documented Xiph URL and matches the strict product
gate: 45,621,044 bytes and SHA-256
`b0e3c35eff9fe1da91521a02eb9f0e3ef4bc5aec341ecef1567cd87f1711267b`.

Next: commit this green build-infrastructure unit, then run fresh Stage 4a
preflight and baseline measurements.

## Unit 3 — portable fixed-Q reference harness

The E9 fixed-Q diagnostic silently skipped libvpx on macOS because it only
looked for `D:\language\libvpx-stock\vpxenc.exe`. It now prefers the repository's
approved `build/libvpx` tools, retains the known Windows locations, and finally
searches `PATH`. The sibling decoder derives the correct platform suffix.

Fresh pinned-reference E9 results at commit `79667d8`:

- Q80: rvp9 `40.50 dB / 28,540 bits`; libvpx `41.78 dB / 30,595 bits`;
  gap `1.29 dB`
- Q120: rvp9 `37.61 dB / 15,004 bits`; libvpx `39.23 dB / 15,602 bits`;
  gap `1.62 dB`
- Q160: rvp9 `34.50 dB / 7,678 bits`; libvpx `36.19 dB / 7,329 bits`;
  gap `1.69 dB`

This confirms a coding-efficiency gap independent of the VBR rate controller.
The product baseline also chooses intra prediction for `20.34%` of blocks at
250 kbps versus libvpx's `2.00%`, so the next single hypothesis is that the
real-time intra fallback over-selects intra blocks.

The existing `RVP9_RT_NO_INTRA_FALLBACK=1` diagnostic produced only small
fixed-Q gains:

- Q80 gap `1.25 dB` (`+0.04 dB`)
- Q120 gap `1.61 dB` (`+0.01 dB`)
- Q160 gap `1.64 dB` (`+0.05 dB`)

It lowered mode-syntax cost but worsened prediction PSNR and raised coefficient
bits, so it is not established as a Pareto improvement. Next: run the legacy
Stage 4a VBR curve with the diagnostic enabled. Reject it unless the curve-level
gain and timing justify a production policy change.

The legacy VBR gate had the same native-tool defect: it found `vpxenc` but
hardcoded `vpxdec.exe`, silently omitting the reference curve. The gate now uses
the approved repository tools and derives the platform suffix.

Paired legacy four-point curves:

- default: matched-bitrate deficit `0.96 dB` across 2 in-range points
- no intra fallback: matched-bitrate deficit `0.64 dB` across 2 in-range points

The diagnostic improves the useful VBR points by `0.32 dB`, much more than its
fixed-Q movement. The legacy BD-rate result (`-4.59%` default versus `+13.47%`
diagnostic) is not trustworthy here: the curves overlap narrowly and the two
lowest rvp9 points are outside the reference range. Next: commit the portable
gate unit, make the no-intra policy a clean-tree candidate, and judge it with
the strict seven-point product gate.

## Unit 5 — post-policy hypothesis triage

Clean-tree strict and `Slack1Over8` product reports at commit `31329be`:

- strict:
  `target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-31329be00b25-1785411821194086000-p32109/stage4a_product_report.json`
- slack:
  `target/stage4a_product/candidate-31329be00b25-1785411832426320000-p32109/stage4a_product_report.json`

Slack lowered BD-rate from `+15.7683%` to `+15.4795%` and the matched-rate
deficit from `0.81462 dB` to `0.80200 dB`, but raised aggregate median time
from `11.9760 ms` to `12.1105 ms`. The important 250/400 kbps points slowed
about `2.8%/2.4%`. The quality movement is too small to justify the measured
speed loss, so this remains rejected.

The dormant libvpx-style VBR golden-frame allocator was also retested under
the accepted no-intra policy with `RVP9_VBR_GF_RC=1`. Its four-point legacy
curve produced `+18.52%` BD-rate and a `2.61 dB` matched-rate deficit, worse
than the accepted allocator. This hypothesis remains rejected.

A matched-rate block-stat comparison used rvp9's 400 kbps point
(`285.075 kbps`) against libvpx's 250 kbps point (`278.895 kbps`). Despite
rvp9 using the finer average inter Q (`146.37` versus `151.27`), it remained
about `0.83 dB` worse. The key-frame payload was only `68,376` bits versus
libvpx's `109,608`, while rvp9 spread inter bits almost uniformly instead of
libvpx's roughly ten-frame boosts. Rvp9 also used substantially more NEWMV
and ZEROMV area, much less skip, and many 4x4 transform leaves; libvpx used no
4x4 transform leaves in this sample.

This rules out a simple quantizer-only explanation. The next experiment is
raw per-block picker evidence from a separately patched, exact-revision
libvpx build and the current rvp9 VBR product path. The tracked F.0.2
comparator remains untouched because claiming its existing comparison defect
would require an external claim push, which this goal does not authorize.

## Unit 6 — matched VBR picker oracle

Built a disposable, instrumented libvpx at the pinned
`12f3a2ac603e8f10742105519e0cd03c3b8f71dd` revision. The patched binary is
`/tmp/rvp9-f02-oracle.3etdN3/build/vpxenc`, SHA-256
`27524b5f791e529a82de0ed59128e4337aabde1e62491aa5f739c6a35da76619`.
The repository submodule remains clean.

The libvpx sidecar contains `32,346` records for the 63 inter frames. Joining
them to `RVP9_TRACE_INTERMI=1` decoder leaves by frame, position, and block
size matched all `32,346` leaves exactly, with no missing or duplicate keys.
This proves the hook records committed picker leaves rather than abandoned
candidates.

Extended `f02-block-oracle` with an opt-in `--vbr` mode so the rvp9 leg uses
one-pass VBR and a GOP longer than the 64-frame sample, while the libvpx leg
keeps its native VBR default. The default harness path remains CBR. The rvp9
raw record is:

`/tmp/rvp9-f02-oracle.3etdN3/rvp9/run_6a6b3b8f/foreman_250kbps_rvp9.jsonl`

At 250 kbps, the aligned inter-frame distributions show:

- rvp9 skip area `54.98%`; libvpx picker skip area `72.72%`
- rvp9 NEWMV area `25.14%`; libvpx `28.57%`
- rvp9 NEWMV absolute L1 motion `39.17` versus libvpx `21.78` in Q3 units
- rvp9 NEARESTMV area `43.06%`; libvpx `58.50%`
- rvp9 ZEROMV area `22.03%`; libvpx `3.00%`

The SSE fields use different internal domains and are not cross-leg
comparable. The motion distribution is evidence of weaker causal reuse, but
the existing fixed-Q oracle also shows a more direct structural difference:
rvp9 repeatedly selects 4x4 transforms while libvpx selects none. A global
maximum-transform policy was already refuted. The next bounded experiment
will instead floor eligible inter-luma searches at 8x8, leaving sub-8x8
blocks and larger transform choices unchanged.

The paired legacy four-point VBR gate produced:

- default: BD-rate `+13.47%`, matched-rate deficit `0.64 dB`
- minimum Tx8: BD-rate `+9.62%`, matched-rate deficit `0.67 dB`

The floor improves the interpolated curve by `3.85` percentage points, but
the two-point matched-rate mean moves `0.03 dB` in the wrong direction. The
250 kbps point loses `0.12 dB`; lower points gain up to `0.05 dB`. The
legacy curve overlaps too narrowly to decide. Promote the floor as a
clean-tree candidate and use the strict seven-point product gate, including
its timing and cross-decoder evidence, as the decision oracle.

Strict product report for the clean-tree minimum-Tx8 candidate `23e5414`:

`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-23e5414710e3-1785412997401048000-p66005/stage4a_product_report.json`

Compared with the accepted full-depth search, the candidate worsened BD-rate
from `+15.7683%` to `+16.1021%` and the matched-rate deficit from
`0.81462 dB` to `0.84250 dB`. Its aggregate median was `12.0565 ms`, which
does not compensate for the quality loss. Every stream still passed the
rvp9 decoder and was byte-exact under vpxdec and ffmpeg. Reject the floor and
restore the full Tx4-through-max search.

## Unit 7 — exact zero MV at frame edges

The VBR picker record localized the remaining ZEROMV excess to the frame
edge. The strict no-halo envelope covers `21.32%` of inter-frame area, but it
contains `71.45%` of all rvp9 ZEROMV area (`15.99` of the total `22.38`
percentage points). At the top-left leaf, `(0,0)` was classified out of
bounds because the conservative sub-pel envelope starts at `(+6,+6)`.

That invariant is stale for exact zero motion. An integer `(0,0)` prediction
reads only the visible block and needs no interpolation halo. The candidate
now treats only exact ZEROMV as safe outside the strict envelope. Non-zero
and fractional vectors keep the existing bounds, so the motion search cannot
introduce a tight-plane over-read.

The focused bound test passes. The paired legacy VBR gate moved:

- matched-rate deficit: `0.64 dB` → `0.56 dB`
- 100 kbps point: `29.11 dB` → `29.24 dB`
- 250 kbps point: `33.87 dB` → `33.90 dB`

Its secondary BD-rate is `-13.92%`, but the curve still has only two
in-range points, so that number is not an acceptance result. Promote this
safe edge candidate to a clean tree and judge it with the strict seven-point
product gate.

Strict product report for `6ec4b93`:

`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-6ec4b9332a33-1785413498436589000-p71741/stage4a_product_report.json`

The candidate is a reproducible coding-efficiency win:

- BD-rate: `+15.7683%` → `+14.0148%`
- matched-rate deficit: `0.81462 dB` → `0.72143 dB`
- 250 kbps ZEROMV area: roughly `22%` → `9.70%`
- 250 kbps NEARESTMV area: roughly `44%` → `56.92%`
- all seven streams: rvp9-decoder PASS, vpxdec BYTE-EXACT, ffmpeg BYTE-EXACT

The change stays. The run's `24.6195 ms` aggregate timing median is not
credible: per-frame times show large, unrelated scheduling spikes and are
roughly double the adjacent clean runs. Remeasure speed after a warmed,
quiet run; do not attribute this host-load sample to the code.

## Unit 8 — visible full-chroma-pel edge candidates

The post-ZEROMV VBR picker record is:

`/tmp/rvp9-f02-edge-6ec4b93/run_6a6b3ff0/foreman_250kbps_rvp9.jsonl`

The strict edge band covers `21.10%` of inter area. Its mode distribution is
ZEROMV `21.37%`, NEARESTMV `65.19%`, NEARMV `0.04%`, and NEWMV `13.41%`.
The interior distribution is ZEROMV `6.67%`, NEARESTMV `54.02%`, NEARMV
`11.08%`, and NEWMV `28.23%`. Within the edge band's selected NEARESTMV
area, `81.22%` has a zero vector and `18.78%` has a non-zero vector.

The next bounded candidate admits a motion vector outside the conservative
filter-halo envelope only when it lands on integer samples for both luma and
4:2:0 chroma, and its complete visible block remains inside the frame.
Motion search limits remain unchanged; this affects only already-derived
candidate vectors such as NEARESTMV and NEARMV.

The first implementation admitted any whole-luma-pel vector. The legacy gate
immediately exposed that rule as unsafe with an out-of-bounds chroma filter
read: an odd whole-luma-pel displacement becomes chroma half-pel and still
needs a filter halo. The corrected predicate requires both Q3 components to
be multiples of 16. Its focused test covers zero, safe and unsafe frame
translations, the excluded odd-luma-pel case, fractional motion, a partial
leaf, and the unchanged strict-envelope path.

The focused test passes. The paired legacy VBR gate moved:

- matched-rate deficit: `0.56 dB` → `0.55 dB`
- 100 kbps point: `29.24 dB` → `29.27 dB`
- 250 kbps point: `33.90 dB` → `33.89 dB`

The evidence is small and mixed. Promote the safe candidate to a clean tree,
then use the strict seven-point product gate to decide whether its aggregate
quality movement is reproducible enough to keep.

Strict product report for `d9cc5a9`:

`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-d9cc5a97aaf9-1785413975514980000-p75688/stage4a_product_report.json`

The candidate is a second reproducible edge-motion win:

- BD-rate: `+14.0148%` → `+13.3964%`
- matched-rate deficit: `0.72143 dB` → `0.70910 dB`
- all seven streams: rvp9-decoder PASS, vpxdec BYTE-EXACT, ffmpeg BYTE-EXACT
- aggregate median: `12.1924 ms`

The median agrees with the adjacent clean-tree runs despite the unrelated
`mddosem` process occupying one CPU. This also confirms the exact-zero run's
`24.6195 ms` median was scheduling contamination, not a code regression.
Keep the visible full-chroma-pel candidate. The remaining Stage 4a gap is
`11.3964` percentage points above its intermediate `+2%` exit.

## Unit 9 — isolate realtime residual-policy differences

At 250 kbps after the edge-motion fixes, rvp9's inter-mode distribution is
close to libvpx's, but rvp9 skips `55.64%` of area versus libvpx's roughly
`77.8%`. rvp9 also emits 4x4 transforms while libvpx's realtime picker floors
transform size at 8x8.

Reusing the ported picker-side `calculate_tx_size` decision in isolation with
`RVP9_RT_EMIT_TX_RD=0` made every meaningful legacy-gate point worse:

- 100 kbps: `29.2725 dB` → `29.1459 dB`
- 250 kbps: `33.8879 dB` → `33.7500 dB`
- matched-rate deficit: about `0.55 dB` → `0.66 dB`

This independently confirms the rejected minimum-Tx8 result. The 4x4
transforms compensate for another mismatch and are not themselves the next
lever.

libvpx speed 7 sets `use_quant_fp = !is_keyframe`; rvp9 still defaulted its
inter-frame emit path to the regular quantizer. Isolating fast quantization
with the existing `RVP9_RT_QUANT_FP=1` diagnostic, while retaining the
accepted emit-side transform RD loop, improved the legacy gate:

- 100 kbps: `71.4898 kbps / 29.2725 dB` →
  `71.2575 kbps / 29.2877 dB`
- 250 kbps: `179.0597 kbps / 33.8879 dB` →
  `179.6591 kbps / 33.9443 dB`
- matched-rate deficit: about `0.55 dB` → `0.52 dB`

Promote fast quantization as the clean-tree realtime default, retain
`RVP9_RT_QUANT_FP=0` as the regular-quantizer diagnostic, and judge the
candidate with the strict seven-point product gate.

Strict product report for `8b295b8`:

`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-8b295b8b8db1-1785414232706674000-p79106/stage4a_product_report.json`

Fast quantization is a reproducible coding-efficiency win:

- BD-rate: `+13.3964%` → `+12.4005%`
- matched-rate deficit: `0.70910 dB` → `0.67993 dB`
- all seven streams: rvp9-decoder PASS, vpxdec BYTE-EXACT, ffmpeg BYTE-EXACT
- aggregate median: `12.5560 ms`

The median is `2.98%` above the immediately preceding `12.1924 ms` run even
though the quantizer itself is cheaper. Fast quantization changes coefficient
survival and caused more high-rate split work, while the host still has an
unrelated process occupying one CPU. Keep the clear quality win, but do not
claim a speed win from this sample. The remaining Stage 4a gap is `10.4005`
percentage points above its intermediate `+2%` exit.

## Unit 12 — rejected VBR keyframe active-quality port

The matched-rate frame table showed rvp9's first keyframe at Q65 and 68,376
bits versus libvpx at Q33 and 109,608 bits. Two source-level VBR divergences
could explain a higher keyframe floor:

- rvp9 reused its CBR active-quality boost of 32, while libvpx one-pass VBR
  initializes `rc->kf_boost` to `DEFAULT_KF_BOOST=2000`;
- rvp9 omitted libvpx's 0.75 quantizer-value adjustment for CIF-or-smaller
  frames.

An isolated candidate ported both rules in
`/Users/md/worktrees/rvp9-2/20260730-TSK-HUM-rvp9-production-parity-with-pinned-libvp/crates/rvp9-encoder/src/rate_control/vbr.rs:active_best_quality_vbr`.
Its focused VBR tests passed, but the four-point foreman gate was byte-for-byte
unchanged from `8b295b8`:

- 25 kbps: `39.2495004995005 kbps / 26.32490493890217 dB`
- 50 kbps: `41.493506493506494 kbps / 26.42613390257229 dB`
- 100 kbps: `71.25749250749251 kbps / 29.287657451896806 dB`
- 250 kbps: `179.6590909090909 kbps / 33.94433077620456 dB`

The rate regulator therefore binds above the corrected active-best floor.
This independently confirms the earlier no-op boost experiment. The candidate
was removed; no shipped code changed. Do not revisit keyframe floor allocation
until inter prediction improves enough to change the regulator's binding
constraint.

The next experiment must target the measured inter residual gap. At matched
achieved rate, rvp9 uses a finer average inter Q (`142.6` versus `151.3`) but
still emits about `8559` versus `7704` inter bits per frame and only skips
`44.7%` versus libvpx's `77.8%`. Mode mix is now close enough that the next
diagnostic should align the selected prediction error and interpolation-filter
choice for the same committed leaves.

## Unit 13 — reject cpu6–8 eighth-pel refinement

Decoder-side mode traces exposed a large motion/interpolation mismatch on the
matched-rate foreman pair. Area-weighted committed inter blocks were:

- rvp9: `42.75%` integer MV, `57.25%` fractional MV, `28.40%` Smooth filter;
- libvpx: `17.90%` integer MV, `82.10%` fractional MV, `15.87%` Smooth filter.

The top-level MV reported for 8x8 blocks can hide libvpx subblock MVs, so these
figures are a direction finder rather than a final oracle. EightTap-only was
also mixed: the legacy matched-rate mean moved from about `0.52 dB` to
`0.46 dB`, but the 25/50 kbps points worsened and the prior fixed-Q and
speed evidence already rejected that policy.

Source comparison found
`/Users/md/worktrees/rvp9-2/20260730-TSK-HUM-rvp9-production-parity-with-pinned-libvp/crates/rvp9-core/src/mode_decision/inter.rs:run_newmv_search`
applies libvpx's speed-9 adaptive half/quarter-pel stop to the scoped cpu-used
6–8 path. Replacing it with libvpx's cpu6–8 `EIGHTH_PEL` default while leaving
the product high-precision frame flag off produced:

- legacy gate: `40.6768/26.3112`, `42.2840/26.4087`,
  `71.1563/29.2245`, and `179.2058 kbps/33.9853 dB`;
- strict report:
  `target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-8a5b72a385db-1785415720320818000-p88125/stage4a_product_report.json`;
- strict BD-rate: `+12.9508%`, worse than accepted `+12.4005%`;
- strict matched-rate deficit: `0.69047 dB`, worse than `0.67993 dB`;
- median encode time: `22.7713 ms`, `81.36%` slower than `12.5560 ms`;
- all strict streams still decoded byte-exactly under vpxdec and ffmpeg.

Enabling the existing `RVP9_HIGHMV=1` flag with eighth-pel refinement initially
panicked on a GOLDEN NEWMV at a 16x8 right-edge leaf. Diagnosis showed the
picker replaced an unsafe `(16,-134)` NEAREST predictor with zero, then reused
that zero as NEWMV's coding reference. It consequently chose `(7,-58)` at
eighth-pel precision, while the writer correctly derived `(16,-134)` and could
only signal a quarter-pel delta. Separating the safe sampling predictor from
the actual coding reference removed the panic, but the legacy matched-rate
deficit worsened to `0.60 dB`. The coding-reference fix also perturbed the
default curve without a reproducible Pareto gain.

Both source candidates were removed. Keep the accepted adaptive forced stop
until another measured prerequisite makes full cpu6–8 refinement affordable.
The exact next experiment is a same-leaf prediction comparison: capture rvp9
and libvpx committed LAST/GOLDEN MV, interpolation filter, prediction SSE, and
residual EOB for aligned frame/block coordinates. First determine whether the
remaining residual gap comes from motion search, filter RD choice, or
transform/skip modelling before changing another policy.

## Unit 14 — repair the F.0.2 picker oracle and isolate partition structure

The existing F.0.2 comparison was invalid. The rvp9 leg center-cropped Foreman
to 256x256, while the libvpx leg encoded the original 352x288 Y4M. It also
labelled post-quant reconstruction distortion as pre-quant prediction SSE on
both legs. Any earlier pairwise block conclusion from this harness must be
discarded.

The repaired harness now:

- writes the exact cropped I420 frames used by rvp9 for libvpx;
- passes the source frame rate and the Stage 4a product-equivalent vpxenc
  options;
- records each winning candidate's real pre-quant luma prediction SSE;
- records the winning interpolation filter;
- accepts `--strict` to select the canonical Stage 4a strict split policy.

The current patch compiled against pinned libvpx commit
`12f3a2ac603e8f10742105519e0cd03c3b8f71dd`. A two-frame strict VBR smoke
parsed 490 rvp9 and 676 libvpx records successfully:

`/tmp/rvp9-f02-schema-current/run_6a6b4d3d`

At matched achieved rate on the corrected 64-frame Foreman comparison, rvp9
target 400 kbps produced about 285 kbps and libvpx target 250 kbps produced
about 288 kbps. Their committed inter-block area differed sharply:

- rvp9: 69.07% 16x16, 3.03% 8x8, 2.93% 32x32, and about 24.98% rectangles;
- libvpx: 52.75% 16x16, 36.27% 8x8, 10.99% 32x32, and no rectangles.

Only 45.49% of the inter area shared the same coordinate and block size.
Across that common area, rvp9's prediction SSE per pixel was 24.6447 versus
libvpx's 16.0552, a 1.535 ratio. Interpolation-filter mismatch was not the
dominant cause: the same-ref/MV but different-filter subset had a 1.146 ratio.

A source-domain check on common integer-LAST leaves removed reconstructed
reference drift from the comparison. The 583 leaves that chose the same MV had
exactly equal SSE. On the 123 leaves that chose different MVs, rvp9's selected
MV cost 23.3601 SSE/pixel versus libvpx's 8.8449, a 2.641 ratio. This confirms
that selected partition/MV propagation remains causal, but does not justify
reopening raw diamond search: the earlier E13 oracle already showed healthy
NEWMV recovery, and full eighth-pel refinement was a large Pareto loss.

Focused validation:

- `cargo test -p rvp9-encoder --lib -- --test-threads=1`: 488 passed, 1 ignored;
- `cargo test -p rvp9-harness --lib -- --test-threads=1`: 37 passed;
- `cargo test -p rvp9-harness --bin f02-block-oracle -- --test-threads=1`: 3 passed;
- `cargo test -p rvp9-harness --test f02_oracle_smoke -- --test-threads=1`: 6 passed;
- focused encoder/harness clippy with `-D warnings`: passed;
- rustfmt, patch apply-check, patched libvpx build, and end-to-end schema smoke:
  passed.

This unit changes diagnostic tooling only. The accepted product baseline
remains commit `8b295b8`, strict BD-rate `+12.4005%`, matched-rate deficit
`0.67993 dB`, and median encode time `12.5560 ms`.

The exact next experiment is a narrow partition-policy comparison. Explain why
rvp9 promotes rectangles where libvpx descends to 8x8 under matched
conditions, then alter only that measured decision. Do not repeat the rejected
blanket split-slack or minimum-transform experiments.

## Unit 15 — reject motion-compensated variance-hint reference

Pinned libvpx cpu-used 7 builds its variance partition tree against the
integer-projection ME predictor, while rvp9 uses the zero-motion LAST region.
This is a real input divergence, so I implemented an exact full-pel,
edge-replicated reference option and tested it as the default.

The baseline zero-motion raw hint at the matched-rate 400 kbps target was
already extremely fine:

- 88.8145% 8x8, 8.8046% 16x16, 2.3810% 32x32, 0% 64x64;
- 880 of 1,008 inter SBs had a non-zero integer-projection seed.

Motion compensation moved the hint only modestly:

- 86.9234% 8x8, 10.4973% 16x16, 2.5794% 32x32, 0% 64x64.

The downstream A4 descent amplified that movement in the wrong direction.
Strict product report:

`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-62b2acd8616f-1785417470362002000-p5597/stage4a_product_report.json`

Compared with accepted report
`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-8b295b8b8db1-1785414232706674000-p79106/stage4a_product_report.json`:

- BD-rate worsened from `+12.4005%` to `+12.8995%`;
- matched-rate deficit improved only from `0.67993 dB` to `0.67162 dB`;
- median encode time worsened from `12.5560 ms` to `12.8412 ms`;
- 250 kbps committed area moved from 3.03% to 1.27% 8x8, from 69.07% to
  76.87% 16x16, and from about 24.98% to 18.91% rectangles;
- all seven streams remained byte-exact under vpxdec and ffmpeg.

This is not a Pareto improvement. The candidate commit was `62b2acd`; restore
the accepted zero-motion hint. More importantly, the result falsifies the
variance-input hypothesis: A4's partition RD descent, not the raw hint, causes
the committed 16x16 lock.

The next experiment must inspect the A4 candidate cost itself. Compare the
descent's winning per-size rate/distortion proxy with the final emitted
rate/distortion for the same leaf. If the proxy systematically favours
16x16/rect over 8x8, repair that causal mismatch instead of changing the
variance hint or adding split slack.

## Unit 16 — reject picker-domain distortion in A4 descent

The Stage 4a picker calibrates inter decisions with coefficient-domain
distortion, but A4 reconstructed its partition candidates from the selected
rate and pixel reconstruction SSE. I tested the narrow correction: recover the
picker's effective distortion from its returned RD cost, then use that value
for A4's partition comparisons.

This changed the measured partition lock, but not in the required direction.
At the strict report's 250 kbps target, committed area changed from 69.07% to
59.58% 16x16 and from 3.03% to 5.34% 8x8. Rectangles grew from about 24.98%
to 31.06%, absorbing most of the released 16x16 area.

Strict product report:

`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-77de10b511af-1785417920741957000-p10165/stage4a_product_report.json`

Compared with the accepted `8b295b8` report:

- BD-rate worsened from `+12.4005%` to `+13.0651%`;
- matched-rate deficit improved only from `0.67993 dB` to `0.67367 dB`;
- median encode time improved from `12.5560 ms` to `12.1156 ms`;
- all seven streams still decoded successfully, byte-exact under vpxdec and
  ffmpeg.

This is a mixed result, not a reproducible Pareto improvement. The candidate
commit was `77de10b`; restore the accepted pixel-domain A4 cost. It proves that
the A4 cost domain controls the partition distribution, while also showing
that using the complete picker-domain cost promotes rectangles rather than
the missing 8x8 splits.

The next experiment must separate the rectangle override from the 16x16 versus
split decision. Measure the NONE, SPLIT, HORZ, and VERT candidate costs on the
same 16x16 nodes under both cost domains. Then change only a measured
inconsistency in the rectangle comparison; do not globally disable rectangles
or reuse the rejected blanket split slack.

## Unit 17 — accept neutral inherited-MV context for the A4 proxy

An env-only diagnostic captured 5,018 16x16 nodes where NONE, SPLIT, HORZ,
and VERT had all been evaluated on the strict 400 kbps Foreman run:

`/tmp/rvp9-a4-node-cost-aa5efa9.log`

Pixel-domain winners were 30.27% NONE, 11.34% SPLIT, 33.84% HORZ, and
24.55% VERT. Picker-domain winners were 14.61% NONE, 25.29% SPLIT, 32.86%
HORZ, and 27.24% VERT. Both domains therefore chose rectangles on about

SPLIT; it did not remove rectangle dominance. This explains Unit 16's
mixed result and rules out a split-only hybrid cost as the next lever.

The causal mismatch is earlier. A4 evaluates all shapes before the current
superblock has populated its mi-grid, while the final picker sees earlier
committed leaves. A 64-frame profile at the 400 kbps target found only

and final `(nearest, near)` pair:

`/tmp/rvp9-a4-profile-aa5efa9.log`

The accepted correction gives every speculative A4 shape a neutral zero
inherited-MV pair for realtime VBR cpu-used 7/8. ZERO and NEWMV remain
available, and the final emitted picker still uses its causal neighbours.

- matched-rate deficit improved from `0.67993 dB` to `0.63404 dB`;
- median encode time improved from `12.5560 ms` to `12.1694 ms`;
- all seven streams decoded successfully and remained byte-exact under
  vpxdec and ffmpeg.

This is a reproducible Pareto improvement, so keep candidate commit
`d782db3`. Focused validation passed: 489 encoder library tests with one

## Unit 18 — isolate the final motion-search penalty

The regenerated matched-rate F.0.2 comparison used rvp9 target 400 kbps
(about 285 kbps achieved) and libvpx target 250 kbps (about 288 kbps
achieved):

- rvp9:
  `/tmp/rvp9-f02-neutral-mv-d782db3/run_6a6b5416/foreman_400kbps_rvp9.jsonl`;
- libvpx:
  `/tmp/rvp9-f02-matched-detail/run_6a6b4a6a/foreman_250kbps_libvpx.jsonl`.

Neutralising A4's speculative inherited-MV context improved common committed
area from `45.14%` to `46.52%`. The common-area prediction-SSE ratio improved
from `1.5555` to `1.5271`, so Unit 17's final quality gain did generalise into
the emitted predictions. The different integer-LAST subset nevertheless
worsened from a `2.849` to a `2.977` source-domain SSE ratio.

A final-picker candidate trace joined 8,083 common leaves. For libvpx's
LAST-selected motion vector:

- `15.20%` of eligible area matched rvp9's winner;
- `8.36%` was present among rvp9's ZERO, NEAREST, NEAR, or searched NEW
  candidates but lost the final RD comparison;
- `76.44%` was absent from that candidate set.

Rank or skip-cost tuning therefore cannot recover most of the vector gap.
To separate search failure from reference drift, I decoded both matched-rate
streams with the pinned `vpxdec` and evaluated the two selected integer
vectors against the same reconstructed references. Across 164 same-shape
leaves with different integer LAST vectors:

- against the original previous frame, rvp9/libvpx SSE per pixel was
  `22.6731/7.6169` (`2.9767x`);
- against rvp9's reconstructed previous frame it was `25.7837/15.5199`
  (`1.6613x`);
- against libvpx's reconstructed previous frame it was `24.3713/10.0479`
  (`2.4255x`).

Libvpx's vector remains much better even on rvp9's own reference. The causal
gap is therefore in rvp9's motion search/candidate production, not mainly in
reconstruction drift or the final candidate ranker.

The exact next experiment is the smallest known source-level divergence in
that search. Replace
`/Users/md/worktrees/rvp9-2/20260730-TSK-HUM-rvp9-production-parity-with-pinned-libvp/crates/rvp9-core/src/mode_decision/inter.rs:DEFAULT_SAD_PER_BIT`
with libvpx's qindex-correlated `sadperbit16` formula:
`(int)(0.0418 * (ac_quant(qindex)/4.0) + 2.4107)`. The fixed value `16`
over-penalises motion at the matched-rate inter qindexes. Measure the strict
product curve before changing search shape or sub-pel depth.

## Unit 20 — accept libvpx's cpu-used 7/8 fast-diamond search

Source inspection found that the old oracle premise was wrong for the scoped
product path. Pinned libvpx selects `FAST_DIAMOND` with
`fullpel_search_step_param=10` for realtime cpu-used 7/8 in
`third_party/libvpx/vp9/encoder/vp9_speed_features.c:699-703`. Its
`fast_dia_search` resolves to only the scale-zero `bigdia` pattern: four unit
neighbours around the predictor seed, followed by the previous direction and
its two adjacent directions until no augmented-SAD improvement remains.
Rvp9 instead ran its multiscale NSTEP-style diamond at every speed.

Commit `c16173d` adds the matching unit-step walk and selects it for realtime
cpu-used 7/8. Cpu-used 6 and non-realtime modes retain NSTEP. A diagnostic
control with `RVP9_FAST_DIAMOND=0` reproduced the accepted four-point curve;
the product default reproduced the `RVP9_FAST_DIAMOND=1` candidate exactly.
On that curve, informational BD-rate improved from about `+4.21%` to
`-7.03%`, and the matched-rate deficit improved from about `0.45 dB` to
`0.33 dB`.

Canonical strict product report:

`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-c16173d2fc12-1785420317410005000-p40037/stage4a_product_report.json`

Compared with accepted report
`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-d782db354456-1785418632037009000-p26216/stage4a_product_report.json`:

- BD-rate improved from `+12.0635%` to `+9.1152%`;
- matched-rate deficit improved from `0.63404 dB` to `0.52174 dB`;
- median encode time improved from `12.1694 ms` to `11.8122 ms`;
- all seven rvp9 streams decoded successfully and remained byte-exact under
  pinned vpxdec and ffmpeg.

This is a reproducible Pareto improvement. Focused validation passed the
full-pel search regression, the realtime speed-policy selector, both A4
partition tests, all 490 non-ignored encoder library tests, rustfmt, and
core/encoder clippy with `-D warnings`.

Stage 4a remains open by `7.1152` percentage points against its `+2%`
intermediate BD-rate exit. Regenerate the matched-rate F.0.2 trace under
`c16173d`, then repeat Unit 18's common-leaf candidate join and
reconstructed-reference comparison. The exact next decision is whether
FAST_DIAMOND closed the absent-vector share enough to move to final RD

## Unit 21 — reject the source-faithful cpu-used 7 quarter-pel stop

The regenerated F.0.2 trace confirmed that Unit 20's fast-diamond gain reaches
the final emitted predictions. Against the same matched libvpx trace, common
committed area grew from `46.52%` to `46.95%`; common-area prediction-SSE ratio
improved from `1.5271` to `1.4731`; and the differing integer-LAST subset's
source-domain SSE ratio improved from `2.9767` to `2.2669`.

An exact Q3 candidate join initially still reported libvpx's selected vector
absent from rvp9's ZERO, NEAREST, NEAR, and NEW candidates over `75.57%` of
eligible area. Distance measurement showed that exact equality was misleading:
rvp9's closest candidate was an exact rounded full-pel match over `60.15%` of
area, within one full pixel over `86.70%`, and within two over `95.13%`.
The residual motion-vector difference is therefore now mainly sub-pixel
refinement, not full-pixel candidate production.

Pinned source inspection exposed a deliberate P14 divergence:

- libvpx realtime cpu-used 6-8 inherits `QUARTER_PEL` from
  `vp9_speed_features.c:562`;
- only cpu-used 9 enables the adaptive quarter/half-pel selector at
  `vp9_speed_features.c:803-806`;
- rvp9 applies that cpu-used 9 selector unconditionally at cpu-used 6-8.

I implemented and tested the source-faithful speed policy, then measured it.
The short four-point Foreman diagnostic moved informational BD-rate from about
`-7.03%` to `-4.45%` and matched-rate deficit from about `0.33` to `0.40 dB`.
The canonical strict candidate report was:

`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-d4578cc54e61-1785421256577823000-p46174/stage4a_product_report.json`

It is marked `VALIDATION_DIRTY` because this was an uncommitted diagnostic, but
the curve and decoder evidence are directly comparable with Unit 20:

- BD-rate worsened from `+9.1152%` to `+9.4344%`;
- matched-rate deficit was flat (`0.52174` to `0.52184 dB`);
- median frame time regressed from `11.8122` to `21.8189 ms`;
- all seven streams still decoded successfully and remained byte-exact under
  vpxdec and ffmpeg.

The quarter-pel candidate is neither a Pareto improvement nor compatible with
the speed objective, so I restored the accepted source tree. The retained
adaptive-policy departure is now explicit in
`/Users/md/worktrees/rvp9-2/20260730-TSK-HUM-rvp9-production-parity-with-pinned-libvp/notes/divergence-ledger.md`.

The next bounded experiment is libvpx's populated five-entry full-pel
`cost_list` path in `vp9_find_best_sub_pixel_tree_pruned_more`. Rvp9 currently
passes the equivalent of `cost_list == NULL` and always walks the sampled
pyramid. Determine how often libvpx's well-behaved parabolic shortcut fires at
cpu-used 7, then port only that shortcut if its candidate output and search
count explain the remaining sub-pixel and speed gap. Do not deepen the

## Unit 22 — accept libvpx's full realtime sub-pixel tree

The Unit 21 next-experiment premise was wrong. Pinned source inspection showed
that non-SVC realtime cpu-used 6-8 never selects `_pruned_more`:

- `vp9_speed_features.c:928-930` initializes `SUBPEL_TREE`, level 2, and
  eighth-pel force-stop;
- `set_rt_speed_feature_framesize_independent` changes the force-stop at
  speed 4 but leaves the search method unchanged for non-SVC cpu-used 6-8;
- only a selected non-reference SVC enhancement layer changes to
  `_pruned_evenmore` at `vp9_speed_features.c:712-716`;
- `cond_cost_list` returns null for `SUBPEL_TREE`, so the proposed parabolic
  `cost_list` shortcut is not active in the scoped reference configuration.

Rvp9 instead used `_pruned_more`, based on a stale module header and ledger
entry that had conflated libvpx's GOOD-mode speed setter with REALTIME mode.
The accepted change ports `vp9_find_best_sub_pixel_tree` and selects it from
`run_newmv_search`. It preserves the existing bilinear variance oracle,
precision stops, level-2 extension count, MV limits, and MV-cost domain. A
focused deterministic regression proves the full tree reaches Q3
`(-12,-4)` at distortion `386332` where `_pruned_more` stops at `(-10,-4)`
and distortion `392169`.

Quick four-point Foreman evidence moved:

- informational BD-rate: about `-7.03%` to `-7.78%`;
- matched-rate deficit: about `0.33` to `0.32 dB`.

Strict candidate report:

`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-4628f811be89-1785421680263263000-p49951/stage4a_product_report.json`

Same-load `_pruned_more` control:

`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-4628f811be89-1785421791260390000-p50850/stage4a_product_report.json`

The candidate is a reproducible Pareto improvement:

- BD-rate improved from `+9.1152%` to `+8.8483%`;
- matched-rate deficit improved from `0.52174` to `0.49409 dB`;
- same-load aggregate median improved from `18.6831` to `18.3473 ms`;
- all seven candidate streams decoded successfully and remained byte-exact
  under pinned vpxdec and ffmpeg.

The older Unit 20 absolute timing (`11.8122 ms`) came from a lighter host-load

matched-rate F.0.2 final-candidate trace with the full tree and measure whether
rounded/Q3 candidate distance and source-domain prediction SSE move toward
libvpx. Use that result to choose between the remaining sub-pixel/MV-cost
differences and final inter prediction/RD modelling; do not revisit the
inactive `cost_list` shortcut.

## Unit 23 — reject qindex-derived sub-pixel MV cost and isolate luma residuals

The clean committed Unit 22 report is:

`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-bbdc975c9c53-1785422051385063000-p52864/stage4a_product_report.json`

It confirms `validation_clean=true`, strict BD-rate `+8.8483148%`,
matched-bitrate deficit `0.4940931 dB`, and aggregate median
`18.385875 ms` under the current loaded host. All seven streams pass the rvp9
decoder and remain byte-exact under pinned vpxdec and ffmpeg. The sole gate
failure is `BD_RATE_GT_2PCT:8.848`.

The fresh matched-rate trace is:

`/tmp/rvp9-f02-fulltree-bbdc975/run_6a6b616e/foreman_400kbps_rvp9.jsonl`

against the existing pinned-reference trace:

`/tmp/rvp9-f02-matched-detail/run_6a6b4a6a/foreman_250kbps_libvpx.jsonl`

The full tree did not improve candidate agreement with libvpx:

- common-leaf prediction-SSE ratio worsened from `1.473081` to `1.483386`;
- differing integer-LAST source-domain SSE ratio worsened from `2.266863` to
  `2.561084`;
- exact rounded full-pel candidate coverage fell from `60.15%` to `55.42%`;
- within-one-full-pixel coverage fell from `86.70%` to `83.71%`;
- exact Q3 coverage fell from `24.43%` to `23.25%`.

Unit 22's product gain therefore did not come from converging on libvpx's
selected vector. It changed the surrounding partition, mode, and residual
decisions beneficially despite worse same-leaf vector agreement. More search
depth is not the next lever.

The next source-level sub-pixel difference was the MV-cost multiplier.
Libvpx uses `max(1, compute_rd_mult(qindex) >> 6)`, while rvp9 uses the fixed
`16`. A source-faithful candidate passed rustfmt and all 54 focused inter-mode
tests, then produced this four-point Foreman curve:

- `35.8104 kbps / 26.1746 dB`;
- `38.7100 kbps / 26.3926 dB`;
- `71.4673 kbps / 29.5721 dB`;
- `179.3369 kbps / 34.0526 dB`.

Informational BD-rate worsened from about `-7.78%` to `-0.65%`; the
matched-bitrate deficit stayed about `0.32 dB`; both the low-rate and
250 kbps points regressed. I rejected the candidate before the expensive
strict matrix and restored the accepted fixed multiplier. The deliberate
departure is recorded in
`/Users/md/worktrees/rvp9-2/20260730-TSK-HUM-rvp9-production-parity-with-pinned-libvp/notes/divergence-ledger.md`.

An aligned skip join identifies the next causal layer. Of `190,080` pixels of
common leaves with the same reference, MV, and interpolation filter, rvp9
skips `57.58%` while libvpx skips `82.22%`. For the `156,288` pixels libvpx
skips:

- rvp9 also has zero emitted residual over `59.99%`;
- rvp9 emits luma-only residual over `37.72%`;
- rvp9 emits chroma-only residual over `0.37%`;
- rvp9 emits both luma and chroma residual over `1.93%`.

The residual mismatch is therefore specifically luma, not chroma or another
MV-cost issue. The exact next experiment is to extend the F.0.2 diagnostic
with per-leaf qindex, transform size, and luma EOB on both legs, then inspect
the 246 same-prediction leaves where libvpx skips but rvp9 emits luma. Separate
reference-reconstruction drift from transform/quantization disagreement
before changing skip thresholds or residual policy.

## Unit 24 — make residual disagreement observable and rule out transform size

The F.0.2 schema now records effective per-leaf `q_index`, selected luma
`tx_size`, and whether the emitted luma residual has a non-zero EOB. The rvp9
hook is after residual emission and the libvpx hook is in the committed
realtime winner path. The parser, JSON writer, harness summary, smoke tests,
and pinned-source patch all use the same required fields.

The first end-to-end run exposed a diagnostic-lifetime defect:
`leaf_y_eob` was accumulated only when the older `RVP9_S2_XTAB` diagnostic
was enabled, so F.0.2 alone reported every luma EOB as zero. The hot loop now
accumulates it when either diagnostic is enabled. A textured two-frame
regression test proves that an F.0.2 run observes a real non-zero emitted
luma EOB.

The pinned reference build used commit
`12f3a2ac603e8f10742105519e0cd03c3b8f71dd` and the canonical configure
arguments:

`--target=arm64-darwin23-gcc --enable-vp9 --disable-vp8 --enable-vp9-highbitdepth --enable-pic --disable-docs --disable-unit-tests`

The patched build completed successfully. Its 64-frame trace contains the
same `32,346` committed libvpx leaves as the earlier matched-rate trace. The
new diagnostic artifacts are:

- libvpx 250 kbps:
  `/tmp/rvp9-f02-residual-schema-lib-arm64/run_6a6b65d2/foreman_250kbps_libvpx.jsonl`;
- rvp9 400 kbps:
  `/tmp/rvp9-f02-residual-schema-rvp9-fixed/run_6a6b6657/foreman_400kbps_rvp9.jsonl`.

Joining by frame, position, and block size produced `8,372` common leaves and
`792` leaves with the same reference, MV, and interpolation filter. Of those,
libvpx skipped `630`; rvp9 emitted a non-zero luma residual on `260`.

The 260-leaf disagreement is not a transform-size choice:

- `259/260` use 8x8 transforms on both legs;
- the remaining leaf uses rvp9 8x8 versus libvpx 16x16;
- rvp9 uses a finer qindex on `238/260`, the same qindex on `5/260`, and a
  coarser qindex on `17/260`;
- mean qindex is `140.57` for rvp9 versus `152.67` for libvpx;
- despite finer quantization, rvp9's pre-quant prediction SSE is `1.2563x`
  libvpx's in aggregate (`1.2759x` median per-leaf ratio).

This rules out transform-size policy and a simple coarse-quantizer cause.
The emitted residual is compensating for a worse predictor. The remaining
split is reconstructed-reference drift versus sub-pixel prediction
implementation. Earlier source-domain evidence covered integer-LAST leaves,
not this mostly sub-pixel residual subset.

Focused validation:

- encoder library: `490 passed`, `1 ignored`;
- harness library: `37 passed`;
- F.0.2 binary tests: `3 passed`;
- F.0.2 smoke tests: `6 passed`;
- encoder/harness clippy with all targets and `-D warnings`: passed;
- rustfmt, `git diff --check`, pinned libvpx patch apply-check, canonical
  patched-libvpx build, and 64-frame end-to-end trace: passed.

This is diagnostic tooling only. The accepted product baseline remains
commit `bbdc975`, strict BD-rate `+8.8483148%`, matched-bitrate deficit
`0.4940931 dB`, and all seven streams cross-decoder green.

The exact next experiment is a cross-reference predictor comparison on the
260-leaf subset. Evaluate the shared MV/filter against (1) the original
previous source frame, (2) rvp9's decoded LAST reference, and (3) libvpx's
decoded LAST reference. If both implementations agree on one shared
reference, reference drift is causal; if they disagree on the same pixels,
fix interpolation. Do not change quantization or skip policy first.

## Unit 25 — correct the matched-Q oracle and isolate causal MV propagation

The old E.9 fixed-Q comparison was not actually matched at the keyframe.
libvpx Q mode applied its normal keyframe boost despite `--cq-level`, so the
requested internal qindices `80/120/160` produced keyframe qindices
`15/31/70`. That made the historical `1.3–1.8 dB` whole-stream deficit an
invalid coding-efficiency oracle.

E.9 now invokes libvpx with:

`--end-usage=q --cq-level=N --min-q=N --max-q=N+8`

The eight-step range is libvpx's minimum valid CLI range. It clamps the
keyframe at the requested qindex while leaving inter frames at the same
constant-quality point. The harness parses the first packet with
`rvp9-decoder`, asserts the observed keyframe qindex, parses IVF payloads,
and reports keyframe quality/bits plus average inter bits. The rvp9 leg now
retains the production Stage 4a VBR picker calibration while
`RVP9_FIXED_Q` overrides only rate-control Q selection.

The corrected 64-frame Foreman results are:

| qindex | rvp9 PSNR / bits per frame | libvpx PSNR / bits per frame | rvp9 inter-bit excess |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 80 | `40.39 dB / 26,087` | `40.37 dB / 21,112` | `+25.3%` |
| 120 | `37.55 dB / 13,435` | `37.52 dB / 10,384` | `+32.7%` |
| 160 | `34.34 dB / 6,204` | `34.37 dB / 4,962` | `+30.2%` |

Keyframes at the same qindex are already close:

- q80: rvp9 `40.83 dB / 56,136 bits`; libvpx
  `40.97 dB / 57,192 bits`;
- q120: rvp9 `37.89 dB / 33,592 bits`; libvpx
  `38.03 dB / 35,368 bits`;
- q160: rvp9 `34.77 dB / 18,272 bits`; libvpx
  `34.86 dB / 20,168 bits`.

The real fixed-Q gap is therefore inter-frame bit efficiency, not decoded
quality or keyframe coding. rvp9 spends `25–33%` more inter bits for
effectively the same decoded PSNR.

F.0.2 now accepts `--fixed-q <internal-qindex>`, validates the exact libvpx
CLI mapping, retains VBR picker policy, and writes both encoders' IVF
streams. The exact q120 run is:

`/tmp/rvp9-f02-fixedq120-ivf/run_6a6b6d26`

Both streams decode successfully. Their traces cover the same
`4,128,768` inter-frame pixels:

- rvp9 records `22,371` leaves; libvpx records `36,828`;
- rvp9 prediction SSE/pixel is `29.667`; libvpx is `19.010`
  (`1.5606x`);
- rvp9 skips `30.34%` of leaf area; libvpx skips `59.03%`;
- rvp9 emits luma residual over `68.42%`; libvpx over `23.48%`;
- rvp9 uses many rectangles and `3,200` 8x8 leaves; libvpx uses no
  rectangles and `27,980` 8x8 leaves.

An exact join has `9,277` common leaves. Across them, rvp9 prediction SSE is
`1.428x` libvpx. The `1,092` leaves with the same reference, MV, and filter
have a ratio of `0.987x`, which confirms the interpolation implementations
agree when the coded choice agrees.

The F.0.2 IVF output enabled exact predictor replay against the original
previous source and each encoder's decoded LAST frame. The replay validated
every measured trace SSE (`7,063/7,063` for both encoders). On the clean
previous source:

- rvp9's chosen MV/filter costs `11.0204` SSE/pixel;
- libvpx's chosen MV/filter costs `9.1350` SSE/pixel;
- rvp9/libvpx chosen-predictor ratio is `1.2064x`;
- using each encoder's reconstructed LAST raises the common-leaf ratio to
  `1.0700x`;
- the `1,088` same-choice leaves are exactly equal on source;
- the `5,975` different-choice leaves have a `1.2357x` source-domain ratio.

Mode-pair attribution makes the causal shape clear. NEAREST/NEAREST is the
largest bucket: `568,640` pixels at `1.2516x`. NEW/NEW is only `1.0850x`.
Rows where rvp9 chooses ZERO and libvpx chooses NEAREST/NEAR/NEW are smaller
but much worse (`2.29–3.36x`). This implicates the causal nearest/near
candidate field shaped by partition decisions, rather than interpolation or
insufficient NEWMV search.

The following narrow probes were rejected and fully backed out:

- A corrected libvpx-like VBR GF cadence produced informational BD-rate
  `+12.43%` and matched-bitrate deficit `2.28 dB`, far worse than the
  accepted quick curve.
- E.8 confirms rvp9's initial VBR keyframe cost model overpredicts the
  observed keyframe by about `4x`, but the historical keyframe-only
  multiplier sweep already refuted that lever.
- Replacing a chosen Block16 rectangle with an earlier winning 8x8 SPLIT
  raised bits at all three fixed-Q points for negligible quality change.
- `RVP9_A4_RD_PARTITION_DISABLE=1` raised inter bits at all three fixed-Q
  points despite improving prediction and skip use.
- The May 30 post-coding RD-skip experiment is already implemented and
  refuted in
  `wrk_journals/2026.05.29 - JRN - Match-vpxenc campaign (quality+speed).md`;
  do not repeat it.

The accepted product baseline remains `bbdc975`, with strict BD-rate
`+8.8483148%`, matched-bitrate deficit `0.4940931 dB`, median
`18.385875 ms`, and all seven streams cross-decoder green. This unit changes
diagnostics only.

The exact next experiment is to compare rvp9's final realtime
`find_mv_refs`/`mv_refs_rt` candidate scan with pinned libvpx
`vp9_pickmode.c:mv_refs_rt` and `vp9_mvref_common.c:find_mv_refs_idx`.
Start with the common NEAREST/NEAREST blocks and determine whether rvp9
orders or extracts MVs differently for rectangular and sub-8x8 causal
neighbors. Add a focused oracle only after locating a concrete semantic
difference; do not globally remove rectangles or alter skip thresholds.

## Unit 26 — locate the fixed-Q excess inside the coupled partition/mode field

Replaying rvp9's `find_mv_refs` implementation against each encoder's own
committed MI grid reproduced every selected causal candidate in the refreshed
q120 trace:

- rvp9 NEAREST `9,612/9,612`, NEAR `2,510/2,510`;
- libvpx NEAREST `17,755/17,755`, NEAR `5,807/5,807`.

The resolver table, neighbour scan, rectangular coverage, previous-frame MV
fallback, and candidate ordering are therefore exact. Feeding libvpx's
committed field into rvp9's resolver produces libvpx's choices. The weaker
candidate field is upstream, in the coupled partition/mode decisions.

Exact predictor replay on `7,201` in-bounds common LAST leaves confirms that
interaction. On rvp9's decoded LAST reference, rvp9's chosen field costs
`15.332245` prediction SSE/pixel while libvpx's choices cost `14.603496`, a
`1.049902x` ratio. On libvpx's decoded reference the ratio is `1.129573x`.
The chosen decisions and reconstructed reference reinforce each other, but
the interpolation implementation remains exact.

Four narrow partition probes were rejected and fully backed out:

- A4 partition `rd_mult` multipliers `0.5x`, `0.75x`, `1.5x`, and `2.0x`
  failed to lower fixed-Q inter bits consistently across q80/q120/q160.
- Replacing every final rvp9 partition shape with libvpx's exact q120
  partition map raised rvp9 inter bits from `13,115` to `14,421` (`+10.0%`)
  and worsened prediction PSNR by `0.24 dB`; q80 and q160 also worsened.
- Shapes alone are therefore not portable between the two mode fields.
- A concurrent E.9 run exposed a harness constraint: ignored E.9 tests share
  temporary libvpx IVF names and must run sequentially.

Pinned libvpx instrumentation then separated the winning picker's modeled
rate from actual entropy output. The fixed-q q120 artifacts are:

`/tmp/rvp9-f02-payload-rate/run_6a6b72e3`

The winning rate model already contains the gap:

- libvpx total modeled rate: `8,036.96` bits/inter frame; actual inter
  payload: `9,886` bits/frame;
- rvp9 total modeled rate excluding only reference syntax:
  `11,232.35` bits/inter frame; actual inter payload: `13,115` bits/frame;
- libvpx's pre-MV/mode/reference payload model: `5,146.98` bits/frame;
- rvp9's coefficient model alone: `8,985.75` bits/frame.

The same payload split generalises:

| qindex | libvpx modeled payload bits/frame | rvp9 coefficient bits/frame |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 80 | `13,361.83` | `21,288.49` |
| 120 | `5,146.98` | `8,985.75` |
| 160 | `1,644.92` | `3,292.95` |

Libvpx's payload column includes skip, interpolation-filter, and optional UV
model costs, so it is a conservative comparison: rvp9's luma coefficient
estimate alone is still `59–100%` larger. Final entropy coding does not create
the excess; the picker enters emission with a much larger residual burden.

A direct primitive oracle rules out the coefficient estimator itself.
For one deterministic 16x16 residual, rvp9 and pinned libvpx produced exactly
the same Hadamard coefficient SATD, qindex-derived dequant/round/multiplier
tables, quantized SATD, and non-zero count at q80/q120/q160. For example,
q120 produced coefficient SATD `109,952`, quantized SATD `732`, and `203`
non-zero coefficients on both implementations.

The decisive counterfactual replays each encoder's complete LAST partition,
MV, filter, and transform field on rvp9's own decoded reference. At q120:

- rvp9 field: `528,314` modeled coefficient bits over `3,881,984` pixels,
  or `0.13610` bits/pixel;
- libvpx field: `317,329` modeled coefficient bits over `3,395,648` pixels,
  or `0.09345` bits/pixel.

On common same-shape leaves, libvpx choices improve the coefficient proxy only
`2.3%`; across each encoder's complete field they improve it `31.3%` per
pixel. Neither an isolated MV resolver change nor an isolated partition-shape
change can close this. The missing efficiency is the coupled square
partition/mode solution.

The accepted product baseline remains `bbdc975`, with strict BD-rate
`+8.8483148%`, matched-bitrate deficit `0.4940931 dB`, median
`18.385875 ms`, and all seven streams cross-decoder green. This unit changes
no shipped code.

The exact next experiment is the untested interaction between two known
libvpx square-path semantics: use the picker's coefficient-domain distortion
for A4 partition comparison and disable rectangular arms, which pinned
realtime cpu-used 7 suppresses. Each change alone was refuted because rvp9's
other arm compensated in the opposite direction. Measure their combination
sequentially at fixed q80/q120/q160 before any product curve. Keep it only if
inter bits fall without decoded-quality loss at all three points; otherwise
back it out and instrument the 16x16 NONE-versus-four-8x8 child cost split.

## Unit 27 — rule out A4 final re-picking as the residual-rate cause

The proposed coefficient-domain-plus-square-path interaction was measured and
rejected. At fixed q80/q120/q160 it produced:

| qindex | candidate inter bits/frame and PSNR | accepted baseline |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 80 | `25,929 / 40.45 dB` | `25,610 / 40.39 dB` |
| 120 | `13,470 / 37.64 dB` | `13,115 / 37.55 dB` |
| 160 | `6,367 / 34.43 dB` | `6,013 / 34.34 dB` |

It spends `1.2–5.9%` more bits for `0.06–0.09 dB`. Disabling rectangles
alone was also retested against the current baseline and raised inter bits to
`25,713`, `13,363`, and `6,173` with negligible quality change.

The new split-component diagnostic explains why. Across q120's 63 inter
frames, the 16x16 descent made `15,862` attempts. It completed `3,881`;
`2,580` beat NONE, `1,301` lost, `755` committed, and rectangles overrode
`1,825`. On completed comparisons, four 8x8 children beat NONE on both
modeled rate and distortion:

- average rate margin: `-17,926.710` probability-cost units, or
  `-35.013` modeled bits;
- average distortion margin: `-3,943.529`;
- average combined RD margin: `-2,766,618.585`.

The remaining `11,981` attempts stop when the non-negative partial child RD
sum already exceeds NONE. That early-out is valid, not a hidden square-path
candidate loss. The aggregate artifact is:

`/tmp/rvp9-a4-split-components-q120/run_6a6b7591`

Three closer libvpx `PC_TREE` simulations were also rejected and fully
backed out:

- retaining speculative child mode state, reusing descent picks, and
  disabling rectangles raised inter bits to `29,379`, `15,675`, and `7,515`;
- adding speculative MI-grid commits improved that to `26,933`, `14,388`,
  and `6,984`, but remained much worse;
- speculative MI-grid commits with final re-picking were neutral:
  `25,432`, `13,055`, and `6,095`; the q160 loss prevents a Pareto win.

Direct profiling confirms final re-picking is not the source of the excess.
At q120, exact descent/final choices match only `36–42%` for the common
8x8-to-16x16 sizes, but final picks have lower modeled rate:

| size | committed | exact match | final-minus-descent modeled rate |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 8x8 | `3,572` | `42.08%` | `-2.942 bits` |
| 8x16 | `4,384` | `40.42%` | `-2.443 bits` |
| 16x8 | `4,946` | `37.40%` | `-3.508 bits` |
| 16x16 | `9,902` | `36.03%` | `-3.313 bits` |

Average final-minus-descent distortion is `-26.67`, `+17.29`, `+7.39`,
and `-59.91` respectively. Reusing the speculative pick therefore discards a
better decision in rvp9's current field.

The accepted product baseline remains `bbdc975`, with strict BD-rate
`+8.8483148%`, matched-bitrate deficit `0.4940931 dB`, median
`18.385875 ms`, and all seven streams cross-decoder green. This unit retains
diagnostics only.

## Unit 29 — honor the realtime picker's luma skip-transform decision

The realtime emitter now honors the winning picker's
`SKIP_TXFM_AC_DC` decision whenever fast quantization is active. It writes
zero-EOB markers for every selected luma transform, still quantizes chroma,
and keeps the existing post-encode all-plane skip decision. This is
independent of whether the transform size came from the picker, emit-side RD,
or the large-frame maximum-transform shortcut.

This closes a real control-flow mismatch with pinned libvpx
`vp9_encodemb.c:590-604`. rvp9 already carried the picker's `skip_txfm` in
`PickInterModeResult`, but its default emit path ignored it solely because
emit-side transform RD was enabled. The old `RVP9_RT_EMIT_TX_RD=0` diagnostic
had coupled the skip decision to a worse transform policy.

The isolated fixed-Q probe shows why both decisions must be separated:

| qindex | default bits/frame / PSNR | skip-transform bits/frame / PSNR |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 80 | `25,610.032 / 40.39609 dB` | `25,435.683 / 40.37078 dB` |
| 120 | `13,158.603 / 37.55250 dB` | `12,919.111 / 37.50391 dB` |
| 160 | `6,012.952 / 34.35344 dB` | `5,960.254 / 34.22781 dB` |

Adjacent default q84/q124/q164 points give matched-quality local payload
movement of `-0.063%`, `-0.591%`, and `+1.969%`. The high-Q point alone is
not a Pareto win, so the seven-point product curve was the keep oracle.

The full strict product candidate report is:

`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-6a085b7d6a5a-1785428653814208000-p96436/stage4a_product_report.json`

It improves every primary aggregate:

- BD-rate: `+8.8483148%` → `+6.8760597%`;
- matched-bitrate deficit: `0.4940931 dB` → `0.3658350 dB`;
- aggregate median encode time: `18.385875 ms` → `14.975459 ms`;
- 250 kbps decoded skip area: `52.7561%` → `59.5145%`;
- all seven streams: rvp9-decoder PASS, pinned vpxdec BYTE-EXACT, ffmpeg
  BYTE-EXACT.

The report deliberately records `VALIDATION_DIRTY` because it measured the
one-line source probe before commit. Its only substantive gate failure is
still `BD_RATE_GT_2PCT:6.876`. A committed clean-tree rerun is required before
this becomes the accepted baseline.

The large-frame path is also green. PLAN's synthetic fixture was regenerated
with:

`ffmpeg -f lavfi -i testsrc2=size=1280x720:rate=30 -frames:v 60 -pix_fmt yuv420p -f yuv4mpegpipe tests/corpus/testsrc_720p30.y4m`

The ignored fixture SHA-256 is
`4acce2037fa770c5d48ea56534284f721341d1756d16d46cd9785b21b37a98ea`.
At 1280x704, 30 frames, cpu-used 7 realtime CBR:

- median: `23.44 ms` against `33.00 ms`;
- p99: `25.07 ms` against `50.00 ms`.

An independent eight-frame 1280x704 encode with `--test-decode=fatal`
decoded byte-exact under rvp9-decoder, pinned vpxdec, and ffmpeg. All three
raw outputs have SHA-256
`a9b9d35ee1a4966e950436316c8c3eb7e86adeeb3bee693cc515701709d21ec8`.

The first full encoder-library run exposed an AQ interaction: with the new
faithful luma skip signal, all selected boost cells could skip, and rvp9's
cyclic-refresh cursor stayed pinned to that SB. Pinned libvpx advances
`cr->sb_index` inside `cyclic_refresh_update_map`, after every inspected SB;
`vp9_cyclic_refresh_update_sb_postencode` only updates Q history.

rvp9 now follows that ownership. `pre_encode_pick_segments` advances the
cursor past the last SB inspected, while `post_encode_update` retains the
skip-aware age and Q-map updates. A focused regression proves that an
all-skipped selected SB does not rewind or pin the cursor.

Validation after the AQ correction:

- `cargo test -p rvp9-encoder cyclic_refresh --lib -- --test-threads=1`:
  `30 passed`;
- `cargo test -p rvp9-encoder --lib -- --test-threads=1`:
  `492 passed, 1 ignored`;
- `cargo clippy -p rvp9-encoder --all-targets -- -D warnings`: PASS;
- `cargo fmt --all --check`: PASS;
- `git diff --check`: PASS.

The broader non-ignored integration run passed all tests until
`w_2pass_vbr::two_pass_allocates_lower_q_to_busier_frames`, which failed its
busy-Q ordering assertion. A detached clean `6a085b7` worktree reproduces the
same assertion failure (`busy_mean_q == flat_mean_q == 191`), so it predates
this unit and is outside the declared one-pass scope. No test was weakened.

The next action is to commit this focused-green unit and rerun the strict
product gate from the clean committed tree. After that, regenerate the
accepted q120 F.0.2 trace with skip-transform active and remeasure the
remaining same-predictor residual subset. The earlier default trace is no
longer the product baseline.

## Unit 31 — isolate the post-skip fixed-Q residual burden

The refreshed fixed-Q F.0.2 artifact under the accepted luma-skip baseline is:

`/tmp/rvp9-f02-acdc-rate-q120/run_6a6b8000`

It used the instrumented pinned-v1.14.1 encoder at:

`/tmp/rvp9-f02-schema.n5Qsoc/libvpx/build_arm64/vpxenc`

The 63 inter-frame averages at q120 are:

- rvp9 actual inter payload: `12,860 bits/frame`;
- libvpx actual inter payload: `9,886 bits/frame`;
- rvp9 actual coefficient payload: `9,672 bits/frame`;
- libvpx instrumented modeled residual payload: `5,507.714 bits/frame`;
- rvp9 estimated coefficient payload: `9,066.9 bits/frame`;
- libvpx modeled total candidate rate: `8,036.959 bits/frame`.

The default fixed-Q E.9 sweep confirms the same pattern:

| qindex | rvp9 PSNR / inter bits | libvpx PSNR / inter bits |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 80 | `40.36 dB / 25,404` | `40.37 dB / 20,437` |
| 120 | `37.49 dB / 12,860` | `37.52 dB / 9,886` |
| 160 | `34.23 dB / 5,889` | `34.37 dB / 4,619` |

Matched-q quality is nearly equal, while rvp9 spends about `24%`, `30%`,
and `27%` more inter bits. The burden is therefore predominantly a
rate-allocation / residual-selection problem, not a reconstruction-quality
ceiling.

Three transform hypotheses were rejected:

1. Disabling the content DCT-EOB skip veto collapses q120 to
   `30.90 dB / 4,082 inter bits`; this is not a usable quality trade.
2. Reusing the picker transform loses quality and raises payload at q80 and
   q120 (`37.29 dB / 13,136` at q120 versus the accepted
   `37.49 dB / 12,860`).
3. Forcing the maximum transform loses quality at every sampled qindex and
   raises payload at q80 and q120 (`37.09 dB / 12,983` at q120).

The refreshed cross-predictor replay still finds `323` common LAST-reference
leaves where libvpx emits zero luma residual while rvp9 does not. Most already
use TX8 on both sides (`204` leaves), so transform-size selection cannot
explain that subset.

The next measured hypothesis is the missing color-sensitivity consumer gate.
`PreAnalysisState::color_sensitivity_sb` already produces per-SB `[U, V]`
flags, but `pick_and_simulate_inter_encode_partitions` does not pass them to
the picker. Consequently `evaluate_candidate` always adds UV model-RD,
whereas pinned libvpx only does so when either `x->color_sensitivity` flag is
set (`vp9_pickmode.c:2381-2395`). Wire the existing flags into both descent
and final picker calls, add a focused disabled-gate regression, then rerun
fixed-Q E.9 before deciding whether to retain the change.

## Unit 32 — reject the existing color-sensitivity producer as a picker gate

The full libvpx UV gate was prototyped locally: per-SB `[U, V]` flags flowed
through descent and final picks; insensitive planes contributed no UV model
rate or distortion; large-block early termination bypassed UV. Focused model
and picker tests passed.

The fixed-Q E.9 result did not meet the Pareto rule:

| qindex | accepted rvp9 PSNR / inter bits | gated rvp9 PSNR / inter bits |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 80 | `40.36 dB / 25,404` | `40.36 dB / 25,486` |
| 120 | `37.49 dB / 12,860` | `37.50 dB / 12,956` |
| 160 | `34.23 dB / 5,889` | `34.24 dB / 5,883` |

At q80/q120 the gate costs `82`/`96` inter bits per frame for at most
`0.01 dB`; q160 is effectively flat. The source probe was therefore removed
and the worktree returned to the committed baseline.

This does not refute libvpx's gate. It refutes wiring rvp9's current
`PreAnalysisState::compute_color_sensitivity` output into production:
that producer explicitly compares luma against Last_Source and chroma
against each SB's own mean because it lacks libvpx's per-candidate prediction
buffers. Those flags are not an equivalent oracle. Do not reconnect them
without first measuring or correcting the producer.

The residual gap remains: matched-q quality is nearly equal, but rvp9 spends
about `24–30%` more inter payload, mostly coefficients. The next experiment
must target why rvp9 selects predictors / partitions that leave more coded
residual, not transform-size reuse or this approximate chroma gate.

## Unit 33 — reject an isolated 8×8-average partition variance substitution

Pinned libvpx's cpu-used 7 realtime path builds the variance-based partition
hierarchy from 8×8 source/reference averages in
`third_party/libvpx/vp9/encoder/vp9_encodeframe.c:fill_variance_8x8avg`.
rvp9 instead measures every residual pixel in
`crates/rvp9-core/src/mode_decision/partition.rs:block_per_pixel_variance`.
This is a confirmed semantic difference: balanced texture within an 8×8
block affects rvp9's hierarchy but is invisible to libvpx's hierarchy.

A local diagnostic replaced only rvp9's hierarchy samples with rounded 8×8
average residuals. A focused checkerboard test proved the intended primitive,
and the encoder compiled. The paired seven-point foreman sweep was worse:

| metric | accepted full-pixel tree | 8×8-average tree |
|---|---:|---:|
| PSNR BD-rate versus libvpx | `+6.05%` | `+6.75%` |
| matched-bitrate mean deficit | `+0.33 dB` | `+0.36 dB` |
| matched-bitrate maximum deficit | `+0.62 dB` | `+0.69 dB` |

At q120 fixed-Q, the candidate also rose from `12,860` to `13,068`
inter bits/frame while quality moved only from `37.49` to `37.51 dB`.
The isolated primitive is therefore not a Pareto improvement. All diagnostic
source and test changes were removed.

This result does not refute an exact libvpx partition port. Its decision path
also carries conditional 4×4 averages, force propagation, integer-pel motion
compensation, rectangular partitions, and partition reuse. It does show that
changing the hierarchy statistic alone cannot close Stage 4a.

The next experiment is narrower and residual-focused: on the q120 F.0.2
common LAST-reference leaves with identical motion vector and interpolation
filter, measure source-versus-predictor residual energy and quantized
non-zero counts on both encoders. This will distinguish prediction-buffer
differences from transform/quantization differences before another product
change.

## Unit 34 — reject Hadamard-skip ranking and isolate predictor selection

A decoded-reference replay of the q120 F.0.2 traces separated reference drift
from predictor choice. Across `6,933` common in-bounds LAST-reference leaves:

- rvp9's chosen predictor has `1.2493x` libvpx's source-frame SSE;
- using each encoder's own decoded reference reduces that ratio to `1.0995x`;
- on rvp9's decoded reference, the libvpx-selected predictor still has
  `7.95%` less SSE than the rvp9-selected predictor;
- the `1,064` identical MV/filter picks have exactly equal source-frame SSE;
- the `5,869` different picks have `1.2899x` source-frame SSE in rvp9.

Thus reference drift contributes, but the larger measured loss is selecting a
worse predictor. A quantized DCT replay on the common leaves also estimates
libvpx's decision field at `89.59%` of rvp9's coefficient payload when both
use rvp9's reference.

The libvpx trace field previously named `luma_eob_nonzero` was clarified. It
records the winning realtime picker's final luma coding decision
(`!skip && skip_txfm == NONE`), not a raw FDCT EOB. Among the `323`
same-predictor leaves where rvp9 codes luma and libvpx does not, `113` are
libvpx skip-flag-only, `72` are `SKIP_TXFM_AC_DC`-only, and `138` set both.
This invalidates attempts to explain all `323` as transform quantization
disagreement.

One small source candidate made rvp9 rank Hadamard/quantize-fp skippable
leaves on its skip branch, matching libvpx's immediate `block_yrd` control
flow and the luma skip already honored by rvp9's emitter. The focused
self-consistency test and encoder check passed, but the strict seven-point
product curve regressed:

| metric | accepted baseline | Hadamard-skip ranking |
|---|---:|---:|
| PSNR BD-rate versus libvpx | `+6.8761%` | `+7.1759%` |
| matched-bitrate mean deficit | `0.3658 dB` | `0.3831 dB` |

All seven candidate streams still passed rvp9-decoder and remained byte-exact
under pinned vpxdec and ffmpeg. The source and test changes were removed.
The dirty diagnostic report is
`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-a6cff389c75e-1785432726817555000-p5251/stage4a_product_report.json`.

The exact next experiment is candidate availability. For common-shape
LAST-reference leaves where libvpx's predictor is materially better on the
same rvp9 reconstructed reference, classify the libvpx MV against rvp9's
ZERO, NEAREST, and NEAR candidates. For remaining NEWMV cases, trace the
full-pixel and sub-pixel search result. This distinguishes an available
candidate lost by RD costing from an MV that rvp9 never searches.

## Unit 35 — isolate coupled context and harden dormant intra reconstruction

Candidate replay found that the residual predictor gap is not a local
motion-search miss. Among common LAST-reference leaves where libvpx's
predictor is more than 1% better on rvp9's reconstructed reference:

- `58` use rvp9's NEAR candidate, `36` use NEAREST, `38` use ZERO, and
  `15` use rvp9's own NEWMV;
- the remaining `2,250` use another MV;
- `1,034` of those other MVs differ by only one quarter-pel step, but only
  `25/228` same-mode NEWMV pairs at that distance share the same NEAREST
  candidate and only `1/228` shares the same NEAREST/NEAR pair;
- just `189/2,250` other MVs appear within four cells of rvp9's causal or
  previous-frame field.

The nearby search objective often prefers libvpx's vector, but the candidate
contexts almost never match. This confirms the remaining motion difference is
upstream in the coupled partition/mode field, not another isolated sub-pixel
search rule.

Partition stability initially looked causal. Adjacent frames preserve the
exact superblock partition on `140/992` libvpx superblocks versus `10/992`
rvp9 superblocks; per-cell equality is `78.98%` versus `64.84%`. Instrumented
pinned libvpx showed that its low-source-SAD prior-partition copy path fires
`80` times on the q120 F.0.2 encode. The diagnostic binary remained
bit-identical to the canonical reference:

`10608f4a7eb5bd77d496215a345cb3f701899331284f746a1ff1b33059e0e900`

A source-faithful rvp9 prototype reused the prior variance partition for at
most two low-source-SAD frames. It did not improve fixed-Q coding:

| qindex | accepted inter bits/frame | partition-copy prototype |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 80 | `25,404` | `25,404` |
| 120 | `12,860` | `12,904` |
| 160 | `5,889` | `5,896` |

The copy is therefore a speed shortcut in libvpx's current field, not the
missing rate-distortion lever in rvp9's descent. The prototype was removed.

The q120 traces exposed a larger deliberate field difference: rvp9 chooses no
intra blocks on inter frames, while libvpx chooses `1,011`. Retesting rvp9's
dormant intra fallback first exposed two correctness faults. The live causal
grid recorded an intra winner as an invalid inter cell, and the encoder's
loop-filter grid did the same. The writer and decoder recorded both as intra,
so later NEAREST/NEAR resolution and filtering diverged. Before the fixes the
12-frame reference diagnostic first diverged at frame 3 with luma SSE
`44,924` and maximum error `51`; after the causal-grid fix, luma was exact but
chroma still diverged by up to `7`. Preserving intra in both grids made Y/U/V
pixel-exact on all 12 frames.

The reconstruction-safety prerequisite landed as `13dcac2`. Focused
validation passed:

- `cargo test -p rvp9-encoder
  tests::causal_grid_splats_intra_winner_as_intra -- --exact`;
- `cargo test -p rvp9-encoder
  loopfilter::w7_4_tests::inter_frame_lf_grid_preserves_intra_winner
  -- --exact`;
- `cargo test -p rvp9-encoder
  frame_loop::tests::build_inter_mi_grid_intra_winner_emits_block_mode_intra
  -- --exact`;
- `cargo fmt --all -- --check`;
- `cargo clippy -p rvp9-encoder --lib --tests -- -D warnings`.

The now-safe policy still failed the Pareto rule:

| qindex | safe intra inter bits / PSNR | accepted baseline |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 80 | `26,402 / 40.34 dB` | `25,404 / 40.36 dB` |
| 120 | `13,604 / 37.44 dB` | `12,860 / 37.49 dB` |
| 160 | `6,268 / 34.22 dB` | `5,889 / 34.23 dB` |

The accepted no-intra product default remains unchanged.

The exact next experiment is an intra-selection cost oracle, not another
policy toggle. On the safe q120 path, record every rvp9 intra candidate's
best-inter and best-intra rate/distortion components, then join by covered
cell with libvpx's emitted intra field. Separate true-positive, false-positive,
and false-negative regions. The largest false-positive cost component will
identify whether the simplified fallback is missing UV rate/distortion,
mispricing the intra reference/mode syntax, or using the wrong transform
decision. Only correct that measured component before retesting q80/q120/q160.

## Unit 36 — reject broad intra gating and isolate the RD-domain mismatch

The q120 cost oracle joined `13,611` eligible rvp9 intra evaluations to
libvpx's emitted intra field by frame and covered MI cell. It classified
`250` true-positive leaves, `2,739` false-positive leaves, `251`
false-negative leaves, and `10,371` true-negative leaves. The false positives
cover `9,708` cells versus only `781` true-positive cells, so the dormant
picker overselects intra by roughly one order of magnitude.

The false-positive mean intra-minus-inter components were:

- RD cost `-3,154,695`;
- total rate `+4,020`, comprising coefficient rate `+6,715` and syntax rate
  `-2,695`;
- reconstructed-pixel distortion `-1,750`.

Rate alone does not explain the wins. Even requiring lower total rate leaves
`952` false-positive leaves versus `163` true positives. Requiring lower
coefficient rate leaves `202` false positives versus `90` true positives.

Pinned libvpx's full entry condition additionally rejects low-source-SAD
superblocks and any inter search that set `x->skip`. A source-SAD-only gate
still produced `26,198`, `13,372`, and `6,278` inter bits/frame at
q80/q120/q160. Adding the corresponding rvp9 picker-skip and early-search
guards reduced those to `26,122`, `13,322`, and `6,158`, still above the
accepted `25,404`, `12,860`, and `5,889`. PSNR remained effectively flat at
`40.36`, `37.46`, and `34.22 dB`. The policy therefore fails the Pareto rule,
and all diagnostic source changes were removed.

The remaining measured mismatch is the RD distortion domain. Libvpx's
realtime intra path ranks the Hadamard/quantize-fp coefficient error returned
by `block_yrd`, while rvp9 ranks post-reconstruction pixel SSE. The exact next
experiment is to rank safe intra candidates with the existing
`BlockYrdResult::coef_dist` (`pixel SSE << 4` for the all-zero shortcut),
while retaining pixel SSE only as the surfaced reconstruction diagnostic.
Retest fixed q80/q120/q160 before enabling any product policy.

distortion domain. Post-reconstruction pixel SSE remains the reported
diagnostic. The speculative partition descent still excludes intra; only the
selected leaf runs the fallback, matching libvpx's control-flow location.

The fixed-Q oracle became a Pareto improvement:

| qindex | accepted inter bits / PSNR | coefficient-domain intra |
|---:|---:|---:|

| 120 | `12,860 / 37.49 dB` | `12,844 / 37.51 dB` |
| 160 | `5,889 / 34.23 dB` | `5,914 / 34.25 dB` |

The canonical clean-tree product report is:

`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-68013955e2ed-1785435624502661000-p42259/stage4a_product_report.json`

Compared with the accepted `00d4e45` baseline:

Focused validation:

  -- --exact`;
