The exact next experiment is to compare the accepted 250 kbps block field
against libvpx after removing the now-matched intra cells. Recompute
same-predictor and alternate-predictor residual burden on the q120 F.0.2
trace. Select one remaining rate component or mode-field difference with
enough area to close at least the `2.925` percentage-point Stage 4a gap.

## Unit 38 — repair the intra-aware F.0.2 oracle

The first post-`6801395` q120 trace exposed an oracle-only schema violation.
Final intra winners carried the picker's inter placeholder (`ZEROMV`) into
F.0.2, producing impossible `ref_frame=INTRA, mode=ZEROMV` records. Commit
`9ce897a` now derives the oracle mode from `picked_ref_frame` and
`picked_intra_mode`, and emits zero MV plus no interpolation filter for intra.
The focused
`tests::f02_oracle_serializes_intra_winner_as_intra_mode` regression passed,
as did encoder clippy and formatting.

The corrected q120 artifact is:

`/tmp/rvp9-f02-coef-intra-q120-fixed/run_6a6b971f`

It contains `879` rvp9 intra leaves (`408` 8x8, `470` 16x16, one 32x32)
versus `1,011` libvpx leaves (`939` 8x8, `72` 16x16). Expanded to MI cells,
rvp9 covers `2,304`, libvpx covers `1,227`, and only `283` overlap. The new
policy is useful but still chooses intra at a much coarser partition shape.

After excluding intra and comparing common in-bounds LAST-reference leaves:

- same-pick leaves are only `1,143/6,702`;
- different-pick leaves cover `1,160,640/1,408,704` pixels and have
  `1.22495x` source-prediction SSE;
- replaying libvpx's pick on rvp9's reconstructed reference lowers SSE by
  `4.91%`;
- rvp9-only residual disagreements remain `2,037` leaves versus `188` in
  the opposite direction.

## Unit 39 — reject libvpx's intra entry shortcuts on rvp9's field

The source-faithful low-source-SAD and inter-search-skip guards were retested
after the coefficient-domain fix. Fixed-Q results were mixed: q120/q160
inter payload fell to `12,803`/`5,878` bits, while q80 rose to `25,303`.
The clean strict report is:

`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-12fbf91a3ae8-1785436114740116000-p55293/stage4a_product_report.json`

It regressed aggregate BD-rate from `+4.9246%` to `+5.4522%` and the
matched-rate deficit from `0.2841` to `0.2999 dB`. Intra area at 250 kbps
fell from `3.3606%` to `1.6896%`. Although median time improved from
`13.2028` to `12.8105 ms`, the quality loss violates the Pareto rule. The
guard source changes were removed; rvp9 benefits from some coefficient-domain
intra winners that libvpx's shortcut suppresses on its different field.

The exact next experiment is partition coupling. Enable the now-safe,
coefficient-domain intra fallback inside the partition descent as well as on
the final selected leaf. Pinned libvpx runs `vp9_pick_inter_mode` for each
evaluated partition candidate, so its intra result can justify finer
partitions; rvp9 currently introduces intra only after choosing the leaf.
The q120 shape mismatch (`408` versus `939` 8x8 intra leaves) makes this a
large measured lever. Test fixed q80/q120/q160 first and retain it only if
the payload/quality trade improves before advancing to the product curve.

## Unit 40 — reject intra-aware partition descent

Enabling the coefficient-domain intra fallback in partition descent did move
the field toward finer shapes. At 250 kbps, 8x8 area rose from `1.0293%` to
`4.4767%`, and intra area rose from `3.3606%` to `5.4750%`. Fixed-Q
prediction quality improved at all three points, but q160 inter payload rose
from `5,914` to `5,984` bits/frame.

The clean strict report is:

`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-f6eee898378d-1785436285048786000-p58990/stage4a_product_report.json`

It rejects the coupling:

- BD-rate worsened from `+4.9246%` to `+5.3802%`;
- matched-rate deficit moved from `0.2841` to `0.2882 dB`;
- aggregate median time rose from `13.2028` to `14.7600 ms`;
- 250/400 kbps medians rose to `39.8026`/`42.2440 ms`;
- all seven streams remained cross-decoder byte-exact.

The descent evaluates candidates before reconstructing their within-SB
causal neighbours, so its intra edges are stale. More importantly, the full
curve shows that buying finer partitions this way costs too much syntax and
search time. The one-line candidate was removed.

The exact next experiment is intra chroma semantics. Pinned libvpx assigns
`mi->uv_mode = this_mode` when a realtime intra-in-inter candidate wins,
while rvp9 currently hardcodes UV DC in the simulator, causal grid, and
loop-filter grid. On the accepted field, q120 contains `309` V/H intra
leaves and the 250 kbps product point has `3.36%` intra area. Make UV follow
the selected Y mode for these leaves, add a focused grid/reconstruction
regression, and test q80/q120/q160 before the product curve.

## Unit 41 — reject luma-mode chroma prediction for realtime intra

then constructs its variance tree. Rvp9 already computes the same int-pro MV
for final mode search but its partition prepass compares source against

test fixed q80/q120/q160 before the product curve. This isolates the missing
motion compensation without attempting the larger exact partition port.

## Unit 43 — reject source-faithful Y/U/V encode breakout

Source inspection exposed a semantic mismatch larger than the planned
attribution counter. Pinned libvpx's `encode_breakout_test` checks Y, U, and V,
then sets `x->skip`; the current candidate wins regardless of prior RD rank,
intra fallback is bypassed, and reconstruction is prediction-only. Rvp9's
existing port checks only Y and uses the result as a mode-loop early exit.
Normal quantization still decides the final residual and skip flag.

A source-faithful prototype added the chroma thresholds, forced the current
candidate to win, bypassed intra fallback, and made the emitter retain pure
motion-compensated reconstruction. Focused luma/chroma threshold tests and the
two-frame decoder test passed.

The fixed-Q effect was effectively neutral:

- q80 remained `25,280 / 40.38 dB`;
- q120 moved from `12,844 / 37.51 dB` to `12,845 / 37.51 dB`;
- q160 moved from `5,914 / 34.25 dB` to `5,917 / 34.27 dB`.

The dirty candidate report is:

`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-f285190c454b-1785437787926127000-p90671/stage4a_product_report.json`

It rejects the source behavior:

- aggregate BD-rate worsened from `+4.9246%` to `+5.5769%`;
- matched-rate deficit worsened from `0.2841` to `0.3023 dB`;
- median time rose from `13.2028` to `13.3620 ms`;
- 250 kbps intra area fell from `3.3606%` to `2.1856%`;
- all seven streams still passed rvp9-decoder and remained byte-exact under
  pinned vpxdec and ffmpeg.

The source prototype was removed. Most breakout candidates already quantize
to zero, while forcing the current candidate suppresses useful later mode and
intra choices on rvp9's different field. The retained departure is now
recorded in `notes/divergence-ledger.md`.

The remaining evidence now converges on the coupled partition/mode field:
common-leaf predictor selection, residual burden, and intra shape differ
materially, while isolated partition statistics, motion compensation,
partition reuse, motion-search penalties, sub-pixel depth, transform policy,
skip policy, Golden selection, and intra shortcuts have all failed their
Pareto screens. The next implementation would replace the compensating A4
speculative RD descent with libvpx cpu-used 7's fixed-tree pipeline: port the
remaining `choose_partitioning` behavior as one coupled package, then run the
causal mode/emit pass once over that tree, equivalent to
`nonrd_use_partition`. That changes a load-bearing partition architecture and
requires Arthur's explicit approval before work begins.

## Unit 44 — add the pure cpu7 fixed-tree parity seam

Arthur approved the bounded partition architecture change. A partner-tier
source audit agreed that pinned libvpx cpu-used 7 uses a fixed
`choose_partitioning` tree followed by one causal `nonrd_use_partition` pass,
not rvp9 A4's speculative recursive mode/partition search. The audit also set
the safe sequence: prove the pure variance tree against pinned libvpx before
changing the product default.

`crates/rvp9-core/src/mode_decision/partition.rs::choose_fixed_tree_inter`
now accepts a materialized 64x64 source and selected predictor plus the exact
thresholds, visible extents, force flags, segment state, qindex, and noise
tier. It returns:

- the final square/rectangular partition grid;
- all 21 force-split bits;
- the exact sum-square/sum/count accumulators and derived variances for the
  canonical 64x64, 32x32, and 16x16 nodes;
- the separate low-resolution 4x4-average `vt2` nodes;
- parent partition symbols and leaves in VP9 coding order.

The source audit found and the implementation fixed three subtle rules:

- low-resolution `vt2` is local to the 16x16 decision; 32x32/64x64 parents
  still aggregate the original 8x8-average `vt`;
- the low-noise spread rule tests the full frame height, not the visible
  height inside the current superblock;
- partial-mi edge superblocks bypass the 64x64 parent probe before descending.

`PartitionDecision::is_valid_partition` now validates legal HORZ/VERT trees as
well as the legacy square-only tree. Eight new focused tests cover flat,
horizontal, vertical, forced, partial-edge, separate-`vt2`, and invalid
rectangular cases.

Observed validation:

- `cargo test -p rvp9-core mode_decision::partition::tests`: 28 passed;
- `cargo clippy -p rvp9-core --lib -- -D warnings`: passed;
- `cargo fmt --all` and `git diff --check`: passed.

This seam is intentionally not wired into encoding, so the accepted product
baseline remains `+4.924609930460733%` BD-rate and `13.202833 ms`. The exact
next unit is the pinned-libvpx replay oracle. It must capture the selected
partition predictor, exact thresholds/flags, original `vt`, local `vt2`,
force bits, visible extents, final parent symbols, and ordered leaves. Product
wiring stays blocked until that oracle is exact across q80/q120/q160.

The later product adapter must also restore libvpx's full six-state
`CONTENT_STATE_SB` producer and raw source-SAD/`zero_temp_sad_source` signal.
The current pre-analysis collapses those states to Normal/VeryHighSad, which
is insufficient for source-faithful cpu7 threshold scaling and partition-copy
shortcuts.

## Unit 45 — prove the cpu7 fixed variance tree exactly

The pinned source reference remains libvpx v1.14.1 commit
`12f3a2ac603e8f10742105519e0cd03c3b8f71dd`.

inside `choose_partitioning`; `scripts/build-vbp-libvpx.sh` applies it in a

reproduced `build/libvpx-vbp/vpxenc` on the pinned Apple arm64 host.

`crates/rvp9-harness/src/bin/vbp_tree_oracle.rs` replays the captured
materialized source/predictor blocks through
`choose_fixed_tree_inter`. It compares every raw sum-square/sum/count
accumulator, derived variance, min/max spread, force bit, local `vt2` choice,
parent partition symbol, and ordered leaf.

The first expanded q80 replay found one genuine mismatch. For very-high-SAD
superblocks, libvpx doubles `vbp_threshold_minmax`; the pure seam had derived
only the unscaled qindex threshold. The seam now takes the exact effective

spread.

q120=`30/30/38`, and q160=`40/40/48`. Each capture contained 90 full-tree
inter superblocks. Replaying all three sidecars produced:

`PASS: 270 pinned-libvpx VBP records matched exactly`

The patched encoder was also compared against stock pinned `vpxenc` at q120
over all eight frames, both with capture enabled and disabled. All three IVF

`19cbe22ba0dc90f11eb1a6d8f5f289cd907c5536a5a277d099fbbdff69fba6e3`

The product encoder remains unchanged, so the accepted strict Stage 4a

## Unit 46 — restore all six source-content states

`ContentStateSb` now preserves libvpx's complete per-superblock classification:
low/high SAD crossed with low/high sum difference, the CBR-only
low-variance/high-sum-difference lighting state, and very-high SAD. `Normal`
remains only as the neutral pre-analysis fallback before a previous source
frame exists.

`PreAnalysisState::compute_avg_source_sad` now follows pinned
`avg_source_sad` ordering exactly. It derives `tmp_sse - tmp_variance`, applies
the 10,000 SAD and 25 sum-difference thresholds, then applies the CBR lighting
override before the final greater-than-20,000 very-high-SAD override. The frame
loop supplies the actual CBR/VBR mode.

This is behavior-neutral for the current encoder path: every existing picker
consumer distinguishes only `VeryHighSad` from all other states. The added
information is the measured prerequisite for the exact cpu7 threshold scale
and source-SAD partition shortcuts.

- `cargo clippy -p rvp9-encoder --lib -- -D warnings`: passed;

## Unit 48 — wire the exact tree and localize the remaining causal-leaf gap

`pick_and_simulate_inter_encode_partitions` now has an off-by-default
`RVP9_EXACT_VBP_TREE=1` product adapter for realtime cpu-used 7/default
content. Per superblock it:

- clamps the cpu7 int-pro Q3 MV against libvpx's `set_offsets`/subpel limits;
- compares the zero-MV GOLDEN SAD with the CBR-biased or VBR-unbiased LAST
  SAD;
- builds the selected 64x64 BILINEAR predictor into a compact scratch block;
- derives the exact effective-segment thresholds from the running average
  inter qindex, content state, dimensions, and noise estimate;
- applies libvpx's base-segment low-SAD 64x64 early exit;
- feeds the proven fixed tree and replaces A4 only for the opt-in experiment;
- then runs the existing causal mode/transform/emit loop exactly once.

The compact predictor helper is a destination-origin refactor of the existing
decoder-matched MC implementation. A focused test proves that its 64x64
scratch output equals the same region produced into a full-frame destination,
including a fractional BILINEAR MV.

The fixed-Q experiment rejected this adapter as a standalone quality change:

- q80 moved from `25,280 / 40.38 dB` inter bits/PSNR to
  `28,153 / 40.34 dB`;
- q120 moved from `12,844 / 37.51 dB` to `14,447 / 37.45 dB`;
- q160 moved from `5,914 / 34.25 dB` to `6,527 / 34.19 dB`.

The result is still a measured prerequisite and a useful localization. The
tree oracle and threshold inputs are exact, while the adapter materially
increases small-leaf work: at q120 its inter 4x4 transform count rose from
34,488 to 43,804 and coefficient bits rose from 9,653 to 11,278 per frame.
The next hypothesis is therefore the causal 8x8/16x16 mode/transform path,
not another partition threshold. Compare common exact-tree leaves with
libvpx's `nonrd_pick_sb_modes` results and repair the first concentrated
mode, skip-transform, or TX-size mismatch.

Default-off behavior remained exactly at the accepted fixed-Q baseline:
q80 `25,280 / 40.38 dB`, q120 `12,844 / 37.51 dB`, and q160
`5,914 / 34.25 dB`.

Observed validation:

- compact partition predictor regression: passed;
- encoder all-target clippy with warnings denied: passed;
- default-off and exact-tree E9 q80/q120/q160 runs: passed;
- `cargo fmt --all` and `git diff --check`: passed.

The F.0.2 pinned-libvpx patch now records each realtime leaf's

`/tmp/rvp9-f02-refctx-q120/run_6a6be64e`

It contains 4,483 pinned-libvpx inter leaves and 11 distinct reference-cost

- single-ref p1: ctx0 33; ctx2 181; ctx4 4,269;
- single-ref p2: ctx2 293; ctx3 4,036; ctx4 154.

Thus 95.2% of leaves used p1 context 4 rather than rvp9's fixed middle
context. The first captured row priced probabilities `(9,142,142)` as

`(9,238,142)` as `[2473,80,2422,2585]`. The new Rust value-level cost function

All seven streams still passed rvp9 decoding and were byte-exact under pinned
vpxdec and ffmpeg.

Under the exact fixed tree, the same costs affect only the causal leaf picker

- q120: `14,447 / 37.45 dB` became `14,349 / 37.45 dB`;
- q160: `6,527 / 34.19 dB` became `6,418 / 34.19 dB`.

The exact-tree adapter therefore uses active costs by default, with

## Unit 51 — reject fast quantization in picker reconstruction

The next concrete mismatch was between the realtime emitter and the picker's
reconstruction/EOB path. The emitter uses libvpx's speed-7
`vp9_quantize_fp`, while `reconstruct_via_round_trip` uses
`vp9_quantize_b`.

On the eight-frame q120 exact-tree trace, the existing picker/emitter luma-EOB
cross-tab was:

- both zero: 1,282 leaves;
- picker nonzero, emitter zero: 191 leaves;
- both nonzero: 2,152 leaves;
- picker zero, emitter nonzero: 0 leaves.

Using `vp9_quantize_fp` for picker reconstruction reduced the false-positive
cell from 191 to 125, but the full fixed-Q sweep rejected it:

- q80 moved from `28,026 / 40.32 dB` to `28,066 / 40.31 dB`;
- q120 moved from `14,349 / 37.45 dB` to `14,320 / 37.43 dB`;
- q160 moved from `6,418 / 34.19 dB` to `6,414 / 34.15 dB`.

The quality loss at every point was not justified by the negligible payload
movement. The change was removed completely. The EOB false positives are
therefore a symptom of the coupled predictor/transform decision rather than a
standalone quantizer mismatch.

The next experiment should not force the picker and emitter together again.
Extend the F.0.2 oracle with the winning candidate's `best_rdc` rate,
distortion, RD cost, model variance/SSE, transform size, and skip-transform
state. Add the corresponding rvp9 diagnostic fields without changing
decisions, then compare common exact-tree leaves by block size and winning
mode. That decomposition can identify whether mode-rate, coefficient proxy,
or reconstructed distortion is the first concentrated causal mismatch.

## Unit 52 — expose winning-picker RD inputs

The shared F.0.2 schema and the pinned-libvpx patch now record the winning
candidate's total rate, surfaced distortion, final RD cost, luma model
variance/SSE, model transform size, and skip-transform state. The fields are
diagnostic only. They do not alter either encoder's decisions.

The rebuilt patched encoder came from pinned libvpx v1.14.1 commit
`12f3a2ac603e8f10742105519e0cd03c3b8f71dd`. The eight-frame q120 exact-tree
capture is:

`/tmp/rvp9-f02-rate-decomp-q120/run_6a6bea55`

Joining by `(frame_idx, mi_row, mi_col, bsize)` found `3,496` common leaves.
Of those, `316` also matched mode, reference, MV, and interpolation filter:
`260` 8x8 and `56` 16x16 leaves.

At fixed q120 both encoders use `rdmult = 64,600`. Removing the rounded rate
term from `picker_rd_cost` therefore recovers the effective ranking
distortion without confusing rvp9's surfaced decoded-pixel SSE with
libvpx's `best_rdc.dist`. On the same-winner leaves:

| size | median rate ratio rvp9/libvpx | median rank-dist ratio | median rvp9/libvpx model SSE |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 8x8 | `0.9665` | `1.6539` | approximately `1.0–1.2` by frame |
| 16x16 | `1.4320` | `0.9924` | approximately `0.9–1.1` by frame |

The 8x8 rank-distortion excess is persistent across all seven inter frames:
per-frame medians range from `1.3833x` to `1.7941x`. It is not explained by
the pre-quant luma predictor error alone. The model transform agrees on all
`260` same-winner 8x8 leaves and on `55/56` 16x16 leaves. The skip-transform
enum does not agree reliably, but that field is downstream of the real
`block_yrd`/skip tie-break and cannot identify which distortion component
caused the winner.

Observed validation:

The exact next experiment is to split the winning 8x8 ranking distortion

term, while source-different, compensates another picker mismatch and is not
an isolated closure lever.

overpriced component before another fixed-Q sweep.

## Unit 55 — gate NEWMV diff-bias to CBR

The realtime encoder now applies `vp9_NEWMV_diff_bias` only in CBR mode.
Pinned libvpx wraps this adjustment in
`rc_mode == VPX_CBR && speed >= 5 && content != screen`; rvp9 had been
applying it to VBR, CQ, and fixed-Q as well. The existing environment switch
remains a CBR negative control.

The default fixed-Q Foreman sweep improved at all three points:

| qindex | accepted inter bits / PSNR | CBR-gated diff-bias |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 80 | `25,280 / 40.38 dB` | `25,215 / 40.39 dB` |
| 120 | `12,844 / 37.51 dB` | `12,751 / 37.52 dB` |
| 160 | `5,914 / 34.25 dB` | `5,800 / 34.26 dB` |

The canonical strict seven-point VBR gate improved:

- BD-rate: `+4.9246099305%` to `+3.5978302724%`;
- matched-rate PSNR deficit: `0.2841111986 dB` to `0.2177688824 dB`;
- all seven streams passed rvp9-decoder and remained byte-exact under pinned
  vpxdec and ffmpeg.

A warmed same-tree A/B ruled out a material speed trade. The former always-on
VBR policy measured `13.304916 ms` aggregate median frame time. The corrected
policy measured `13.402167 ms`, a `1.0073x` ratio. The first corrected run was
slower (`14.509167 ms`), confirming why repeated warmed measurements are
required.

- corrected strict report:
  `target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-99cfa24cd564-1785458710113549000-p23493/stage4a_product_report.json`;
- same-host former-policy control:
  `target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-99cfa24cd564-1785458659812106000-p22713/stage4a_product_report.json`;
- the focused caller-policy regression covers CBR, VBR, CQ, fixed-Q, and the
  environment negative control.

This closes a source-faithfulness defect rather than adding a deliberate
libvpx departure, so the divergence ledger needs no new exception.

The Stage 4a intermediate `<= +2%` BD-rate exit remains open. The exact next

## Unit 56 — do not charge filter syntax for integer-pel MVs

The 16x16 rate investigation found a direct source mismatch before the full
component schema was needed. Rvp9 charged the selected switchable-filter
symbol on every inter candidate. Pinned libvpx adds that rate only when
`(mv.row | mv.col) & 7 != 0`, because integer-pel blocks do not signal an
interpolation filter. In the Unit 53 q120 same-winner set, `38/55` common
16x16 inter leaves used integer-pel MVs.

The picker now makes the same conditional charge. A focused regression
proves that two filters with an integer MV have identical rate and RD cost;
the existing first-filter tie behavior remains EightTap.

The fixed-Q Foreman sweep moved:

| qindex | Unit 55 inter bits / PSNR | integer-MV filter fix |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 80 | `25,215 / 40.39 dB` | `25,196 / 40.39 dB` |
| 120 | `12,751 / 37.52 dB` | `12,792 / 37.54 dB` |
| 160 | `5,800 / 34.26 dB` | `5,853 / 34.27 dB` |

The result is not pointwise dominant, so the canonical product curve decided
promotion:

- BD-rate improved from `+3.5978302724%` to `+3.5384460093%`;
- matched-rate PSNR deficit improved from `0.2177688824 dB` to
  `0.2173301314 dB`;
- aggregate median frame time moved from `13.400125 ms` to `13.518250 ms`,
  a `1.0088x` ratio;
- all seven streams passed rvp9-decoder and remained byte-exact under pinned
  vpxdec and ffmpeg.

Candidate report:
`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-0ac3ad1ee4d3-1785459001418146000-p26744/stage4a_product_report.json`.

This removes a source divergence, so the divergence ledger needs no new
exception. The gain is small and does not explain the full 16x16 rate excess.
The exact next experiment is still to surface the winning mode, MV, selected
reference, filter, and skip rate components on both F.0.2 legs. Compare their
sum against raw luma and UV rate by final skip state; do not infer components
by subtracting from the final total.

## Unit 58 — prove the partition color gate and reject its isolated wiring

The F.0.2 schema now records the partition predictor's U/V color-sensitivity
flags on both encoders. Rvp9 builds the actual 32x32 bilinear chroma
prediction from the selected LAST/int-pro-MV or GOLDEN/zero-MV partition
reference, measures U and V SAD, and applies libvpx's `uv_sad > y_sad >> 2`
test. The producer runs only when the F.0.2 oracle is enabled, so normal
encoding remains byte-identical.

The final eight-frame q120 exact-tree capture is:

`/tmp/rvp9-f02-color-oracle-q120-final-exact/run_6a6bf6f5`

It contains `4,211` rvp9 and `4,483` libvpx decisions. Collapsing repeated
leaf records to `(frame, SB row, SB column)` gives 112 inter superblocks on
each leg. Both encoders reported false/false on all 112, for exact two-plane
agreement on every comparable superblock. Partial-frame edge replication is
covered separately even though the current streaming encoder requires coded
dimensions to be multiples of 64.

I then wired the proven flags through both speculative descent and final
picker calls. The source-faithful behavior failed the fixed-Q Pareto screen:

| qindex | accepted inter bits / PSNR | color gate enabled |
|---:|---:|---:|

`RVP9_COLOR_SENSITIVITY=0` reproduced the accepted curve exactly. The gate
therefore raises payload by `113`, `70`, and `8` bits per inter frame for no
useful quality gain. I removed all behavior wiring and retained only the
exact, output-only diagnostic. The deliberate always-model-both-planes
departure is recorded in `notes/divergence-ledger.md`.

The first rvp9 implementation replayed all four modes after the production
pick. The normalized capture:

`/tmp/rvp9-f02-candidates-q120-normalized/run_6a6bfbd1`

proved that replay was not causal. Its LAST argmin disagreed with rvp9's
actual LAST winner on `422/3,108` leaves because it bypassed the unified
picker's real pruning, interleaving, early exits, and RD biases. Libvpx's
in-loop probe agreed with its winner on every comparable leaf.

The retained implementation records candidates inside rvp9's actual
interleaved loop. A compact handle travels with `PickInterModeResult`; the
full records live in a thread-local diagnostic store only while F.0.2 is
enabled. Normal encoding neither allocates nor synchronises, and the accepted
fixed-Q product output remained exact:

- q80: `25,196 / 40.39 dB`;

close. ZERO's median rvp9-minus-libvpx luma-rate delta is `0`, and its median
luma-distortion delta is `+93`. Rvp9 still charges UV where libvpx's proven
color gate suppresses it, but Units 54 and 58 already rejected that isolated
behavior change.

The causal RD costs expose the next larger component. At fixed q120 pinned

## Unit 61 — add the canonical partition-copy state seam

Arthur approved the remaining bounded partition architecture work. The pure
VBP tree is already integer-exact on 270 pinned records, but the product
adapter still omits libvpx's two early prior-tree copy exits.

`FixedTreeHistoryEntry` now models the common single-layer copy state:
previous legal decision, previous segment, consecutive copy count, and the 25
low-temporal-variance flags. `try_copy` enforces the pinned shared guards:
more than one frame since key, base segment on both frames, no resize,
layer permission, initialized history, and the speed-selected copy limit.
Successful copies return canonical parent symbols and leaves in coding order
and increment the count without replacing their source tree. A fresh update
stores the new tree and resets the count.

This unit is behavior-neutral; no encoder consumer is wired yet. Two focused
tests prove two-copy exhaustion/reset, exact coding-order reuse, carried
variance flags, and every stream/segment guard. Focused core tests and
warnings-denied clippy pass.

The exact next unit is to extend the pinned VBP oracle across all three early
outcomes: low-source-SAD copy, low selected-SAD 64x64, and selected-LAST-SAD
copy. It must expose the history before/after state and remain bit-identical
to stock before the encoder consumes this seam.

flags, and history before and after the decision.

by superblock position. It proves the exact copied coding-order tree, the
bounded copy counter, segment state, and the distinction between the current
and stored 25-element low-variance vectors. A focused regression stores a

## Unit 64 — correct the cpu-used 7 low-variance NEWMV gate

The fresh q120 F.0.2 capture is:

`target/f02-unit64/run_6a6c08c5`

It contains `36,865` rvp9 and `36,828` libvpx records. The two streams share
`32,407` exact `(frame, position, block size)` leaves, but only `3,466` share
the final `(mode, reference, MV)` winner. Their keyframe reconstructions
already differ, so even same-MV candidate costs do not have identical
reference pixels. This live comparison is useful localization, but it is not
a valid causal oracle for changing the luma or UV cost model.

Reviewing the exact low-variance consumer exposed a separate pinned-source
mismatch. Rvp9 treated `short_circuit_low_temp_var=1` like levels 2/3 and
suppressed LAST+NEWMV on every block size. Pinned libvpx uses level 1 for
cpu-used 7 CBR and suppresses LAST+NEWMV only on 64x64; levels 2/3 at
cpu-used 8 apply to all sizes.

`PickInterModeParams` now carries the level explicitly. Focused tests prove
level 1 rejects 64x64 but permits 32x32/16x16, level 2 rejects 16x16, and
the VeryHighSad and disabled guards still win. On the eight-frame 256x256
Foreman CBR exact-tree diagnostic, the correction reduced the stream from
`20,700` to `20,671` bytes. Rvp9-decoder, pinned vpxdec, and ffmpeg remained
byte-exact.

materialized predictor bytes plus the regular model's block size, quantizers,

`model-rd-oracle` harness replays those bytes through rvp9's pure
`model_rd_for_sb_y`.

All `256/256` sampled cpu-used 7 q120 Foreman calls matched exactly:

| output | mismatches |
|---|---:|
| variance / SSE | `0` |
| transform size | `0` |
| skip-transform state | `0` |
| rate / distortion | `0` |

The source-variance sentinel was `UINT_MAX` and transform mode was
`TX_MODE_SELECT`, matching the scoped fixed-Q default-content path. This
closes the regular model arithmetic as a cause of the observed candidate
ranking gap. Do not retune its thresholds or Laplacian model from the
already-diverged live F.0.2 comparison.

Instrumentation is behavior-neutral. Pinned stock libvpx, patched libvpx
with capture disabled, and patched libvpx with capture enabled produced the

`7f9df63b5c44f8bdd91ce6edb66d52d859aa77551ab3af3162c849c3e6daad8f`.

## Unit 67 — restore libvpx's skip-side distortion scale

The two exact pure-seam oracles narrowed the next caller difference to the
skip comparison. Rvp9's Stage 4a VBR calibration compared coefficient-domain
encode distortion against prediction SSE shifted by `2`. Pinned libvpx's
`block_yrd` rescales that same prediction SSE by `<< 4` before the
skip-vs-encode `RDCOST` comparison.

A no-code `RVP9_S3_SKIPSHIFT=4` probe passed the fixed-Q Pareto screen, so
`InterRdCalibration::stage4a_vbr()` now uses the source-faithful shift by
default:

| qindex | Unit 64 baseline inter bits / PSNR | `SSE << 4` |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 80 | `25,196 / 40.39 dB` | `25,183 / 40.41 dB` |
| 120 | `12,792 / 37.54 dB` | `12,750 / 37.56 dB` |
| 160 | `5,853 / 34.27 dB` | `5,803 / 34.33 dB` |

All three points spend fewer bits and improve decoded quality. The focused
caller-policy regression now pins `s3_domain=true`, `s3_skip_shift=4`, and
`skip_modecost=true` for cpu-used 7 realtime VBR while retaining the existing
mode/deadline scope.

Observed validation:

- focused encoder caller-policy test: passed;
- default and environment-probe E9 fixed-Q sweeps reproduced exactly;
- rustfmt.

The exact next measurement is the committed-tree strict seven-point Stage 4a
product report, with `build/libvpx` on `PATH` so the harness resolves the
manifest-approved vpxenc/vpxdec pair. Keep this change only if the complete
BD-rate and cross-decoder report remains a Pareto improvement over
`strict-baseline-0ac3ad1ee4d3-1785459001418146000-p26744`.

## Unit 71 — install byte-exact AArch64 sub-pixel variance

The audit found no AArch64 sub-pixel-variance dispatch. Every production
probe used the generic scalar path, including two heap allocations and three
pixel walks. Pinned libvpx uses specialized NEON bilinear filters and a NEON
variance reducer.

The retained path installs fused NEON filter-and-reduction kernels for all
16/32/64-wide square and rectangular search shapes. Two rolling horizontal
rows avoid both heap allocations and the final temporary-block walk. The
4x4, 8x8, and 8x16 slots remain scalar until profile evidence justifies
their separate narrow kernels.

The focused differential oracle passed all 64 `(xoffset, yoffset)` pairs
across eight shapes, plus edge content and forced scalar/NEON dispatch. The
production microbenchmark measured:

| shape / offset | scalar | NEON | speedup |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 16x16 / (3,5) | `376.8 ns` | `22.4 ns` | `16.81x` |
| 32x32 / (3,5) | `797.1 ns` | `85.8 ns` | `9.30x` |
| 64x64 / (3,5) | `1757.4 ns` | `342.8 ns` | `5.13x` |

Three alternating 120-frame 1280x704 cpu-used 7 runs improved median wall
time from `2.74 s` to `2.45 s` (`10.6%`), or from `22.83` to
`20.42 ms/frame`. All before/after IVF outputs had SHA-256
`aa9c1d98119f20bb977dab0d0335bd60ac151d0344e1299e69b6e68ceee9463c`.
The post-change stream decoded byte-exactly under rvp9-decoder, pinned
vpxdec, and ffmpeg; all three raw outputs had SHA-256
`40c820eff39e9b3b4637a3fa105275258a1330bcac02080819d2ea2116b00be4`.

Validation passed:

- focused NEON differential tests: `3/3`;
- core library excluding the two absent-vector captured-header cases:
  `1529/1529`;
- warnings-denied core and benchmark all-target clippy;
- formatting and diff checks.

This is a reproducible Pareto improvement, but final relative speed remains
roughly `10x` libvpx on this synthetic target. The exact next experiment is
a fresh post-NEON samply profile over the same 120-frame input. Select the
new leading measured leaf rather than assuming the old profile ordering.

## Unit 72 — remove interior reference gathers from integer-projection ME

The post-NEON profile at
`target/bench_inputs/rvp9-encode-profile-post-neon.json.gz` confirmed the
sub-pixel kernel fell from `9.98%` to `1.11%` self-time. The next isolated
leaf was `sad_block_clamped` at `7.84%` self / `9.90%` inclusive.

The expensive part was not the dispatched SAD. Every probe first copied up
to 64x64 pixels through per-pixel coordinate clamps into stack scratch,
including blocks wholly inside the visible plane. Pinned libvpx reads these
blocks directly from its physically extended frame.

The retained fast path recognizes an interior footprint and passes its
native plane slice and stride to the existing SAD dispatch. Probes crossing
any edge retain the exact gathered border-replication path. A focused test
pins that interior calls use the plane stride and requested starting pixel;
all seven pre-existing integer-projection tests still pass.

Three alternating 120-frame cpu-used 7 runs improved:

- median wall time: `2.48 s -> 2.27 s` (`8.47%`);
- median user time: `2.43 s -> 2.23 s` (`8.23%`);
- average wall time: `20.67 -> 18.92 ms/frame`.

Before and after IVF outputs remained byte-identical with SHA-256
`aa9c1d98119f20bb977dab0d0335bd60ac151d0344e1299e69b6e68ceee9463c`.
The cumulative two-unit speed improvement from Unit 70's `2.74 s` baseline
is `17.2%`, without changing compression or decoded output.

Validation passed:

- integer-projection focused tests: `8/8`;
- core library excluding the two absent-vector captured-header cases:

- warnings-denied core all-target clippy;
- formatting and diff checks.

The exact next experiment is a fresh post-fast-path profile on the same
120-frame input. The old `sad_block_clamped` ranking is no longer trusted.

## Unit 73 — reconstruct directly into strided candidate output

The post-gather profile at
`target/bench_inputs/rvp9-encode-profile-post-clamp-fast.json.gz` showed
`sad_block_clamped` down to `1.20%`. Candidate evaluation still consumed
`48.16%` inclusive and `reconstruct_via_round_trip` consumed `7.62%` self /
`30.05%` inclusive.

The round-trip copied each prediction tile into a contiguous staging block,
ran inverse DCT there, then copied it back into the already
prediction-initialised candidate reconstruction. VP9 inverse kernels already
accept arbitrary destination strides. The retained path converts dequantized
coefficients directly into the transform-sized input array and adds the IDCT
at the tile offset using the candidate block's native stride.

Three alternating 120-frame cpu-used 7 runs improved:

- median wall time: `2.26 s -> 2.14 s` (`5.31%`);
- median user time: `2.22 s -> 2.10 s` (`5.41%`);
- average wall time: `18.83 -> 17.83 ms/frame`.

Before and after IVF outputs remained byte-identical with SHA-256
`aa9c1d98119f20bb977dab0d0335bd60ac151d0344e1299e69b6e68ceee9463c`.
The cumulative three-unit improvement from Unit 70's `2.74 s` baseline is
`21.9%`. Compression and decoded output remain unchanged.

Validation passed:

- focused inter-mode tests: `57/57`;
- core library excluding the two absent-vector captured-header cases:
  `1530/1530`;
- warnings-denied core all-target clippy;
- formatting and byte-identical 120-frame encode comparison.

The exact next experiment is another same-input profile. Use it to separate
remaining orchestration cost from scalar quantization, transforms, variance,
and interpolation kernels before selecting the next unit.

## Unit 75 — install byte-exact AArch64 realtime fast quantization

The exact-tree profile at
`target/bench_inputs/rvp9-encode-profile-post-variance.json.gz` contains
`2128` weighted samples. The variance family left the leading tier:
`get_var_sse_8x8_neon` consumed `0.38%` self-time. The next isolated scalar
leaf was `vp9_quantize_fp` at `6.63%` self / `6.95%` inclusive.

Pinned libvpx quantizes coefficients in raster order with NEON and uses
`iscan` to reduce EOB. Rvp9 already owns identical inverse-scan tables but
its scalar API accepted only `scan`. The retained seam passes a complete
`ScanOrder` to realtime non-32x32 quantization. AArch64 loads rvp9's i32
coefficient representation directly, applies the exact fast-quantizer math,
and reduces the one-based inverse-scan positions of nonzero outputs. Other
targets retain the scalar oracle.

The focused differential oracle covers all four transform scan types,
q-indices `0/1/20/80/160/255`, full-range i16 coefficients widened to i32,
and 4x4/8x8/16x16 transform sizes. Skip-block output zeroing is pinned
separately.

The production microbenchmark measured:

| shape | scalar | NEON | speedup |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 4x4 | `24.3 ns` | `3.5 ns` | `6.98x` |
| 8x8 | `64.7 ns` | `10.4 ns` | `6.21x` |
| 16x16 | `235.3 ns` | `36.8 ns` | `6.39x` |

Three alternating 120-frame cpu-used 7 runs improved:

- median wall time: `2.07 s -> 1.98 s` (`4.35%`);
- median user time: `2.03 s -> 1.93 s` (`4.93%`);
- average wall time: `17.25 -> 16.50 ms/frame`.

All six IVF outputs remained byte-identical with SHA-256
`aa9c1d98119f20bb977dab0d0335bd60ac151d0344e1299e69b6e68ceee9463c`.
The cumulative five-unit speed improvement from Unit 70's `2.74 s`
baseline is `27.7%`.

Focused quantizer tests passed `24/24`; the AArch64 differential tests passed
`3/3`, including sparse EOB positions; and encoder trellis/transform-quant
tests passed `4/4`. The widened
coefficient representation is recorded in `notes/divergence-ledger.md`.
The core library passed `1535/1535` after excluding the two absent-vector
captured-header cases. Warnings-denied core, encoder, and benchmark all-target
clippy and formatting also passed.

The exact next experiment is a fresh same-input profile. Do not assume the
remaining scalar `vp9_quantize_b`, forward transform, convolution, or SAD
ranking.

## Unit 77 — remove the duplicate Stage 4a picker transform pipeline

The structural audit rejected the exact libvpx VBP product adapter on the
current large-frame speed input. Although it bypasses A4, it expands each
inter frame from about `410` leaves to `961..1453`; three 120-frame runs were
`2.22 s`, versus the accepted path's `1.90 s`.

The accepted path's candidate evaluator was doing more work than pinned
libvpx: after computing the exact Hadamard `block_yrd` rate/distortion proxy,
it also ran DCT, zbin quantization, inverse DCT, and pixel SSE for every
speculative candidate. The Stage 4a VBR calibration now uses the source
Hadamard skippable result and carries its ranking distortion into the
partition comparison. The selected leaf still uses the unchanged real
DCT/quantize/reconstruction emit path.

The fixed-Q Foreman screen was pointwise Pareto:

| qindex | prior inter bits / PSNR | Hadamard-only |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 80 | `25,166 / 40.42 dB` | `25,085 / 40.47 dB` |
| 120 | `12,788 / 37.59 dB` | `12,624 / 37.66 dB` |
| 160 | `5,856 / 34.35 dB` | `5,770 / 34.48 dB` |

On the same 120-frame 1280x704 cpu-used 7 CBR diagnostic, an explicit
Hadamard-only override measured `1.82/1.84/1.82 s`, a `4.2%` median
improvement over `1.90 s`. The three candidate streams were deterministic
with SHA-256
`8606cc288a5f6d76f17cffb89e113977fe9fee38f1e8b965f80c547a03c07887`.
Production CBR remains unchanged until its own quality matrix is run.

## Unit 78 — install byte-exact AArch64 Hadamard transforms

The post-Unit-77 profile attributed `7.23%` self-time to the scalar
Hadamard row/column kernels. The retained AArch64 implementation is an
integer-exact Rust port of pinned libvpx's
`vpx_hadamard_{8x8,16x16,32x32}_neon`. It uses the same two vector passes,
8x8 transpose, skipped final transpose, and overflow-safe halving
combinations. Other targets retain the scalar oracle.

The extreme-residual differential test exposed a pre-existing scalar
32x32 debug overflow: valid alternating `-255/255` residuals can overflow
an i16 add before the required `>> 2`. The scalar oracle now widens only
the 16x16 and 32x32 combination intermediates to i32, matching libvpx's
mathematical result and the NEON halving operations.

Focused differential coverage compares 8x8, 16x16, and 32x32 kernels over
random residuals, stride jitter `0..8`, zero input, and alternating residual
extremes. All `11/11` Hadamard tests passed.

Three warmed alternating 120-frame 1280x704 Stage 4a VBR cpu-used 7 runs
measured:

- scalar median wall time: `1.92/1.91/1.92 s`, median `1.92 s`;
- NEON median wall time: `1.82/1.81/1.81 s`, median `1.81 s`;
- median improvement: `5.73%`, from `16.00` to `15.08 ms/frame`.

All six IVF outputs were byte-identical with SHA-256
`f29c5a7c748898e6ac2c5cd52e9c1f0537f43d13a6a7c30b7e4179ea8b247ded`.
The core library passed `1541/1541` after excluding the same two absent-vector

all-target clippy and formatting passed.

## Unit 79 — install byte-exact AArch64 8-tap interpolation

The post-Hadamard profile at
`target/bench_inputs/rvp9-encode-profile-post-hadamard.json.gz` contains

became the leading isolated codec leaf:

- horizontal 8-tap convolution: `2.55%` self;
- the 2-D convolve driver: `3.35%` self.

The retained AArch64 path computes eight adjacent output pixels with widened
i32 NEON multiply-accumulates, exact `+64 >> 7` rounding, and saturating u8
narrowing. Horizontal, vertical, and composed 2-D dispatch slots now use the
NEON kernels. A reusable thread-local intermediate removes the scalar
driver's per-call 8.6 KiB stack clear. Widths below eight and odd-width tails
retain the scalar oracle.

Differential coverage spans all four VP9 filter families, all 16 phases,
the complete VP9 block-shape matrix, odd widths `1/7/12/63`, randomized
stride padding, and both single-axis and 2-D paths. A separate test pins

## Unit 80 — reject 16-pixel interpolation widening

The committed post-convolve profile at
`target/bench_inputs/rvp9-encode-profile-post-convolve.json.gz` contains
`1697` weighted samples. The retained interpolation kernels still account
for `5.19%` self-time (`3.01%` horizontal and `2.18%` vertical), so a
bounded second vector-shape experiment was justified.

The candidate processed 16 vertical pixels per loop. On sufficiently wide
horizontal rows it reused two 16-byte loads through lane extraction instead
of issuing eight overlapping 8-byte loads per output group. Differential
tests remained exact and the 120-frame IVF SHA-256 remained
`f29c5a7c748898e6ac2c5cd52e9c1f0537f43d13a6a7c30b7e4179ea8b247ded`.

Three alternating runs were neutral:

- committed eight-lane kernel: `1.81/1.64/1.65 s`;
- 16-pixel candidate: `1.64/1.65/1.66 s`;
- warmed medians after excluding the first cold/outlier run: `1.645 s`
  versus `1.655 s`.

The wider multiply-accumulate dependency chains and horizontal extraction
cost cancel the load reduction on this Apple M5 Max. The candidate was
removed completely; the worktree returned byte-for-byte to commit
`de713d5`.

The next measured isolated leaf is `vpx_sad64x64` at `3.42%` self, with
`vpx_sad32x32` at `0.88%` and the clamped motion-search SAD at `1.89%`.
Audit the AArch64 SAD dispatch and pinned libvpx NEON reduction, then test
one exact SAD family before retaining it.

## Unit 81 — install byte-exact AArch64 SAD reductions

The AArch64 SAD dispatch table still pointed all 13 VP9 block shapes at the
scalar oracle. The retained implementation is a source-faithful Rust port
of pinned libvpx's `vpx_dsp/arm/sad_neon.c`:

- 4-wide blocks pack two exact 4-byte rows per vector;
- 8-wide blocks use widening absolute differences;
- 16/32/64-wide blocks use libvpx's pairwise widening reductions;
- 64-wide blocks keep four independent u16 accumulators before the final
  u32 reduction, preventing intermediate overflow.

The focused oracle compares every block shape over stride jitter `0..8`,
random bytes, equality, and worst-case `0 <-> 255` content. The worst case
initially caught an unsafe horizontal u16 reduction in the candidate at
16x32; changing only the final reduction to widening `vaddlvq_u16` made all
three focused tests exact.

Three warmed alternating 120-frame 1280x704 Stage 4a VBR cpu-used 7 runs
measured:

- scalar SAD: `1.64/1.65/1.65 s`, median `1.65 s`;
- NEON SAD: `1.59/1.60/1.58 s`, median `1.59 s`;
- median improvement: `3.64%`, from `13.75` to `13.25 ms/frame`.

Every IVF remained byte-identical with SHA-256
`f29c5a7c748898e6ac2c5cd52e9c1f0537f43d13a6a7c30b7e4179ea8b247ded`.
The prior three-decoder raw-output proof therefore applies unchanged.

The core library passed `1547/1547` after excluding the same two absent-vector

## Unit 82 — remove inactive picker reconstruction scratch

inside candidate evaluation and `reconstruct_via_round_trip`.

The Stage 4a product calibration already uses the Hadamard-only picker path:
candidate distortion is measured against `pred`, and no inverse transform can
write `recon`. The retained change therefore leaves the 4 KiB reconstruction
array absent on that path. It also scopes the compatibility-only DCT,
quantized-coefficient, and dequantized-coefficient arrays inside the branch
that actually runs the DCT pipeline. Intra and diagnostic compatibility paths
retain their prediction-initialised reconstruction and exact DCT/quantize/IDCT
order.

`f29c5a7c748898e6ac2c5cd52e9c1f0537f43d13a6a7c30b7e4179ea8b247ded`.

## Unit 83 — vectorize four exact 32-point DCTs in parallel

The post-scratch profile measured emitted `fdct32x32` at `4.17%` inclusive.
The retained AArch64 kernel applies the scalar seven-stage 32-point equations
to four independent rows or columns in parallel i32 lanes. Cosine products
widen to i64 and use the exact scalar `+8192 >> 14` reduction. The 2-D wrapper
transposes four-row groups between passes and applies the two distinct VP9
pass reductions before narrowing through the existing 16-bit coefficient
domain.

The focused oracle compared the NEON and scalar 2-D kernels over 128
randomized residual blocks with strides `32..48`, constant `-255/0/255`
blocks, and alternating `-255/255` extremes. Every coefficient matched.

Five standalone 120-frame runs measured `1.54/1.53/1.54/1.54/1.54 s`,
but the timer's 10 ms resolution was too coarse for this unit. Two
order-reversed ten-encode aggregates resolved the effect:

- scalar then NEON: `15.91 -> 15.56 s` (`2.20%`);
- NEON then scalar: `15.36 <- 15.67 s` (`1.98%`).

The scalar and NEON aggregate outputs and the rebuilt final product stream
all remained byte-identical with SHA-256
`f29c5a7c748898e6ac2c5cd52e9c1f0537f43d13a6a7c30b7e4179ea8b247ded`.

The core library passed `1548/1548` after excluding the same two absent-vector
captured-header cases; the encoder library passed `503/503` with one ignored
test. Warnings-denied core/encoder all-target clippy, formatting, diff checks,
and the final release build passed. The four-wide i32 design departure from
libvpx's eight-wide mixed i16/i32 NEON kernel is recorded in
`notes/divergence-ledger.md`.

unit. Select the next measured leaf; the pre-transform ranking is stale.

## Unit 84 — correct the Stage 4a thread-count baseline

The pinned `vpxenc` profile showed four OS threads even though the prior
comparison omitted `--threads`. The CLI's VP9 default requests the maximum
legal number of tile columns (`tile_columns = 6`, clamped by frame width);
at 1280 pixels that is four column tiles. In contrast, rvp9 parses
`--threads` and `--tile-columns` but does not copy either value into
`EncoderConfig`; its frame loop always emits and encodes one tile.

Fresh warmed ten-encode aggregates used identical explicit thread counts,
120 frames of `testsrc_1280x704_120f.y4m`, one-pass VBR, 2 Mbps,
`--rt --cpu-used=7 --lag-in-frames=0 --aq-mode=0`:

- one thread: rvp9 `15.58 s`, vpxenc `3.73 s`, ratio `4.18x`;
- four threads: rvp9 `15.57 s`, vpxenc `1.71 s`, ratio `9.11x`.

The rvp9 `--threads=4` result is identical to `--threads=1`, proving the
flag is currently inert. Pinned vpxenc's default output is byte-identical
to explicit `--tile-columns=2`; explicit log2 tile columns `0`, `1`, and
`2` produce distinct streams. Pinned vpxenc four-thread `--row-mt=0`
matches its default stream and time (`1.70 s` for ten encodes);
`--row-mt=1` produces a distinct stream and measured `1.63 s`. Therefore
the relevant canonical speed mechanism is four independent column tiles,
not row-mt.

The corrected measurement invalidates the earlier 9–11x single-thread
interpretation but exposes the more important product defect: rvp9 cannot
meet the required same-thread-count gate until encoder tile columns and
tile workers are real. The next unit is architecture, not another scalar
leaf optimization: wire explicit tile/thread configuration, make tile
boundaries semantically correct, then parallelize independent column
tiles while keeping a one-thread/single-tile oracle.

## Unit 85 — establish tile-column configuration and writer isolation

Commit `b999c3a` added the encoder-side parallelism contract without changing
the emitted stream. `EncoderParallelism` now carries the maximum worker count
and requested log2 tile columns, clamps the latter through VP9's legal-width
rules, and preserves the legacy single-thread/single-tile constructor.
The CLI now maps its existing flags into that contract and uses libvpx's
eight-thread, maximum-column defaults when the flags are absent.

The next prerequisite made both keyframe and inter tile writers independent
at nonzero column origins. Mode/MV/transform neighbour lookup now rejects
frame cells across any tile edge. Coefficient reads, writes, and skip resets
subtract the tile's starting MI column before indexing `TileCoefCtx`.
Focused tests prove that the second tile cannot see the first tile's left
mode cell and that both inter and keyframe residual walks update local
coefficient columns `0..8`, not frame-relative columns `16..24`.

All 122 `modes_writer` tests passed. Warnings-denied `rvp9-core` all-target
clippy, formatting, and diff checks passed.

The exact next experiment is a shared VP9 SB-aligned tile-offset helper,
including the asymmetric 640-pixel two-column case. Then construct and
cross-decode synthetic multi-tile keyframe and inter packets before changing
the product mode-decision loop.

## Unit 86 — centralize exact VP9 tile geometry

The core now exposes libvpx's `get_tile_offset` geometry and a
`ModesTileBounds` constructor. It divides the aligned superblock count before
converting back to MI units, so odd superblock counts do not accidentally
become equal-width pixel or MI partitions.

Focused cases cover 320, 640, 1280, and 1920 pixels. The verified libvpx
formula splits 320 pixels as `2 + 3` superblocks, 640 as `5 + 5`, 1280 as
four `5`-superblock columns, and 1920 as `7 + 8 + 7 + 8`. The earlier
architecture note's suggested 640-pixel `4 + 6` split was incorrect; the
pinned `vp9_tile_common.c:get_tile_offset` and decoder implementation both
produce `5 + 5`.

The focused geometry test and warnings-denied `rvp9-core` all-target clippy
passed, along with formatting and diff checks.

The exact next experiment is sequential synthetic multi-tile packet assembly
for keyframes and inter frames. Parse the header and tile size prefixes, then
decode through rvp9 and pinned vpxdec before any picker parallelism.

## Unit 87 — assemble sequential multi-tile packets

The keyframe and inter packet builders can now write any caller-selected
legal column layout. Each tile gets exact SB-aligned bounds, an independent
boolean coder, and a fresh `TileCoefCtx`; inter dry counts accumulate in
deterministic tile order before the compressed header. `write_tile_buffers`
adds the required big-endian size prefixes and the uncompressed header
records the selected log2 column count.

Product callers deliberately still pass zero. The current picker and
reconstruction simulator have not yet been made tile-edge aware, so enabling
the CLI's configured layout here would create reconstruction drift on
non-flat content.

Focused 640x64 fixtures cover a two-tile keyframe followed by a two-tile
skip-inter frame. The header reports two columns, both tile payloads are
non-empty, and rvp9-decoder reproduces the flat source exactly. With
`RVP9_CROSS_DECODER_GATE=1`, the same generated IVF decoded successfully
through pinned libvpx `build/libvpx/vpxdec` and ffmpeg; their complete
two-frame I420 outputs were byte-identical and every sample matched the
expected value.

The existing A10 fixed-clip hash test reports length `6632` and FNV-1a
`0x199f6f8880aee622` both before and after this refactor. Its checked-in
`6563`/`0x612efb7c61faa3d5` golden is independently stale on committed
`b44b8e2`, so it remains a known pre-existing red test rather than evidence
of a one-tile behavior change. The focused two-tile tests, warnings-denied
encoder all-target clippy, formatting, and diff checks passed.

The exact next unit is tile-edge-correct picker/reconstruction execution.
Start with a sequential tile kernel and fixed-layout one-versus-four-worker
byte identity before introducing the persistent worker pool.

## Unit 88 — activate tile-edge-correct product encoding

The product frame loop now consumes its configured column layout for
keyframes, show inter frames, and non-show ALTREF frames. Picker-side intra
edges, intra-mode contexts, inter mode/MV neighbours, and reconstruction
DC availability all stop at the current tile's left edge. Reference pixels
and previous-frame co-located MVs remain frame-global, as VP9 requires.
The packet builders receive the same effective layout, closing the prior
picker/writer mismatch.

A focused three-frame 640x64 moving-pattern test uses two column tiles and
constructs otherwise-identical encoders with maximum worker counts one and
four. Their packets are byte-identical for every frame, each header reports
log2 tile columns one, and all Y/U/V samples from rvp9-decoder match the
encoder's committed LAST reconstruction exactly.

A release CLI gate encoded a separate three-frame ffmpeg `testsrc2` input
with `--rt --cpu-used=7 --lag-in-frames=0 --tune-content=default
--end-usage=vbr --threads=4 --tile-columns=1`. rvp9-decoder, pinned
libvpx vpxdec, and ffmpeg all accepted the stream and produced identical
raw I420 SHA-256
`67ef79c9320bea1e46d078b5194dcaa2d8e9f8a2a81dba0e245a9c1323ae5c01`.

All 508 active `rvp9-encoder` library tests passed (one ignored), as did
warnings-denied encoder all-target clippy, formatting, and diff checks.

The exact next unit is to replace the stream-global adaptive RD-threshold
table with persistent per-tile state and extract a tile-owned pick/reconstruct
result. That removes the remaining decision dependency between columns
before a bounded worker pool runs those tile jobs concurrently.

## Unit 89 — isolate adaptive RD thresholds per column tile

`StreamEncoder` now allocates one persistent `RdThreshTable` per effective
column tile. The inter picker selects the current tile's table before its SB
walk, so winner decay and loser increments cannot cross tile boundaries.
Non-show ALTREF coding clones the complete table vector and remains isolated
from show-frame state.

The existing two-tile product oracle now uses deliberately different tile
classes: a flat left tile and a moving textured right tile. It proves the
two tables adapt differently, while otherwise-identical one-worker and
four-worker encoders leave both the packets and each per-tile table
identical. The legacy single-tile path still owns exactly one table.

All 508 active encoder library tests passed (one ignored). Warnings-denied
all-target clippy for `rvp9-core` and `rvp9-encoder`, formatting, and diff
checks passed.

The exact next unit is a tile-owned keyframe work result: independent
partition descriptors, coefficient storage, reconstruction rectangle, and
diagnostic state. Merge results in tile-column order and prove its sequential
output matches the current product before dispatching the same jobs to a
bounded scoped worker set.

## Unit 90 — extract deterministic inter tile jobs

The inter picker can now restrict an invocation to one logical column tile.
For multi-tile encodes with more than one configured worker, the frame loop
creates owned per-tile reconstruction planes, partition descriptors, MV grid,
adaptive-threshold state, exact-VBP history, coefficient source, and split
statistics. It merges only the selected tile rectangle and SB state in fixed
column order. Coefficient keys stay in global MI coordinates and are asserted
disjoint during merge.

This unit keeps dispatch sequential on purpose. The existing product oracle
therefore compares the old one-worker monolithic call with the new
four-worker-ready job/merge path. Three frames remain packet-identical,
reconstruction-identical, and per-tile-threshold-identical despite the
different execution shape.

All 508 active encoder library tests passed (one ignored). Warnings-denied
encoder all-target clippy, formatting, and diff checks passed.

The exact next unit is to dispatch these unchanged owned jobs through scoped
threads, capped by `min(max_threads, tile_count)`. Preserve column-order merge
and keep the deterministic packet/state oracle as the acceptance test before
measuring speed.

## Unit 91 — run inter column tiles concurrently

The extracted inter jobs now run through scoped threads, capped at
`min(max_threads, tile_count)`. When fewer workers than tiles are available,
the frame loop dispatches fixed column-order batches and still merges every
result in column order. Each worker owns its reconstruction, partition, MV,
coefficient, VBP-history, split-statistic, and adaptive-threshold state.

The first concurrent oracle exposed a hidden dependency: the content-adaptive
S2 frame gate is thread-local. A worker without the main thread's decision
diverged on frame one. The core now exposes that decision for propagation,
and every worker installs it before mode selection.

The focused 1280x64 four-tile test compares one, two, and four workers over
three non-flat frames. Packets, reconstructed references, and all four
persistent RD-threshold tables are byte-identical, including the two-worker
batching path. All 508 active encoder library tests passed (one ignored), as
did warnings-denied core and encoder all-target clippy, formatting, and diff
checks.

On `target/bench_inputs/testsrc_1280x704_120f.y4m`, the exact Stage 4a-style
CLI with four workers and four tiles fell from a 1.61 s median to 0.92 s
across three warmed runs, a 1.75x speedup. Both streams have SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.
rvp9-decoder, pinned libvpx vpxdec, and ffmpeg produced the same raw I420
SHA-256
`97e827210c43a5a849300946da40db96026969e071b2e2b4e1b59a0e7e80feec`.

Pinned vpxenc's median under the same input and CLI-equivalent configuration
is still 0.17 s, so the current ratio is about 5.41x and the final speed gate
remains open. The exact next experiment is a current-build all-thread profile
plus one/two/four-worker scaling measurements. Use those results to choose
between serial keyframe work, scalar tile leaf work, or job setup/merge as
the next single bottleneck.

## Unit 92 — remove environment locking from candidate evaluation

A five-encode, all-thread samply profile of commit `3672c19` collected
10,674 samples. Process-environment access was present in 528 samples
(4.95%). The callers were not frame configuration: they were repeated
candidate and leaf diagnostic checks. The largest sites were the two
`RVP9_DIAG_EVAL*` checks, `RVP9_RT_EIGHTTAP_ONLY`,
`RVP9_PICKER_HADAMARD_ONLY`, and three `RVP9_DIAG_PICKER` checks.

The core process-wide trace snapshot now includes the two candidate dump
flags. The filter-search and Hadamard override gates use `OnceLock`, matching
the existing treatment of other diagnostic overrides. The encoder snapshots
`RVP9_DIAG_PICKER` once per tile job and reuses the bool for every leaf.
Default output is unchanged.

On the same warmed 120-frame four-worker command, five runs were all 0.90 s
wall time with 1.64–1.66 s user time, down from Unit 91's 0.92 s median.
The IVF SHA-256 remains
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.
All 508 active encoder library tests passed (one ignored), as did
warnings-denied core and encoder all-target clippy, formatting, and diff
checks.

The exact next experiment is a post-change all-thread profile and fixed
one/two/four-worker scaling sweep. Separate main-thread join/keyframe cost
from worker leaf cost before selecting the next optimization.

## Unit 93 — make the VP9 two-loop count pass count-only

The fixed four-tile layout remains byte-identical at every worker count, but
the warmed medians are 1.55 s with one worker, 1.15 s with two, and 0.91 s
with four. The sub-linear 1.70x scaling confirms a material serial floor.
The post-Unit-92 profile places the inter modes writer and token writer on
that main-thread path.

The encoder previously implemented VP9's two-loop coefficient-probability
update by arithmetic-encoding every tile into a large throwaway buffer before
the real tile encode. Only the explicit `FrameCounts` mutations from that
first traversal are consumed. `BoolWriter::start_count_only` now preserves
the identical syntax/token walk while making its binary-symbol writes no-ops.
It also removes four full-frame-sized scratch allocations per inter frame.

A focused oracle runs the same inter tile through the arithmetic writer and
the count-only writer and requires complete `FrameCounts` equality. The
four-tile product oracle remains byte-identical for one, two, and four
workers. A ten-pair alternating warmed comparison measured the count-only
build at
`[0.88,0.87,0.88,0.88,0.88,0.88,0.89,0.88,0.88,0.88]` seconds versus
Unit 92 at
`[0.91,0.91,0.92,0.92,0.92,0.92,0.93,0.92,0.92,0.93]`: 0.88 s versus
0.92 s median, a 4.35% speedup. The IVF SHA-256 remains
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.

All 508 active encoder library tests passed (one ignored). All 1,553 core
library tests not requiring absent captured-vector fixtures passed. The two
unfiltered core failures are unchanged missing files in
`captured_header_round_trip` and
`write_compressed_header_captured_frame_round_trip`. Warnings-denied core
and encoder all-target clippy, formatting, and diff checks passed.

The exact next experiment is a post-count-only profile. If the real tile
writer remains a material serial component, encode its independent column
tiles concurrently with tile-local counts and deterministic count merge.
Otherwise target the next measured main-thread or leaf-picker component.

## Unit 94 — encode arithmetic column tiles concurrently

The post-count-only profile placed the real inter tile writer on 10.9% of
main-thread wall samples. After the compressed header fixes the frame
context, each column tile now arithmetic-encodes into an owned buffer with
owned `TileCoefCtx` and `FrameCounts`. Scoped workers are capped by
`min(max_threads, tile_cols)`. The main thread joins and merges in column
order, preserving deterministic adapted counts and packet assembly.
Thread-local bit-accounting diagnostics deliberately retain the sequential
path.

The existing three-frame four-tile product oracle passed for one, two, and
four workers. Its packets, encoder reconstruction, and persistent per-tile
RD state remain byte-identical across worker counts. The full output stream
also remains byte-identical to Unit 93 with SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.

On the same warmed 120-frame Stage 4a command, ten alternating pairs measured
Unit 93 at
`[0.87,0.88,0.87,0.87,0.87,0.88,0.88,0.88,0.88,0.88]` seconds and the
concurrent writer at
`[0.85,0.85,0.85,0.85,0.85,0.85,0.85,0.86,0.86,0.86]`: 0.88 s versus
0.85 s median, a 3.41% speedup.

All 508 active encoder library tests passed (one ignored). All 1,553 core
library tests not requiring absent captured-vector fixtures passed.
Warnings-denied core and encoder all-target clippy, formatting, and diff
checks passed.

The next measured serial component is full-frame loop filtering at about
74 ms per encode. Reuse the core split-edge row-MT primitives through a
small encoder dispatch wrapper, then require serial/parallel reconstructed
frame identity and unchanged product-stream bytes before benchmarking.

## Unit 95 — run encoder loop filtering on a persistent wavefront pool

`StreamEncoder` now owns a long-lived loop-filter worker pool capped by
configured threads, legal column tiles, and superblock rows. After
reconstruction completes, the encoder reuses the core split-edge
column-wavefront driver: each worker builds its row masks, applies independent
SB rows, and uses the existing Acquire/Release column progress protocol for
the cross-row filter hazard. Single-worker encoders retain the serial driver.

A focused 256x256 differential test filters the same Y/U/V planes through
the serial driver and a four-worker wavefront and requires complete plane
identity. The existing three-frame four-tile product oracle remains
byte-identical for one, two, and four configured workers. The 120-frame
product stream remains byte-identical to Unit 94 with SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.

Ten warmed alternating pairs measured Unit 94 at
`[0.84,0.85,0.85,0.85,0.85,0.85,0.85,0.86,0.86,0.85]` seconds and the
parallel loop filter at
`[0.77,0.78,0.78,0.78,0.78,0.78,0.78,0.77,0.77,0.78]`: 0.85 s versus
0.78 s median, an 8.24% speedup.

All 509 active encoder library tests passed (one ignored). Warnings-denied
encoder all-target clippy, formatting, and diff checks passed.

The exact next experiment is a fresh all-thread profile of this committed
unit. The former 74 ms serial loop-filter leaf is no longer authoritative;
select the next unit from the new main-thread wait/merge and worker-kernel
distribution.

## Post-Unit-95 profile and rejected experiments

A five-encode all-thread samply profile
(`/tmp/rvp9-unit95-profile.json.gz`) collected 3,847 main-thread samples,
5,632 persistent loop-filter worker samples, and 2,725 ephemeral tile-worker
thread records. The main threads spent 71.7% of wall samples below
`StreamEncoder::try_encode_one_popped`, 41.4% below
`encode_inter_internal`, and about 46% waiting in `std::thread::join`.
The ephemeral workers were dominated by
`pick_and_simulate_inter_encode_partitions` (44.7% inclusive),
`nonrd_partition::descend` (43.3%), mode picking (41.7%), and candidate
evaluation. Persistent loop-filter workers spent about 14% of their samples
filtering and the rest waiting between frame jobs, confirming that Unit 95
removed the former serial filter floor.

Three measured hypotheses were rejected and fully reverted:

- Reusing the loop-filter `WorkerPool` for tile picker and packet jobs removed
  ephemeral thread creation but produced no reproducible speed improvement.
  Order-reversed ten-encode aggregates were 7.87 s versus 7.83 s and 7.94 s
  versus 7.89 s. A first attempt also exposed that a pool sized by loop-filter
  SB rows cannot serve wider tile concurrency; any future shared pool must be
  sized for tile jobs and cap filter dispatch separately.
- Replacing the causal MI-grid row/column splat loop with slice `fill` kept
  identical bytes but was flat to slightly slower over ten alternating pairs.
  LLVM already lowers the simple cell loop effectively.
- Caching all segment quantizer/dequant/rdmult packs once per tile kept the
  cyclic-refresh and product oracles exact but split the alternating timing
  comparisons evenly. The per-leaf setup was too small to produce a stable
  end-to-end gain.

A reusable 16 KiB candidate-evaluation scratch pack likewise kept all
inter-mode, cyclic-AQ, and product oracles exact, but a clean 12-pair
comparison split 6–6 with only about 0.1% median difference. The compiler
already avoids the expected repeated initialization. It was fully reverted.

## Unit 96 — reuse model-RD prediction SSE

Every inter candidate already runs `model_rd_for_sb_y` or its large-block
variant. Both return the exact full-block source-versus-prediction SSE as
`ModelRdResult::sse_y`. The skip-flag tie-break then scanned the same source
and prediction a second time with `sse_block`. It now consumes `mrd.sse_y`
directly, matching the libvpx data flow and removing one complete pixel
reduction per evaluated filter candidate.

The complete 58-test inter-mode suite and 48-test model-RD suite passed. The
four-tile one/two/four-worker oracle and the cyclic-refresh encoder/decoder
reconstruction oracle remained bit-exact. The 120-frame product stream is
unchanged with SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.

On a contended host, twelve alternating four-worker pairs measured Unit 95 at
a 1.157834 s median and Unit 96 at 1.083475 s, a 6.42% reduction; Unit 96 won
9 of 12 pairs. A separate one-worker comparison reduced scheduler
interaction: Unit 96 won 9 of 10 pairs and moved the median from 1.751067 s
to 1.682598 s, a 3.91% reduction. The exact per-pair four-worker Unit 95
times were

The one-worker Unit 95 times were
`[1.990719,2.004000,1.796312,1.750705,2.826042,1.683835,1.735816,1.708936,1.695703,1.751428]`;
Unit 96 times were
`[1.865731,1.803954,1.641290,1.656694,1.764317,1.705517,1.649167,1.642098,1.676939,1.688256]`.

## Unit 97 — expose coefficient reductions to vectorization

The five-encode all-thread Unit-96 profile is
`/tmp/rvp9-unit96-profile.json.gz`. It collected 46,146 weighted samples,
including 10,788 samples in rvp9 code. Inter tile picking remained the
largest compute region. `reconstruct_via_round_trip` alone held 1,428 self
samples, 13.24% of rvp9's actual CPU samples; its inclusive count was 1,555.
The main thread spent 35.2% of wall samples joining tile pickers, while
thread creation was below 1%, again confirming that persistent picker-pool
reuse is not the next bottleneck.

The Hadamard kernels were already NEON. The remaining coefficient-domain
SATD and squared-error reductions used `saturating_add` for every element.
Those saturations are impossible in the supported domain: 1,024
`i16::MIN` magnitudes sum to 33,554,432, and 1,024 maximum differences
between two i16-domain coefficients square and sum to
4,398,046,413,824. Both fit their `u32`/`i64` accumulators. Plain addition
preserves every valid result and lets LLVM vectorize the reductions.
Focused extreme-value tests pin both bounds.

The 120-frame output remains byte-identical with SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.
The four-tile one/two/four-worker product oracle passed.

Ten alternating one-worker pairs measured Unit 96 at a 1.614243 s median
and Unit 97 at 1.537416 s, a 4.76% reduction; Unit 97 won 9 of 10 pairs.
Twelve alternating four-worker pairs were cleaner: Unit 97 won all twelve
and moved the median from 0.797736 s to 0.777440 s, a 2.54% reduction.
The exact four-worker Unit 96 times were
`[0.813109,0.786080,0.800758,0.802455,0.819405,0.827549,0.819755,0.789561,0.794714,0.793138,0.790539,0.792322]`;
Unit 97 times were
`[0.769510,0.766790,0.799221,0.785333,0.798148,0.798742,0.788298,0.779756,0.770381,0.771975,0.775124,0.767754]`.
The one-worker Unit 96 times were
`[1.636683,3.326075,1.649129,1.631903,1.594539,1.611928,1.616558,1.585736,1.574548,1.572994]`;
Unit 97 times were
`[1.581131,1.543638,1.641821,1.692368,1.531193,1.545344,1.530834,1.516056,1.521674,1.511316]`.

The next measured target is the remaining self time inside
`reconstruct_via_round_trip`. Profile its residual construction, i16-to-i32

## Unit 98 — vectorize transform residual construction

The clean five-encode Unit-97 profile is
`/tmp/rvp9-unit97-profile.json.gz`. It attributed 802 self samples to
`reconstruct_via_round_trip`, 10.68% of sampled rvp9 compute. Address-level
symbolization placed roughly half of that self time in the scalar nested loop
that built each 8x8, 16x16, or 32x32 source-minus-prediction residual.

The AArch64 path now mirrors pinned libvpx's
`vpx_dsp/arm/subtract_neon.c:vpx_subtract_block_neon`: it loads 8 or 16
source and prediction bytes at a time, widens while subtracting, and stores
signed i16 residuals. The safe rvp9 wrapper is deliberately narrower than
libvpx's generic block API because this caller only accepts square VP9
transforms with tightly packed output. It asserts the exact source,
prediction, and destination footprints before entering the NEON inner loop.
Other architectures retain the scalar loop.

Focused randomized tests compare scalar and NEON results for every supported
side, source/prediction stride jitter `0..16`, and deliberately unaligned
slice starts. Extreme `0 - 255` and `255 - 0` cases pin the full signed
range. The 120-frame product stream remains byte-identical with SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.
rvp9-decoder, pinned vpxdec, and ffmpeg all decoded it successfully and
produced bit-identical I420 SHA-256
`97e827210c43a5a849300946da40db96026969e071b2e2b4e1b59a0e7e80feec`.

Ten warmed alternating one-worker pairs measured Unit 97 at a 1.588827 s
median and Unit 98 at 1.547775 s, a 2.58% reduction; Unit 98 won all ten.
The exact Unit-97 times were
`[1.638496,1.574366,1.603971,1.587179,1.579544,1.575832,1.590475,1.577080,1.593151,1.594617]`;
Unit 98 measured
`[1.534442,1.518341,1.567840,1.548170,1.525017,1.547380,1.545829,1.549385,1.554290,1.548755]`.

Twelve warmed alternating four-worker pairs measured Unit 97 at a 1.026225 s
median and Unit 98 at 0.983292 s, a 4.18% reduction; Unit 98 won 11 of 12.
The exact Unit-97 times were
`[1.031417,1.066707,1.063941,0.975663,0.987703,1.090502,1.107547,0.974996,0.956863,1.021034,1.052861,0.956774]`;

All 1,557 applicable core tests passed; the same two absent-vector captured

## Post-Unit-98 profile and rejected tile-seed copy experiment

The five-encode all-thread profile of exact commit `bbde201` is
`/tmp/rvp9-unit98-profile.json.gz`. It collected 27,929 weighted samples,
including 8,217 self samples in rvp9 code. The new residual kernel reduced
`reconstruct_via_round_trip` from Unit 97's 802 self samples to 556, a 30.7%
drop. The current compute ranking is:

- `pick_and_simulate_inter_encode_partitions`: 1,746 self / 5,915 inclusive;
- `reconstruct_via_round_trip`: 556 self / 643 inclusive;
- `evaluate_candidate_with_filter_set`: 478 self / 1,607 inclusive;
- `_platform_memmove`: 468 self;
- horizontal convolve: 321 self;
- token writer: 279 self;
- vertical convolve: 200 self;
- bool writer: 195 self.

The main thread spent 45.81% of wall samples joining tile pickers, 14.18% in
the one keyframe, 10.96% in the parallel loop-filter driver, 10.76% in the
packet writer, and 7.38% in `_platform_memmove`. Caller-stack resolution
split the memory moves among keyframe internals, per-tile LAST-reference
seeding, tile-result merge, diagnostic snapshots, pool commit, ME-reference
mirrors, input reads, and inter prediction; no single inter-frame copy
dominated.

One measured copy hypothesis was rejected and fully reverted. Parallel tile
jobs own full-frame scratch but touch one column tile. Seeding only that tile
from LAST, instead of copying the whole reference, preserved exact product
bytes and passed a focused untouched-region oracle plus the one/two/four
worker oracle. It did not improve end-to-end speed. Six one-worker pairs
measured Unit 98 at 1.461867 s and the candidate at 1.472622 s medians
(candidate +0.74%, one win). Twelve four-worker pairs measured 0.927440 s
and 0.930202 s (+0.30%, 6-6 split). The code and test were removed.

Address-level attribution places the largest surviving picker-self cluster
at `int_pro_motion_estimation`. Its projection builders still clamp every
pixel even for interior superblocks, unlike libvpx's direct reads from a
physically extended reference. The exact next experiment is an
interior-footprint fast path for horizontal and vertical projections, keeping
the clamped edge path as the scalar oracle.

## Unit 99 — read interior motion projections directly

The SB-level integer-projection ME built horizontal and vertical reference
projections exclusively through `clamp_px`. That faithfully emulates libvpx's
extended border at frame edges, but repeated two coordinate clamps for every
pixel of every interior projection. Unit 98's address-level profile placed
the largest picker-self cluster at this call.

The horizontal and vertical builders now prove their complete footprint is
inside the visible plane once per projection. Interior windows read native
rows with the plane stride through the existing canonical projection
reducers. Edge windows retain the per-pixel replicated-border path unchanged.
A focused randomized-texture oracle compares both direct builders with the
clamped reference over complete 128-entry Block64x64 search windows. All
eight existing projection-ME and edge-seeding tests also pass.

The 120-frame product stream remains byte-identical with SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.
rvp9-decoder, pinned vpxdec, and ffmpeg all decoded it successfully and
produced bit-identical I420 SHA-256
`97e827210c43a5a849300946da40db96026969e071b2e2b4e1b59a0e7e80feec`.

## Unit 100 — vectorize integral-projection row sums

The committed Unit-99 five-encode profile is
`/tmp/rvp9-unit99-profile.json.gz`. It collected 24,106 weighted samples,
including 6,692 rvp9 self samples. The former all-clamped picker leaf fell
from 1,746 self samples to 432, confirming Unit 99's causal effect. Direct
row projection then appeared as a distinct 456-sample leaf, 6.81% of rvp9
compute and the largest canonical SIMD gap in the new ranking.

The AArch64 path now mirrors pinned libvpx
`vpx_dsp/arm/avg_neon.c:vpx_int_pro_row_neon`. Four source rows at a time
are widened and accumulated into unsigned 16-bit lanes, then shifted by the
height-specific normalization for 16, 32, or 64 rows. The defensive
Block8x8 projection remains on the scalar path because libvpx does not call
this kernel at height eight. The safe wrapper proves each 16-byte row load
and output store, and the worst lane sum is only `64 * 255 = 16,320`.

Randomized differential tests cover every supported height, stride jitter
`0..16`, deliberately unaligned slice starts, and all-zero/all-255 extremes.
The complete projection-ME suite remains exact. The 120-frame product stream
is unchanged with SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.

`97e827210c43a5a849300946da40db96026969e071b2e2b4e1b59a0e7e80feec`.
