## Unit 102 — reject a global EightTap-only interpolation policy

The committed Unit-101 five-encode profile is
`/tmp/rvp9-unit101-profile.json.gz`. It collected 21,994 weighted samples,
including 5,946 rvp9 self samples. The largest remaining encoder clusters
were reconstruction, candidate evaluation, the tile picker, horizontal
convolution, token packing, loop filtering, and boolean writing. Runtime A4
instrumentation showed that 32x32 and 64x64 descent mode picks still consume
roughly 1.96–2.78 ms per inter frame; the realtime final pick correctly
reuses the descent result and costs zero.

Forcing the existing diagnostic `RVP9_RT_EIGHTTAP_ONLY=1` reduced ten
one-thread pairs from a 1.240953 s median to 1.201840 s, a 3.15% reduction
with ten wins. Twelve four-thread pairs moved from 0.730036 s to 0.712461 s,
a 2.41% reduction with ten wins. Pinned libvpx does not apply this global
shortcut: at cpu-used 7 it searches EightTap versus EightTapSmooth on a
chessboard subset and reuses neighbouring filter choices elsewhere
(`vp9_speed_features.c:699-749`, `vp9_pickmode.c:1851-1869,2307-2325`).

A temporary unshared clean-tree commit `00eeada` made the diagnostic the
default so the strict gate could measure the exact candidate. The canonical
Foreman product curve passed with `-7.9703376854%` BD-rate and a
`-0.3301974356 dB` matched-bitrate mean:
`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-00eeada63d73-1785482383954120000-p11178/stage4a_product_report.json`.
The result looked strong but did not generalise:

- 120-frame 320x256 Foreman CBR: `-0.322%` BD-rate versus Unit 101, with
  per-point PSNR movement from `+0.27` to `-0.11 dB`.
- 120-frame 1280x704 testsrc CBR: `+0.081%` BD-rate, but every matched-rate
  point lost `0.23–0.43 dB`.
- 120-frame 1280x704 moving noisy texture CBR: `+52.166%` BD-rate and every
  matched-rate point lost `0.15–0.43 dB`.

The noisy-content regression violates the per-clip and generalisation gates.
The candidate commit and source change were removed completely. Unit 101
remains the accepted baseline at `5ea223c`.

The exact next experiment is to reduce redundant A4 descent candidates using
libvpx's cpu-used 7 adaptive RD-threshold machinery without introducing
cross-tile or traversal-order dependence. Measure candidate counts first,
then test one deterministic threshold policy against Unit 101.

## Unit 103 — reject adaptive RD thresholds in speculative descent

The A4 descent normally passes no adaptive RD-threshold table because its
partition shapes are speculative and the persistent table is updated only
after a final leaf is committed. A diagnostic attached the current tile's
table read-only during descent while preserving the existing post-commit-only
updates.

Ten one-thread pairs improved from a 1.251055 s median to 1.243029 s
(`-0.64%`, eight wins). Twelve four-thread pairs improved from 0.736173 s to
0.731728 s (`-0.60%`, nine wins). The quality cost was not acceptable:

- 120-frame 1280x704 testsrc CBR regressed `+10.575%` BD-rate versus
  Unit 101, losing `0.07–0.30 dB` at the four measured targets.
- 120-frame 1280x704 moving noisy texture CBR regressed `+2.366%`
  BD-rate, crossing the per-clip limit.

The candidate did not reach a commit. The source change was removed
completely. Adaptive thresholds derived from previously committed leaves are
not a valid proxy for alternate partition shapes in rvp9's A4 descent.

The exact next experiment is to profile the keyframe path separately. It is
16.9% of main-thread samples and is independent of the inter-frame A4
quality-sensitive search. Select the largest keyframe-only leaf and require a
byte-exact speed improvement.

## Unit 104 — stop cloning full-frame pre-loopfilter diagnostics by default

An all-keyframe, five-iteration main-thread profile at
`/tmp/rvp9-unit101-keyframes-sym.json.gz` collected 39,317 weighted samples.
`_platform_memmove` was the largest leaf at 8,060 samples (20.50%). Of those,
7,375 came directly from `encode_keyframe_internal`'s unconditional
pre-loopfilter diagnostic snapshot. The regular Unit-101 profile's 289 main-
thread memmove samples had the same inter-frame source: three full-plane
clones on every frame even when no diagnostic read them.

The pre-loopfilter Y/U/V snapshot now exists only while the existing
prediction-stage diagnostic gate is enabled. Production encoding leaves
`diag_prelf_recon` at `None` and performs no copy. The ignored drift
diagnostic explicitly enables the same gate. A focused helper test pins both
the no-copy default and exact opt-in snapshot contents. The real eight-frame
Foreman drift diagnostic ran at cpu-used 7, cpu-used 4, and the 256x256
cross-check; every post-loopfilter encoder reconstruction remained
byte-identical to the decoder and every opt-in pre-loopfilter snapshot was
present.

Ten warmed alternating one-thread product pairs measured Unit 101 at a
1.237164 s median and Unit 104 at 1.232565 s, a 0.37% reduction; Unit 104 won
eight of ten. Exact Unit-101 times were
`[1.237612,1.239855,1.233787,1.235086,1.237083,1.245569,1.235362,1.237246,1.237003,1.237845]`;
Unit 104 measured
`[1.230871,1.229832,1.238968,1.236777,1.229818,1.232501,1.232629,1.233926,1.232713,1.230560]`.

Twelve four-thread pairs measured Unit 101 at a 0.704306 s median and Unit
104 at 0.702028 s, a 0.32% reduction; Unit 104 won ten of twelve. Exact
Unit-101 times were
`[0.702543,0.700538,0.716520,0.706877,0.704284,0.705779,0.704329,0.704530,0.701840,0.703206,0.703088,0.707372]`;
Unit 104 measured
`[0.700924,0.697004,0.702569,0.700945,0.702815,0.703432,0.702278,0.701779,0.702971,0.704248,0.700226,0.701593]`.

An all-keyframe 60-frame control was noise-neutral: one-thread moved
`-0.10%` with four of five wins; four-thread moved `+0.04%` with three of
five wins. This bounds the change's absolute effect and avoids claiming a
keyframe-only throughput win from profile share alone.

One/two/four-thread product streams remained byte-identical with SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.
rvp9-decoder, pinned vpxdec, and ffmpeg all decoded the candidate and

`97e827210c43a5a849300946da40db96026969e071b2e2b4e1b59a0e7e80feec`.
All 510 active encoder tests passed with one ignored. Warnings-denied encoder
all-target clippy, formatting, and diff checks passed.

The exact next experiment is a new all-thread profile after Unit 104. Confirm
that regular-frame memmove fell, then select a remaining byte-exact packet,
loopfilter, transform, or synchronization leaf.

## Unit 105 — share immutable lookahead source planes

The Unit 104 all-thread profile attributed recurring copies to the source
pipeline: `try_encode_one_popped` cloned all three lookahead planes to release
the ring borrow, and `PreAnalysisState::capture_last_source` copied luma again
after every encode. This was an ownership mismatch rather than codec work.
Libvpx retains ref-counted source buffers while rvp9 stored each immutable
plane in a `Vec<u8>`.

`LookaheadEntry::{y,u,v}` now use `Arc<[u8]>`. A popped entry clone increments
three reference counts, keeps the ring's `peek(-1)` source continuously valid,
and leaves pixels in the original lookahead allocations. Pre-analysis retains
the same luma allocation through
`PreAnalysisState::capture_last_source_shared`. The ordinary borrowed-slice
capture helper remains available for isolated callers and tests. TPL forward
lookahead now shares the same source planes instead of deep-copying them.
Focused tests prove cloned entries and Last_Source preserve both contents and
allocation identity.

On `target/bench_inputs/testsrc_1280x704_120f.y4m`, cpu-used 7, RT, lag 0,
default tune, VBR 2 Mbps, AQ off, two tile columns, and 120 frames, sixteen
warmed alternating one-thread pairs measured Unit 104 at a 1.221642 s median
and Unit 105 at 1.215041 s. The paired median delta was `-0.574%`; Unit 105
won fifteen of sixteen pairs. Exact Unit 104 times were
`[1.223288,1.220559,1.220441,1.222555,1.227336,1.219178,1.227773,1.221802,1.221244,1.225378,1.222660,1.222883,1.219201,1.217694,1.221482,1.216651]`;
Unit 105 measured
`[1.215260,1.223047,1.220114,1.218204,1.211558,1.216755,1.220164,1.218763,1.214077,1.208723,1.214822,1.212477,1.212356,1.215319,1.213071,1.211547]`.

The first ten four-thread pairs completed before an unrelated `mddosem`
benchmark started at 08:54:02 and occupied one core. Those uncontaminated
pairs measured Unit 104 at a 0.689506 s median and Unit 105 at 0.680371 s.
The paired median delta was `-1.028%`; Unit 105 won nine of ten pairs. Exact
Unit 104 times were
`[0.694507,0.682431,0.685553,0.689975,0.688221,0.689036,0.696742,0.686024,0.707482,0.711345]`;
Unit 105 measured
`[0.691646,0.679426,0.677837,0.679771,0.681925,0.693283,0.680971,0.679636,0.676240,0.684833]`.
The later contaminated pairs are excluded rather than folded into the
benchmark claim.

The one/two/four-thread product stream remains byte-identical to Unit 104
with SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.
rvp9-decoder, pinned vpxdec, and ffmpeg all decoded the candidate to the same

`97e827210c43a5a849300946da40db96026969e071b2e2b4e1b59a0e7e80feec`.

All 512 active encoder library tests passed with one ignored. All one-pass
integration tests passed. The out-of-scope
`two_pass_allocates_lower_q_to_busier_frames` integration assertion failed
twice with the same deterministic pre-existing allocation result
(`busy_mean_q=191.2`, `flat_mean_q=191.0`); no two-pass code is touched in
this unit. Warnings-denied encoder all-target clippy, formatting, and diff

The exact next experiment is an all-thread profile of the committed Unit 105

## Unit 106 — reject packed coefficient-map keys

The Unit 104 main-thread profile attributed 5.13% inclusive time to the
bitstream's coefficient-map inserts and 1.38% to `DefaultHasher` tuple writes.
The tested hypothesis packed `(mi_row, mi_col, plane, tx_x4, tx_y4)` into one
linear `u64`, reducing each map operation from five integer hash writes to
one. Focused uniqueness, coefficient-source round-trip, AQ, release-build, and
byte-exact stream checks passed.

The candidate did not reduce compute cost. Sixteen alternating one-thread
pairs ran while an unrelated `mddosem` suite made wall time unusable, so the
decision used the child process's user CPU time as a provisional micro-oracle.
Unit 105 used a 1.195144 s median and the packed-key candidate used 1.202083 s;
the paired median delta was `+0.413%`, with only six candidate wins. Wall time
also moved `+0.125%` with seven wins, but is explicitly not trusted because
several pairs were descheduled for 0.5-1.1 seconds.

The extra multiply/add chain outweighed the reduced tuple hashing. The source
and test changes were removed completely; the worktree returned exactly to
committed Unit 105. Do not retry coordinate packing without a profile showing
different hardware/code-generation behavior. A custom trusted-key hasher is a
different hypothesis, but its current maximum measured opportunity is too
small to prioritize ahead of picker, packet, transform, or loopfilter work.

## Unit 107 — right-size Hadamard metric scratch

The Unit 104 worker profile attributed 12.67% self time to
`reconstruct_via_round_trip`. Every Tx8x8 and Tx16x16 Hadamard-only metrics
call zeroed four scratch arrays sized for a Tx32x32 transform, even though
only the first 64 or 256 elements could be read. A local size-specialized
`hadamard_metrics!` expansion now allocates exactly 64, 256, or 1024 elements
for Tx8x8, Tx16x16, and Tx32x32 respectively. It calls the same transform,
quantizer, SATD, and block-error functions as before. A focused regression
test exercises all three realtime transform sizes twice and checks stable
rate, distortion, skip, and EOB results.

The first timing attempt was discarded: the supposed Unit 105 baseline path
had been overwritten by the Unit 107 release build. A detached worktree at
commit `b2e0168f9d38f947305291ccca845103de4a1016` produced a fresh baseline
binary. Its SHA-256 was

the candidate binary SHA-256 was
`43670e42df53398b686f8c98a7160dc3267cb79b786f58f95471819be76b0d92`.

## Unit 108 — vectorize the 16x16 forward DCT on AArch64

The five-encode committed Unit 107 profile is
`/tmp/rvp9-unit107-profile.json.gz`. Its ephemeral tile workers collected
4,293 samples. `reconstruct_via_round_trip` fell from Unit 104's 576 self
samples to 391, confirming that Unit 107 removed the targeted scratch cost.
The scalar `fdct16x16` path then held 195 inclusive samples (4.54%), including
137 in `fdct16_butterfly`; pinned libvpx dispatches its 16x16 DCT to NEON.

The AArch64 path now applies four exact 1-D 16-point transforms in parallel.
It widens every input lane to i32, widens cosine products to i64, preserves
the scalar `+8192 >> 14` rounding, wraps each 1-D output through i16 exactly
like `wraplow`, and applies the scalar `(x + 1) >> 2` inter-pass reduction.
Four-lane transposes retain the scalar coefficient layout. Other
architectures keep the scalar implementation.

A focused oracle compares the NEON kernel against the scalar transform over
128 deterministic random residual blocks, strides 16 through 32, constant
`-255/0/255` blocks, and alternating residual extremes. Every coefficient is
exact.

On the standard 120-frame Stage 4a input, sixteen warmed alternating
one-thread pairs measured Unit 107 at a 1.190997 s median and Unit 108 at
1.175369 s. The paired median delta was `-1.241%`; Unit 108 won fifteen of
sixteen pairs. Exact Unit 107 times were

Child user CPU improved `-1.254%`, with fifteen wins.

The first fifteen warmed alternating four-thread pairs completed before
unrelated host load raised both encoders from about 0.67 s to 0.9-1.2 s.
Those clean pairs measured Unit 107 at a 0.679841 s median and Unit 108 at
0.672061 s. The paired median delta was `-1.336%`; Unit 108 won twelve of
fifteen pairs. Exact clean Unit 107 times were
`[0.672459,0.679841,0.678727,0.676741,0.675986,0.674276,0.676723,0.677854,0.685214,0.690202,0.680642,0.680319,0.688649,0.680858,0.684218]`;

## Unit 109 — reject preseeded tile reconstruction clones

The five-encode committed Unit 108 profile is
`/tmp/rvp9-unit108-profile.json.gz`. Its ephemeral tile workers collected
4,370 samples. The new 16x16 forward DCT removed the scalar butterfly:
`fdct16x16` fell to 100 inclusive samples and only 69 self samples remained.
The largest worker costs were partition picking (4,023 inclusive samples),
sub-pixel filtering (`evaluate_filter` at 1,361 inclusive samples),
reconstruction (479 inclusive samples), horizontal and vertical convolution
(308 and 187 self samples), sub-pixel variance (217 self samples), and frame
scratch movement (`memset` at 165 self samples). The main thread collected
3,741 samples; waits held about 38%, while `memmove` held 307 self samples,
including 136 in keyframe processing.

Tile workers allocated zero-filled full-frame Y/U/V reconstruction planes,
then immediately copied the LAST reference into all three planes before
partition picking. The tested candidate instead cloned LAST into each worker
plane and passed a contract flag that suppressed the later copy. Focused tests
proved both the copied and already-seeded paths. A four-tile product encode
was byte-identical to Unit 108, with stream SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.

Two independent twenty-pair four-thread timing runs did not show a reliable
compute improvement. The first run measured Unit 108 at a 0.668450 s median
and the candidate at 0.666911 s. Paired wall time moved `-0.329%` with twelve
candidate wins; child user CPU moved only `-0.088%` with eleven wins. The
second run measured Unit 108 at 0.668047 s and the candidate at 0.667909 s.
Paired wall time moved `-0.130%` with eleven wins; child user CPU moved only
`-0.055%` with eleven wins. The candidate merely moved the same full-plane
copy into allocation, and the extra ownership contract added complexity
without a reproducible Pareto gain.

All candidate source and test changes were removed. Do not retry preseeded
full-frame reconstruction clones. A compact tile-local reconstruction layout
is a distinct architectural hypothesis, but it needs its own measured design
and is not justified by this result.

The exact next experiment is the measured AArch64 inverse 16x16 transform.
The Unit 108 profile attributes 91 self samples (2.08% of worker CPU) to
`idct16`, while pinned libvpx has a NEON implementation. Preserve the scalar
kernel as the oracle, add randomized coefficient/EOB coverage, and require
byte-exact stream and cross-decoder output before timing it.

## Unit 110 — vectorize the 16x16 inverse DCT on AArch64

The 10-, 38-, and 256-coefficient inverse 16x16 DCT paths now dispatch to
an AArch64 NEON kernel. It applies four independent 1-D transforms in
parallel in i32 lanes, widens cosine products to i64, and wraps every
butterfly store through i16 exactly like the scalar `wraplow` oracle.
Four-row transposes feed the row pass; four adjacent columns feed the
column pass. Other architectures retain one shared scalar implementation
parameterized by the active EOB rows.

A focused oracle covers all three active-row buckets (4/8/16), strides
16/19/32, 64 deterministic full-i16 coefficient seeds per combination,
random destination pixels, and clipping. All 576 cases are bit-exact.
The existing 13 inverse 16x16 tests also pass.

## Unit 111 — vectorize fast 32x32 quantization on AArch64

The realtime 32x32 fast quantizer now dispatches to an eight-coefficient
AArch64 NEON kernel through a complete `ScanOrder`. It keeps rvp9's i32
coefficient inputs, applies the exact rounded 32x32 quant multiplier and
`dequant >> 2` gate, implements signed `/ 2` dequantization with truncation
toward zero, and reduces EOB from raster-order inverse-scan positions.
Other architectures retain the scalar scan-order implementation.

A focused differential oracle covers q indices `0/1/20/80/160/255`, random
full-i16 coefficient blocks, the dequant gate at threshold minus one/at/plus
one, empty/DC/early/middle/final EOB positions, and skip output clearing.
Quantized coefficients, dequantized coefficients, and EOB are bit-exact.

On the standard 120-frame Stage 4a input, sixteen warmed alternating
one-thread pairs measured Unit 110 at a 1.197289 s median and Unit 111 at
1.183059 s. The paired wall delta was `-1.248%`; Unit 111 won all sixteen
pairs. Exact Unit 110 times were

## Unit 112 — reject explicit vector multiplies in the 32x32 forward DCT

At committed Unit 111 baseline `9627fb7`, the captured `fdct32x4`
disassembly contains 260 lines and 24 scalar x-register multiplications.
The experiment rewrote `mul_add_round` with explicit duplicated constants,
`vmull`, and vector addition, intending to prevent scalarization.

The focused `fdct16x16` and `fdct32x32` scalar differential oracles passed,
but generated code became much worse: the candidate disassembly grew to
1,172 lines and 140 scalar x-register multiplications. The source and test
changes were fully reverted.

The baseline and candidate captures are
`/tmp/rvp9-unit111-fdct32x4.asm` and
`/tmp/rvp9-unit112-fdct32x4.asm`. Do not retry this helper-level rewrite.
A worthwhile 32x32 DCT change would require a faithful eight-wide i16
libvpx-style port, not another expression-level vector rewrite.

The next experiment must measure architecture-level redundant work first:
count speculative versus committed picker calls, candidates actually
evaluated, filter evaluations, and reconstructions by partition shape.
Use those counts to select one cache or work-elimination hypothesis.

## Unit 113 — measure picker work; reject luma-only early termination

Temporary thread-local counters on the exact 120-frame cpu-used 7 VBR input
measured 60,349 picker calls: 45,852 at 32x32 and 14,497 at 64x64. Every
queried `(position, bsize)` was a committed leaf under the large-frame
partition policy, so there is no discarded speculative picker layer to
cache away on this workload.

Those calls evaluated 122,982 mode candidates and 139,413 filter instances.
The averages were 2.356 candidates per 32x32 pick and 1.033 per 64x64 pick.
The exact mode distribution was:

- LAST NEAREST: 60,349;
- LAST NEW: 26,094;
- GOLDEN NEAREST: 20,222;
- GOLDEN NEW: 16,317;
- ZERO and NEAR: zero, because the existing duplicate-MV and mode-mask
  gates remove them before evaluation.

The temporary counters were removed after the measurement. Their
instrumented stream remained byte-identical with SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.

A one-line experiment then skipped the Hadamard/quant pass whenever the
large luma model returned `SkipTxfm::AcDc`, mirroring only the luma half of
libvpx's early-termination structure. Sixteen one-thread pairs improved
wall time by `-2.648%` and child CPU by `-2.702%`, with sixteen wins.
Twenty four-thread pairs improved child CPU by `-2.319%`, with twenty
wins; loaded-host wall time improved only `-0.269%`.

The shortcut failed quality immediately. At nominal 2 Mbps, output fell
from 715,048 to 712,593 bytes, while average PSNR fell from 31.849277 dB
to 31.699229 dB and luma PSNR fell by 0.221720 dB. This is far too large
for a 0.34% payload reduction and does not merit a curve run. The source
change was fully reverted.

The remaining picker work is intrinsic candidate evaluation, not duplicate
partition calls. Do not retry luma-only model early termination. The next
byte-exact target is emitted transform/quant storage: each nonzero TX block
currently heap-allocates both qcoeff and short-lived dqcoeff vectors, even
though only qcoeff survives into the writer.

## Unit 114 — reject fixed dequant-coefficient return scratch

The emitted transform/quant experiment reused the existing 1,024-entry
transform-coefficient array as the returned i32 dequant buffer after
quantization. It removed the short-lived dqcoeff heap allocation on each
nonzero TX block while retaining qcoeff ownership in the writer.

The complete 512-test active encoder library suite passed with one ignored,
and the 120-frame product stream remained byte-identical with SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.
The allocation removal nevertheless made the product slower:

- sixteen one-thread pairs: wall `+0.448%`, child CPU `+0.382%`, only three
  candidate wins;
- twenty four-thread pairs: wall `+0.206%`, child CPU `+0.204%`, only four
  candidate wins.

Returning and moving the fixed 4 KiB array costs more than the allocator's
short-lived vector path on this host. The source change was fully reverted.
Do not replace the dqcoeff vector with a large by-value return again.

## Unit 115 — use AArch64 dot products for horizontal interpolation

The unscaled horizontal eight-tap interpolation path now follows pinned
libvpx's AArch64 dot-product arithmetic. Each eight-pixel group shares one
16-byte source load, uses libvpx's three permutation vectors to construct the
overlapping tap windows, and evaluates them with four `sdot` instructions.
The stable Rust toolchain still marks its dot-product intrinsics unstable, so
one scoped inline-assembly helper emits `sdot` under an explicit
`target_feature = "dotprod"` contract. `otool` confirms the release binary
contains the intended four-instruction sequence.

The safe wrapper detects dot-product support at runtime. It retains the
existing widened-i16 NEON path for a filter containing `128`, notably the
full-pel identity phase, because that tap cannot be represented in signed i8.
All other VP9 sub-pel filters use the new kernel. Existing differential
coverage exercised every phase of the regular, smooth, sharp, and bilinear
families over every supported shape; all cases matched the scalar oracle.

The standard 120-frame cpu-used 7 VBR stream remained byte-identical with
SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.
rvp9-decoder, pinned vpxdec, and ffmpeg each produced the same 162,201,600-byte
I420 output with SHA-256
`97e827210c43a5a849300946da40db96026969e071b2e2b4e1b59a0e7e80feec`.

Sixteen warmed alternating one-thread pairs measured Unit 111 at a
1.139109 s median and Unit 115 at 1.105332 s. The paired wall delta was

## Unit 116 — use AArch64 dot products for vertical interpolation

The vertical eight-tap path now filters four output rows across eight columns
with signed dot products. Each group loads its eleven-row source window,
transposes the eight overlapping four-row groups with libvpx's table layout,
and evaluates four low/high tap pairs per output row. It deliberately reloads
the seven overlapping rows at the next group instead of carrying libvpx's
larger rolling transpose state. The existing widened-i16 NEON path remains the
full-pel fallback, and a scalar-NEON tail preserves arbitrary-height behavior.

The existing all-filter differential oracle covers every codec block shape
plus irregular `7x3`, `12x5`, and `63x9` dimensions. Horizontal, vertical,
and separable 2-D output all matched the scalar implementation.

I420 output with SHA-256

## Unit 117 — profile Unit 116; reject exact-size residual enum

The five-encode committed Unit 116 profile is
`/tmp/rvp9-unit116-profile.json.gz`. Across ephemeral tile workers, horizontal
and vertical convolution fell from 329 + 206 self samples at Unit 111
(`12.57%`) to 78 + 86 (`4.47%`). The separable 2-D wrapper fell from 340
inclusive samples (`7.99%`) to 103 (`2.81%`). This confirms Units 115 and 116
removed about two thirds of the sampled interpolation cost.

The leading remaining worker self costs are candidate evaluation
(`438`, `11.95%`), Hadamard-only `reconstruct_via_round_trip`
(`395`, `10.77%`), frame scratch memset (`175`, `4.77%`), sub-pixel variance
(`171`, `4.66%`), bool writing (`162`, `4.42%`), clamped projection-ME SAD
(`149`, `4.06%`), forward 32x32 DCT (`140`, `3.82%`), memmove
(`137`, `3.74%`), and token writing (`125`, `3.41%`). The main thread still
spends `36.17%` of samples joining tile workers; reducing worker compute
remains the right lever.

The first scratch experiment replaced the fixed `[i16; 1024]` residual with
an enum holding exactly 64, 256, or 1,024 coefficients for the selected
transform. Focused Hadamard-only tests passed and the product stream remained
byte-identical, but sixteen one-thread alternating pairs were neutral:

- Unit 116 median `1.085236 s`;
- candidate median `1.085538 s`;
- paired wall `-0.033%`, nine wins;
- child CPU `-0.014%`, eight wins.

The candidate source was fully reverted. Do not retry an enum-shaped residual
buffer. It either cannot reduce the dominant 32x32 work or LLVM reserves and
initializes the same largest stack slot.

The exact next experiment is the redundant Hadamard coefficient widening in
`reconstruct_via_round_trip`. The Hadamard kernels already return i16, matching
libvpx, but rvp9 copies every coefficient to i32 before fast quantization and
coefficient error. Add a focused i16-input fast quantizer or fused metric
kernel, keep the existing widened transform API elsewhere, and require exact
qcoeff/dqcoeff/EOB/rate/dist results before product timing.

## Unit 118 — retain narrowed Hadamard coefficients through quantization

The Hadamard-only picker no longer copies every i16 coefficient into a
separate i32 array before fast quantization. New AArch64 picker-only entry
points load eight i16 coefficients, widen them in registers, and reuse the
existing exact i32 arithmetic for regular and 32x32 fast quantization.
Coefficient-error reduction now widens directly from the Hadamard i16 array.
The general transform and emitted-coefficient APIs remain unchanged.

Focused differential tests compare the narrowed and widened paths across
8x8, 16x16, and 32x32 transforms; q indices `0/1/20/80/160/255`; random
full-i16 coefficients; explicit `i16::MIN` and `i16::MAX`; and qcoeff,
dqcoeff, EOB, and coefficient-error outputs. All outputs are exact.

## Unit 119 — fuse Hadamard quantization and RD metrics

The AArch64 Hadamard-only picker now quantizes native i16 transform
coefficients and returns EOB, qcoeff SATD, and squared coefficient error in
one pass. It no longer writes 64, 256, or 1,024 qcoeff and dqcoeff values to
stack arrays solely for two later reductions. The general quantizer and
emitted-coefficient paths remain unchanged.

The first fused implementation used one SATD accumulator and two error
accumulators. It was exact but introduced two dependent widening
multiply-accumulates per eight coefficients. Sixteen alternating one-thread
pairs regressed paired child CPU by `+0.321%`, with one win. Splitting SATD
across low/high lanes and error across four independent i64 lanes removed
that dependency chain and reversed the result. Keep the split accumulator;
do not retry the compact serial form.

The differential oracle compares fused metrics with the existing widened
NEON quantizers across 8x8, 16x16, and 32x32 transforms; q indices
`0/1/20/80/160/255`; random full-i16 values; explicit `i16::MIN` and
`i16::MAX`; and all-zero, DC-only, and final-scan-position blocks. EOB, SATD,
and squared error all match exactly.

The standard 120-frame cpu-used 7 VBR stream remained byte-identical with
SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.

I420 output with SHA-256
`97e827210c43a5a849300946da40db96026969e071b2e2b4e1b59a0e7e80feec`.

## Unit 120 — gather clamped SAD blocks by row spans

The five-encode committed Unit 119 profile is
`/tmp/rvp9-unit119-profile.json.gz`. Ephemeral tile workers collected 3,724
samples. Their largest self costs were candidate evaluation at 454
(`12.19%`), `reconstruct_via_round_trip` at 431 (`11.57%`), projection-ME
`sad_block_clamped` at 163 (`4.38%`), sub-pixel variance at 154 (`4.14%`),
32x32 forward DCT at 138 (`3.71%`), `_platform_memset` at 130 (`3.49%`),
and token writing at 129 (`3.46%`). The main thread still spent `36.17%` of
its samples joining ephemeral tile workers.

The fused quantizer is fully inlined into `reconstruct_via_round_trip`, so it
does not appear as an independent symbol. That parent leaf measured 431
samples versus Unit 116's 395, while total worker samples increased from
3,666 to 3,724. Sampling noise and changed inlining therefore cannot verify
Unit 119 by leaf counts; its alternating paired CPU result remains the causal
evidence.

Disassembly made the next cost concrete. An edge call to
`sad_block_clamped` first zeroed a 4,096-byte scratch array, then its nested
loop performed two coordinate clamps, a bounds check, one load, and one store
for every pixel. The new gather computes left/right replicated spans once per
block, clamps the vertical coordinate once per row, copies the visible middle
span, and fills only the two border spans. Interior probes retain their
direct-plane fast path.

A focused oracle compares the rowwise gather with the original per-pixel
`clamp_px` semantics for 8x8, 16x16, 32x32, and 64x64 blocks; partial
crossings on every edge; blocks wholly beyond the top-left and bottom-right;
and an interior block over a padded-stride plane. Every byte matches.

The standard 120-frame cpu-used 7 VBR stream remained byte-identical with
SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.
rvp9-decoder, pinned vpxdec, and ffmpeg each produced the same 162,201,600-byte
I420 output with SHA-256
`97e827210c43a5a849300946da40db96026969e071b2e2b4e1b59a0e7e80feec`.

Sixteen warmed alternating one-thread pairs measured Unit 119 at a
1.099502 s median and Unit 120 at 1.072855 s. The paired wall delta was
`-2.371%`, with sixteen wins. Exact Unit 119 times were

## Unit 121 — lazily initialize and reuse edge SAD scratch

The six post-projection SAD probes in one motion search previously gave each
edge call its own zero-initialized 4,096-byte array. Unit 121 carries one
`Option<[u8; 4096]>` through the search instead. Rust safely leaves the
inactive payload uninitialized. Interior-only searches never activate it;
the first edge probe initializes it once, and later edge probes overwrite and
reuse the active block. This removes repeated zero-fills without adding an
unsafe block.

Focused tests prove an interior probe leaves the option inactive and a second
8x8 edge probe preserves a marker outside its 64-byte active block, so it
cannot have zeroed the whole array again. Release disassembly shows the
expected option-discriminant check and one conditional `_bzero` on first use.

The standard 120-frame cpu-used 7 VBR stream remained byte-identical with
SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.
rvp9-decoder, pinned vpxdec, and ffmpeg each produced the same 162,201,600-byte
I420 output with SHA-256
`97e827210c43a5a849300946da40db96026969e071b2e2b4e1b59a0e7e80feec`.

The final exact-code run used a seven-argument helper with absolute
coordinates grouped as `(y, x)`. Sixteen warmed alternating one-thread pairs
measured Unit 120 at a 1.065941 s median and Unit 121 at 1.064660 s. The

## Unit 122 — split sub-pixel variance reduction dependencies

The five-encode committed Unit 121 profile is

samples. `sad_block_clamped` fell from Unit 119's 163 self samples (`4.38%`)

edge-gather leaf.

The new worker ranking was candidate evaluation at 426 (`12.06%`),
`reconstruct_via_round_trip` at 405 (`11.47%`), sub-pixel variance at 179
(`5.07%`), `_platform_memset` at 163 (`4.61%`), bool writing at 162
(`4.59%`), `_platform_memmove` at 155 (`4.39%`), 32x32 forward DCT at 135
(`3.82%`), and token writing at 134 (`3.79%`).

The existing fused AArch64 sub-pixel kernel updated one sum vector twice and
each of two squared-error vectors twice for every 16 predicted pixels. Unit
122 splits this into two independent sum vectors and four independent
squared-error vectors. The six vectors combine only once after the final
pixel. Bilinear filtering, rounding, dispatch, and variance arithmetic are
unchanged.

The existing differential oracle covers every one of the 64 `(x, y)` offset
pairs across all eight production shapes, explicit extreme-content cases,
and forced scalar/NEON dispatch. All variance and SSE outputs remain exact.

Caller attribution assigned 101 memset samples and 62 memmove samples to

measured leaf with an exact behavioral oracle.

## Unit 124 — reuse encoder coefficient-context scratch

`encode_coefs` allocated and zeroed a token-context vector for every selected
transform block, including empty blocks. The decoder already carries a
thread-local cache without clearing it: VP9 context lookup reads only causal
neighbours, and the current scan writes every such entry before it can be
read. Unit 124 gives the encoder its own 1,024-byte thread-local cache and
puts the common empty-block EOB path ahead of the scratch borrow.

A focused regression first encodes the target sparse 4x4 block, then dirties
all 1,024 cache bytes through a dense 32x32 block, and encodes the sparse block
again. Its bytes remain identical. All 21 focused `encode_coefs` tests pass.
The standard 120-frame cpu-used 7 VBR stream remains byte-identical with
SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.

I420 output with SHA-256
`97e827210c43a5a849300946da40db96026969e071b2e2b4e1b59a0e7e80feec`.

## Unit 125 — force-inline the arithmetic bit writer

The five-encode committed Unit 124 profile is
`/tmp/rvp9-unit124-profile.json.gz`, with its symbol sidecar at
`/tmp/rvp9-unit124-profile.json.syms.json`. Ephemeral tile workers collected
3,405 samples. Unit 124 reduced `write_block_tokens` self samples from 123 to
95 (`-22.8%`), inclusive samples from 297 to 246 (`-17.2%`), and
`_platform_memset` from 179 to 142 (`-20.7%`).

The remaining worker ranking was reconstruction at 422 (`12.39%`), candidate
evaluation at 419 (`12.31%`), inter-partition picking at 350 (`10.28%`),
`BoolWriter::write` at 157 (`4.61%`), sub-pixel variance at 149 (`4.38%`),
memset at 142 (`4.17%`), memmove at 137 (`4.02%`), `fdct32x4` at 133
(`3.91%`), and token writing at 95 (`2.79%`).

libvpx declares `vpx_write` as a static inline function. Rust's ordinary
`#[inline]` hint instead left `BoolWriter::write` as a standalone release
symbol and emitted a call for every coded bit. Unit 125 changes only that
method to `#[inline(always)]`. The standalone symbol disappears. Mach-O text
grows from 1,163,264 to 1,179,648 bytes, a 16 KiB (`1.41%`) increase.

All fifteen focused bool-writer tests pass. The standard 120-frame cpu-used 7
VBR stream remains byte-identical with SHA-256

must pass the existing scalar differential extremes before any product
timing and remain byte-identical on the Stage 4a stream.

## Unit 126 — eight-wide i16 first pass for the 32x32 DCT

The encoder calls rvp9's full-precision `fdct32x32`, matching
`vpx_fdct32x32`, rather than libvpx's all-i16 `vpx_fdct32x32_rd` shortcut.
Unit 126 therefore keeps the full transform. It factors the audited
seven-stage schedule into one width-generic implementation, instantiates the
bounded first pass over eight i16 NEON lanes, halves intermediate storage,
then widens each eight-lane row group into two existing i32x4 second passes.
This differs from libvpx only in widening the full second pass rather than
selected overflow-sensitive stages; `notes/divergence-ledger.md` records the
trade.

The focused scalar differential passed all 128 randomized valid-residual
blocks with stride jitter, three constant blocks, and the alternating
`-255/255` extreme. The standard 120-frame cpu-used 7 VBR stream remained
byte-identical with SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.

I420 output with SHA-256
`97e827210c43a5a849300946da40db96026969e071b2e2b4e1b59a0e7e80feec`.

from surrounding work even though paired child CPU improved by 1.0-1.4%.

Immediate-caller attribution assigns 94 of the 181 memset samples to
`pick_and_simulate_inter_encode_partitions`, 35 to inlined candidate
evaluation, and 18 to reconstruction. Disassembly at RVA `0x3ff2c` proves
the candidate leaf: it calls memset for exactly `0x1000` bytes at
`mode_decision/inter.rs:3022`, corresponding to the eager
`pred = [0u8; 64 * 64]` before every `(mode, MV, filter)` evaluation.
`build_inter_prediction` overwrites the complete active `bw * bh` prefix,
and every consumer is bounded to that prefix.

The exact next experiment is one per-thread reusable 4,096-byte prediction
buffer acquired around `evaluate_candidate_for_filter`. Add a causal
regression that evaluates a small block, overwrites the scratch through a
64x64 candidate, then repeats the small block and requires identical full
results. Keep the change only if the product stream stays exact and paired
wall/CPU timing is Pareto-positive.

prefix to match.

## Unit 130 — profile Unit 129; select count-pass reuse

The five-encode committed Unit 129 profile is
`/tmp/rvp9-unit129-profile.json.gz`, with its symbol sidecar at
`/tmp/rvp9-unit129-profile.json.syms.json`. Ephemeral tile workers collected
3,626 samples. The 16x16 forward-DCT leaf fell from Unit 128's 100 samples
(`2.78%`) to 71 (`1.96%`), a 29% absolute sample reduction.

The updated worker ranking is candidate evaluation at 479 (`13.21%`),
reconstruction at 440 (`12.13%`), inter-partition picking at 397 (`10.95%`),
token writing at 205 (`5.65%`), the 32x32 transform at 150 (`4.14%`),
memmove at 149 (`4.11%`), sub-pixel variance at 143 (`3.94%`), and memset at
125 (`3.45%`).

The token leaf reveals duplicate work across the two mandatory tile
traversals. The pre-header count-only pass already accumulates the complete
probability-independent `FrameCounts`, including the full coefficient
histogram needed to choose coefficient-probability updates. The later
arithmetic pass nevertheless allocates per-tile count banks, repeats every
count update, and merges those banks solely to recreate the same adaptation
input. `s5_tile_inter_accumulates_frame_counts` already proves that count-only
and arithmetic traversals produce equal counts.

The exact next experiment is to reuse the count-only pass's complete
`FrameCounts` for backward adaptation and run the arithmetic tile traversal
without count banks. Preserve additive output-slot semantics, discard only
the encoder-only full coefficient histogram after building the header plan,
and strengthen the focused oracle to cover coefficient counts across
different probability tables. The product stream and decoded output must
remain byte-identical before timing.

## Unit 130 — reuse pre-header count-pass results

The pre-header count-only traversal is now the canonical source for both the
coefficient-probability update plan and the frame's backward-adaptation
counts. After building the plan, the encoder discards only the encoder-only
full 12-token histogram, adds the remaining decoder-shaped counts to the
caller's output slot, and runs arithmetic tile emission without per-tile
count banks. This preserves the existing additive output-slot contract.

The focused
`s5_tile_inter_count_only_matches_emit_across_probability_updates` oracle now
uses a non-skip block, confirms coefficient EOB counts are exercised, and
compares the complete count banks while the two traversals use different
skip and coefficient probability tables. The count banks are identical.

## Unit 131 — profile Unit 130; select fixed descent-cache indexing

The five-encode committed Unit 130 profile is
`/tmp/rvp9-unit130-profile.json.gz`, with its symbol sidecar at
`/tmp/rvp9-unit130-profile.json.syms.json`. Samply reported one transient
thread-name lookup error but retained 3,534 weighted samples across 2,636
unnamed scoped workers. The count-free arithmetic tile shape is visible:
`write_inter_block_leaf` has only 16 self samples, while token writing remains
inside it at 236 samples (`6.68%`).

The updated compute ranking is reconstruction at 490 (`13.87%`), candidate
evaluation at 450 (`12.73%`), inter-partition picking at 353 (`9.99%`), token
writing at 236 (`6.68%`), the 32x32 transform at 166 (`4.70%`), memmove at
134 (`3.79%`), sub-pixel variance at 128 (`3.62%`), and memset at 99
(`2.80%`).

Two near-equal Unit 130 captures attribute 72-74 inclusive samples to
`BuildHasher::hash_one`, 53-74 to `HashMap::insert`, and 44-64 to
`RawTable::reserve_rehash`. Direct stack inspection found 88 of 91 sampled
hash frames under `pick_and_simulate_inter_encode_partitions`; only four were
under the emitted coefficient source. The dominant hash work is therefore
the per-superblock `descent_pick_cache`, not the coefficient map whose packed
key Unit 106 rejected.

That cache's legal key space is bounded and collision-free without hashing.
One 64x64 superblock can evaluate at most 85 square candidates
(`1 + 4 + 16 + 64`) and 42 horizontal/vertical rectangle candidates
(`2 * (1 + 4 + 16)`), for 127 total. A key maps directly from its local 8x8
cell, `BlockSize::idx()`, and a superblock-scoped fixed slot table.

The exact next experiment is a generic fixed-slot superblock cache backed by
an insertion-order value vector. Preserve replacement behavior, test all 127
legal square/rectangle keys and absolute-superblock alias isolation, and
change only `descent_pick_cache`. Require exact product bytes and repeated
paired speed evidence before keeping it.

## Unit 131 — direct-index the partition-descent result cache

The first focused implementation used the estimated 127 square/rectangle
candidate bound from Unit 131's selection note. The product stream and focused
test passed, but the full encoder library suite correctly found eight
configurations that evaluate more distinct keys and tripped the bound. No
commit was made. The corrected implementation covers the complete bounded
domain: 64 local superblock cells × all 13 VP9 block sizes = 832 slots, with
`u16::MAX` as the empty sentinel and a growing insertion-order value vector.

The focused
`descent_pick_cache_covers_complete_superblock_key_space` oracle exhausts all
832 keys, verifies every lookup, replaces an existing value without growing
the vector, and checks that local-slot aliases remain isolated by the
per-superblock cache scope. The complete encoder suite then passed.

## Unit 132 — profile Unit 131; select reconstruction scratch reuse

The final-source five-encode Unit 131 profile is
`/tmp/rvp9-unit131-profile-final.json.gz`, with its symbol sidecar at
`/tmp/rvp9-unit131-profile-final.json.syms.json`. It collected 4,548 samples
across 2,564 unnamed scoped workers while the host was slower than the
benchmark window. Stack inspection confirms the Unit 131 target is gone:
hash-leaf samples below `nonrd_partition::descend` fell from fifteen to zero.

The updated self-time ranking is candidate evaluation at 683 (`15.02%`),
`reconstruct_via_round_trip` at 567 (`12.47%`), inter-partition picking at
492 (`10.82%`), memmove at 183 (`4.02%`), the 32x32 transform at 182
(`4.00%`), token writing at 175 (`3.85%`), horizontal interpolation at 148
(`3.25%`), sub-pixel variance at 144 (`3.17%`), and memset/vertical
interpolation at 128 each (`2.81%`).

Address-level symbolization attributes 669 of 683 candidate-evaluator samples
to the inlined per-filter call site. Its AArch64 prologue reserves `0x2ab0`
bytes (10,928 bytes) and touches three stack pages. The body declares an
optional 4,096-byte reconstruction array even though the production
Hadamard-only calibration never initializes or reads it. Rust correctly
skips the pixel copy on that path, but a stack-resident `Option<[u8; 4096]>`
still reserves the maximum payload in every call.

The exact next experiment is a second per-worker thread-local 4,096-byte
scratch for the compatibility reconstruction path. The Hadamard-only path
must continue to borrow or initialize no reconstruction pixels; the
compatibility path must overwrite only its active prefix before use. Add a
causal oracle that dirties the scratch with a 64x64 candidate between two
smaller identical evaluations. Require a smaller candidate-evaluator stack
frame, exact results, exact product bytes, and paired speed evidence.

## Unit 132 — reject reusable reconstruction scratch

The experiment moved compatibility-mode reconstruction pixels into a second
thread-local buffer and force-inlined the shared evaluator body. The existing
small/large/small candidate oracle passed after its assertion was widened to
cover both prediction and reconstruction scratch. All 1,569 applicable core
tests and 514 active encoder tests passed, with the usual two absent-vector
filters and one ignored encoder test. Warnings-denied core clippy also passed.

The structural result was real. Unit 131's inlined evaluator reserved
10,928 stack bytes and touched three pages. The final experimental build
reserved 2,992 bytes and had no out-of-line evaluator call. Product and
forced compatibility streams both remained byte-identical to Unit 131. The
standard product stream retained SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.
rvp9-decoder, pinned vpxdec, and ffmpeg each produced the same 162,201,600-byte
I420 output with SHA-256
`97e827210c43a5a849300946da40db96026969e071b2e2b4e1b59a0e7e80feec`.

Speed did not improve reproducibly. A first non-inlined version regressed by
`+0.227%` at one thread and `+0.577%` at four threads. Force-inlining removed
the call, but the two one-thread trials reported paired wall deltas of
`+0.332%` and `+0.606%`; child CPU deltas were `-0.178%` and `+1.302%`.
The two four-thread trials reported paired wall deltas of `+1.429%` and
`-0.149%`; child CPU deltas were `+0.224%` and `-0.148%`. The host carried a
concurrent emulator benchmark and Spotlight activity, but the paired results
still provide no repeatable product-speed win. The source change was fully
reverted. No divergence-ledger entry is needed.

The exact next experiment is a fresh committed Unit 131 profile after the
host load settles. Treat the large but cold reconstruction stack reservation
as rejected unless a future measured stack-pressure bottleneck supplies new
evidence. Select one remaining sampled leaf and require exact bytes plus
paired speed evidence.

## Unit 133 — profile Unit 131; select direct inter-coefficient slots

The five-encode Unit 131 capture is
`/tmp/rvp9-unit133-profile.json.gz`, with symbols in
`/tmp/rvp9-unit133-profile.json.syms.json`. Concurrent host work inflated
kernel wait samples, so the ranking below uses the 4,707 samples whose leaf
PC resolves inside rvp9. The largest self symbols are
`reconstruct_via_round_trip` at 419 (`8.90%`), candidate evaluation at 403
(`8.56%`), `try_encode_one_popped` at 375 (`7.97%`), coefficient token
writing at 336 (`7.14%`), inter partition simulation at 316 (`6.71%`), and
the 32x32 forward transform at 159 (`3.38%`).

Hash-table machinery accounts for 182 self samples (`3.87%`):
`DefaultHasher::write` 88, `HashMap::insert` 37,
`BuildHasher::hash_one` 33, and `reserve_rehash` 24. Stack attribution puts
nearly all of those samples below
`pick_and_simulate_inter_encode_partitions`,
`StreamEncoder::encode_inter_internal`, or
`VecBackedInterCoefSource::get_block_coefs`. The few remaining paths belong
to the single keyframe's intra coefficient map.

The inter map's six-field tuple is a sparse representation of a bounded
physical coordinate. For luma, global 4x4 coordinates are
`(mi_col * 2 + tx_x4_local, mi_row * 2 + tx_y4_local)`; for 4:2:0 chroma
the multipliers are one. Three dense plane ranges therefore need only
84,480 `u32` slots at 1280x704, or about 330 KiB per tile source, while the
coefficient payloads remain in their existing insertion-order vector.

The exact next experiment is to replace only
`VecBackedInterCoefSource`'s `HashMap` with `u32` slot-to-value indices.
Keep the keyframe intra source unchanged. Preserve replacement behavior,
zero-EOB markers, disjoint tile merges, and `InterCoefSource: Sync`. Add a
focused oracle that exhausts every luma/chroma slot plus merge and
replacement tests. Require exact product bytes, cross-decoder identity, and
repeated one-/four-thread speed evidence before keeping the change.

## Unit 133 — direct-index inter coefficient payloads

`VecBackedInterCoefSource` now maps physical plane-relative 4x4 coordinates
through dense `u32` slots into its insertion-order coefficient vector. Luma
uses two 4x4 cells per MI; Profile-0 U/V use one. Tile merges append the
owned payload vectors and rebase only occupied slots. The keyframe-only intra
coefficient source remains hashed.

Three focused tests exhaust all luma/U/V slots at odd dimensions, verify
replacement and disjoint merge behavior, and require overlapping tile
merges to panic. The existing round-trip and zero-EOB-marker tests pass.

## Unit 134 — exact AArch64 NEON 16-tap loop filter

The four AArch64 16-tap DSP slots now dispatch to eight-lane NEON kernels.
Horizontal kernels load contiguous lanes. Vertical kernels transpose a
16-by-8 neighborhood, filter across vectors, and scatter the changed columns.
Dual slots apply the same single-edge kernel at the two VP9 edge offsets.

The mask deliberately differs from libvpx's saturating-byte implementation:
rvp9 widens
`2 * abs(p0 - q0) + abs(p1 - q1) / 2` to `u16` before comparing it with
`blimit`. That preserves the existing scalar result across the complete
`u8` threshold API, including `blimit = 255`. Staged vector selects preserve
mixed flat/flat2 lanes without aggregate branch shortcuts.

Two randomized differential tests cover 256 seeds each across single and
dual horizontal/vertical kernels, varied strides, random thresholds, flat
regions, step edges, and all-255 thresholds. They compare the complete
backing plane with the scalar kernels. The existing
`split_edge_parallel_filter_matches_serial_planes_bit_exact` reconstructed
Y/U/V oracle also passed. All 1,572 applicable core tests passed after
filtering the two absent-vector cases; all 517 active encoder library tests
passed with one ignored. Warnings-denied core all-target clippy, formatting,
and diff checks passed.

The standard 120-frame cpu-used 7 VBR stream remained byte-identical with

rvp9-decoder, pinned vpxdec, and ffmpeg each produced the same 162,201,600-byte
I420 output with SHA-256
`97e827210c43a5a849300946da40db96026969e071b2e2b4e1b59a0e7e80feec`.

## Unit 135 — select redundant product-picker SSE removal

The committed Unit 134 profile attributes 640 rvp9 self samples (`10.26%`)
to the inlined candidate evaluator. Of those, 460 land in the loop at
binary offsets `+0x10a0..+0x10f0`; disassembly shows source/pred byte loads,
subtract-square-accumulate, and its row/column bounds. This is
`sse_block(src, recon_pixels)`.

The Stage 4a product calibration always enables Hadamard-only picking.
Therefore `recon_pixels` is exactly `pred`, and the scan returns
`SSE(src, pred)`. The earlier `model_rd_for_sb_y{,_large}` call has already
computed that same complete block reduction as `mrd.sse_y`; the skip
tie-break deliberately reuses it later in the function. The product path
currently scans every candidate's luma pixels twice for one identical value.

The exact next experiment is to use `mrd.sse_y` as the surfaced pixel
distortion when `hadamard_only` is true, retaining `sse_block` only for the
compatibility reconstruction path. Add a focused result-identity oracle that
exercises both calibrations and a direct varied-shape SSE/model equality
oracle. Require exact product bytes, cross-decoder identity, and repeated
paired one-/four-thread speed evidence before keeping it.

## Unit 135 — reuse exact model-RD SSE in the product picker

The product Hadamard-only evaluator now surfaces `mrd.sse_y` instead of
rescanning source against the unchanged prediction. The compatibility
calibration still computes pixel SSE after its inverse-transform
reconstruction. The focused oracle independently builds zero-MV predictions
for 8x8, 16x16, 32x32, and 64x64 blocks, then requires direct pixel SSE,
model-RD `sse_y`, and candidate `best_distortion` to agree exactly.

## Unit 136 — select full-width Hadamard dequant multiply

The committed Unit 135 address map puts most
`reconstruct_via_round_trip` samples in the 8x8/16x16 Hadamard
quantize-metrics body. Its `quantize_i16_8` dequant step expresses the low
and high halves as two widening four-lane multiplies, then narrows and
combines them. Disassembly strength-reduces each widening/narrowing chain,
but still emits two `mul.4h` instructions per eight coefficients.

The scalar contract casts `qcoeff * dequant` back to `i16`, so only the low
16 product bits are observable. A single `vmulq_s16` over all eight lanes is
exactly equivalent, and the existing metric accumulator can widen that
result unchanged.

The exact next experiment is to replace only that Hadamard-metrics dequant
expression with one eight-lane low-half multiply. Require the existing
random/extreme metrics differential, exact product bytes, and paired timing.
Reject it if the final machine text or product timing does not improve.

## Unit 136 — full-width Hadamard dequant multiply

`quantize_i16_8` now uses one `vmulq_s16` for eight dequant products. The
final machine text replaces each pair of `mul.4h` instructions with one
`mul.8h`. The existing differential covers random, zero, DC-only,
final-scan-only, `i16::MIN`, and `i16::MAX` coefficients for Tx8x8, Tx16x16,
and Tx32x32 across qindices 0, 1, 20, 80, 160, and 255.

## Unit 137 — select mixed direct/clamped integer projections

The committed Unit 135 profile attributes 81 self samples directly to
integer-projection ME: 32 at the clamped row/column loop backedge, 15 in the
projection setup, 17 in `vector_match`, and 17 in its supporting sampled
symbols. Disassembly shows the edge path repeatedly clamping both coordinates
inside every pixel sum.

At a 64x64 frame-edge superblock, most of the 128-column projection window
is still visible. The current all-or-nothing `fill_int_pro_rows` gate sends
all eight 16-column chunks through scalar clamping when only two chunks lie
outside the left or right edge. The column projection similarly sends every
row through per-pixel clamping when only the top or bottom portion is
replicated.

The exact next experiment is to choose the direct NEON row kernel per
16-column chunk, hoist fixed coordinates out of clamped inner loops, and sum
clamped column rows as left/middle/right spans. Add a pixel-oracle
differential for partial and fully outside projection windows. Preserve every
integer projection, selected MV, SAD, and product byte before measuring when
host contention permits.

## Unit 137 — mixed direct/clamped integer projections

`fill_int_pro_rows` now chooses the direct 16-column NEON kernel per chunk,
so six of eight chunks remain vectorized at a 64x64 left/right edge SB.
Clamped row projections hoist their fixed x coordinate and use a direct
vertical walk whenever y is visible. Clamped column projections reduce a
row as replicated-left, contiguous-visible, and replicated-right spans
after clamping y once.

The independent pixel oracle covers projection widths/heights 16, 32, and
64; partial and fully outside x/y windows; a 97x83 visible plane; and a
104-byte stride. Every result matches the literal per-pixel clamp. All 1,574
applicable core tests passed after filtering the two absent-vector cases;
all 517 active encoder tests passed with one ignored. Warnings-denied
core/encoder all-target clippy, formatting, and diff checks passed.

## Unit 138 — select retained coefficient tokens

The committed Unit 137 profile attributes 312 self samples, 8.47% of all
rvp9 samples, to `write_block_tokens`. Source inspection finds that rvp9
scans, classifies, and context-models every coefficient twice: once in the
pre-header count pass and again in the final arithmetic pass. The count pass
must precede coefficient-probability updates, but coefficient values, token
classes, scan bands, and neighbour contexts do not depend on those
probabilities.

Pinned libvpx already separates these concerns. `vp9_tokenize.c::tokenize_b`
retains `TOKENEXTRA` records before the compressed header, then
`vp9_bitstream.c::pack_mb_tokens` replays them against the post-header
probability tables. The exact next experiment is the same architecture in
the narrow inter-tile path: retain symbolic coefficient records per tile
during the canonical count pass, then replay them during final tile packing.

Keep the existing public tile writer unchanged for decoder tests and other
callers. Add an opt-in record/replay writer entry point, reject a replay whose
block count differs from the syntax traversal, and preserve final
post-header probability lookup. Require byte-identical product output,
focused direct-vs-replay token tests, all three cross-decoders, and repeated
one-/four-thread timing before accepting the change.

## Unit 138 — retained coefficient tokens

The pre-header count pass now retains one compact coefficient stream per
tile. Each four-byte token stores its signed value plus scan band and
neighbour-derived context; separate block boundaries retain the coefficient
context writeback flag. Final tile workers replay immutable per-tile streams
against the post-header coefficient probabilities. The direct public tile
writer remains unchanged, and replay returns
`ModesWriterError::RetainedCoefMismatch` unless it consumes every recorded
block and token.

The count-only writer skips discarded coefficient arithmetic while recording.
Replay bypasses scan selection and neighbour-context folding. A focused
probability-change oracle covers empty blocks, zero runs, signed low tokens,
CAT1 through CAT6 representatives, terminal EOB, and full-to-`seg_eob`
blocks. It proves replay bytes match a direct encode under a probability
table different from the recording table.

All 1,576 applicable core tests passed after filtering the two absent-vector
cases; all 517 active encoder tests passed with one ignored. Warnings-denied
core/encoder all-target clippy, formatting, and diff checks passed. The
standard cpu-used 7 product stream remains byte-identical with SHA-256
`7b60c3c0e59d69ed0199729744aab63c9cde6887438e94e5f905b6ff2158c8da`.
rvp9-decoder, pinned vpxdec, and ffmpeg each produced the same 162,201,600-byte
I420 output with SHA-256
`97e827210c43a5a849300946da40db96026969e071b2e2b4e1b59a0e7e80feec`.

The matched five-encode profiles are
`/tmp/rvp9-unit137-profile-current.json.gz` and
`/tmp/rvp9-unit138-profile-v3.json.gz`. The inclusive coefficient path fell
from 427 baseline self samples to 359 retained-path samples. Repeated timings
were not usable as final evidence: load average reached 41 while unrelated
`mddosem`, compiler, git, Spotlight, and media-analysis work saturated
multiple cores. One-thread paired child-CPU results ranged from `+0.384%` to
`+1.324%`, and the four-thread series jumped between about 0.7 and 3.6
seconds. Keep this unit as the measured libvpx-shaped prerequisite and
re-measure its cumulative effect in a clean host window.

The next experiment is to use the retained syntax boundary to avoid more of
the duplicate final tile traversal, or select the next top profile cluster
if that extension cannot be kept narrow and byte-exact.

## Unit 139 — select libvpx two-stage interpolation-filter search

The committed Unit 138 profile attributes 750 inclusive samples to
`evaluate_candidate_with_filter_set` and 393 to
`reconstruct_via_round_trip`. rvp9 currently calls the complete candidate
evaluator for both `EightTap` and `EightTapSmooth`, so every searched
fractional-MV candidate pays Hadamard coefficient RD, skip arbitration, and
UV model RD twice.

Pinned libvpx does not. `vp9_pickmode.c::search_filter_ref` builds each luma
prediction and ranks filters using only `model_rd_for_sb_y{_large}` plus the
switchable-filter signalling cost. After it selects the filter, the outer
picker calls `block_yrd` once and then UV model RD once. The current rvp9
function comment claiming that full `block_yrd` runs per filter is stale and
behaviorally wrong.

The exact next experiment is to factor rvp9's existing luma model into a
shared helper, rank multi-filter sets in that model domain, and invoke the
complete candidate evaluator only for the selected filter. Preserve the
single-filter path. Add a focused oracle for model-domain selection and
require cross-decoder correctness, measured speed, and fresh Stage 4a
quality evidence because the corrected filter choice can deliberately change
the stream.

## Unit 139 — two-stage filter-search candidate

Multi-filter sets now build and score each luma prediction with the existing
`model_rd_for_sb_y{_large}` result plus its switchable-filter cost. The
complete Hadamard/skip/UV candidate evaluator runs only for the model-domain
winner. Single-filter calls are unchanged. The focused sub-pel oracle
independently computes both model costs and requires the multi-filter result
to equal the complete direct evaluation of the expected winner.

`c938181f215f71b246a8b10bf2ba9477c6f6e04b49c0c0ab6917aa692e79102e`.

## Unit 169 — accept paired widened 32x32 DCT groups

The committed Unit 168 profile selected the 32x32 forward DCT again: 165
self samples, or `4.31%` of resolved rvp9 self work. Its deterministic kernel
measured `724.15 ns`, versus pinned libvpx near `503.50 ns`.

Both encoders keep second-pass stages 1 and 2 in eight i16 lanes and widen at
stage 3. The remaining structural mismatch was that rvp9 ran the low and high
four-lane groups through the generic i32 schedule in two separate calls,
while libvpx advances paired `c_lo`/`c_hi` arrays together. The retained
`VPair` specialization advances both groups in one schedule and preserves the
same i32 arithmetic and final narrowing.

The kernel improves to `687.23..708.00 ns` (`-2.23%..-5.10%`) with
unchanged audit hashes: input `7ff2a73daf767cfc`, output
`e3d9e87fbeeef2a4`. Two twenty-pair one-thread comparisons improve total
child CPU by `-0.739%` and `-0.697%`.
Two twenty-pair four-thread comparisons improve it by `-1.491%` and
`-0.261%`; paired wall time changes by `-0.900%` and `-0.197%`. The second
one-thread wall trial was invalidated by severe external contention, but its
child-CPU result stayed aligned.

The standard cpu-used 7 stream remains exactly 716,330 bytes with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.
Run the full forward-transform tests, clippy, formatting, and diff checks
before committing, then profile the committed binary again.

The green gate completed: all 40 forward-transform tests pass. The
randomized/extreme scalar differential also passes after the final loop
syntax cleanup. Core all-target clippy passes with warnings denied;
formatting and diff checks pass. The final stream hash and size remain exact.

## Unit 170 — reject rounded-high 32-bit DCT multiply

Pinned libvpx uses AArch64 `sqrdmulh` for the 32-bit one-coefficient
butterflies after the 32x32 second-pass widening boundary. rvp9 uses an
explicit i64 product and exact `+8192 >> 14` reduction. Replacing only those
one-coefficient operations with `vqrdmulhq_s32` remained bit-exact across the
randomized, constant, alternating, and stride-jitter differential.

It was slower on this Apple-silicon host. Two deterministic runs measured
the candidate at `739.54 ns` and `759.69 ns`, versus Unit 169's
`687.23..708.00 ns`. The source change is fully removed. Do not copy
libvpx's rounded-high instruction choice without a different measured host
or a larger schedule change that alters its surrounding dependencies.

The standard 120-frame stream grows from 715,048 to 716,330 bytes (`+0.179%`)
while average PSNR improves from 31.849277 to 31.935117 dB (`+0.085840 dB`);
luma PSNR improves `+0.148990 dB`. Sixteen alternating one-thread pairs
improve paired wall/child CPU by `-2.144%`/`-1.857%`, with 11/16 wins.
Four-thread measurements remain contaminated by external saturation:
paired wall reported `-0.497%`, but child CPU `+0.295%`.

Commit the compiling candidate so the official product runner can enforce
its clean-tree provenance gate. Accept it only if the fresh seven-point
Foreman Stage 4a report remains within the strict BD-rate gate.

## Unit 139 — accept libvpx two-stage interpolation-filter search

The clean-tree strict Stage 4a product report at
`target/stage4a_product/strict-baseline-d538603c0d52-1785503420444809000-p18395/stage4a_product_report.json`
passes. It uses pinned libvpx v1.14.1 commit
`12f3a2ac603e8f10742105519e0cd03c3b8f71dd`, cpu-used 7, realtime VBR,
lag zero, AQ off, and seven Foreman operating points. Its aggregate PSNR
BD-rate is `-8.585847331540%`, improving the previous committed strict
baseline's `+0.156544919758%`. An independent normal-equation cubic fit over
the report's raw RD points reproduced `-8.585847331270%`, an absolute
difference of `2.70e-10` percentage points. The changed curve improves every
rvp9 point by `+0.236` to `+1.091 dB` at nearly unchanged bitrate. All seven
streams pass rvp9-decoder, and pinned vpxdec and ffmpeg are byte-exact with
its decoded output.

The official paired product-policy run repeated the decoder results and
reported `-8.309905438780%` BD-rate with the `Slack1Over8` split policy. Its
timing verdict is rejected as contaminated evidence: unrelated Rust builds,
two `mddosem` jobs, and Spotlight held the host above load 19, and individual
frame samples stalled for 20–154 ms. The candidate's aggregate median was
8.292 ms against a 4.257 ms strict baseline, tripping only
`TIMING_REGRESSION_GT_15PCT`. This does not overturn the alternating
unit-specific result: sixteen one-thread pairs improved wall/child CPU by
`-2.144%`/`-1.857%`. Re-run the product-policy timing gate in a pinned clean
host window before enabling the slack split policy.

The matched five-encode post-change profile is
`/tmp/rvp9-unit139-profile-final.json.gz`. It attributes 930 inclusive
samples to `evaluate_candidate_with_filter_set`, now including the two luma
model ranks, and 466 to the single winning-filter reconstruction path. The
adjacent Unit 138 binary, profiled under the same host load, retained 770 and
410 samples respectively but required roughly four times as much wall time;
sample counts are not a call-count oracle because the stream and scheduling
changed. The repeated alternating child-CPU result and the official quality
curve remain the acceptance evidence.

Retain commit `d538603c0d52520e29ad8b104737ee6c20408a59`. It corrects a
libvpx behavioral mismatch, materially improves both quality and one-thread
speed, and closes the Stage 4a cpu-used 7 intermediate quality gate. The next

until external saturation clears.

## Unit 140 — select retained filter-winner prediction and model

The Unit 139 source correction exposed one remaining duplicate step at its
new boundary. `select_interp_filter_by_luma_model` builds both filter
predictions and computes both luma models, but returns only the winning enum.
`evaluate_candidate_for_filter` immediately rebuilds the winning prediction
and recomputes the same luma model before its single Hadamard/UV evaluation.
The committed profile attributes 201 inclusive samples to the shared inter
predictor and 131 to `luma_model_rd`.

Pinned libvpx's `search_filter_ref` retains the winning `PRED_BUFFER`,
`pf_rate`, `pf_dist`, `pf_var`, `pf_sse`, and `pf_tx_size`; its caller passes
`rd_computed=1`, so neither the predictor nor the model is repeated. Rvp9 can
mirror that ownership without unsafe code: keep a second causal thread-local
prediction scratch, copy only the active winning prefix when the model argmin
changes, and pass the owned `ModelRdResult` into the complete evaluator.
The existing direct single-filter entry point remains unchanged.

The exact next experiment is this reuse only. Extend the focused filter oracle
to compare the retained prediction and model against an independent rebuild,
then require the standard product stream and three decoder outputs to match
Unit 139 exactly. Keep the change only if repeated child CPU improves; reject
it if the extra active-prefix copy costs as much as the avoided convolution
and variance-model pass.

The filter search now owns two prediction arrays in one thread-local scratch

alternate; the model argmin returns its buffer slot plus the already-computed
`ModelRdResult`. The complete candidate evaluation consumes that exact
prediction/model pair. Single-filter calls keep the original direct path.

Two intermediate layouts did not meet the keep rule. Copying the active
winner prefix into a second TLS array produced paired child-CPU results of

wins. Twenty four-thread pairs were neutral at `-0.006%` child CPU with 10/20
wins; their `+0.879%` wall result is rejected because the host remained above

compares every active pixel and every luma-model field, and proves neither

`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`,
716,330 bytes. Rvp9-decoder, pinned vpxdec, and ffmpeg each produce the same

## Unit 141 — select persistent tile workers

Matched `samply` runs now compare the committed Unit 140 encoder with pinned
libvpx v1.14.1 under the same 120-frame 1280x704 cpu-used 7, realtime VBR,
lag-zero, AQ-off, four-thread configuration. The libvpx profile is
`/tmp/libvpx-cpu7-profile-current.json.gz`; its IVF SHA-256 is
`fd4bc6b42b8618dcf4588424d46cc3d2370765dbfe63b39a3b44a34da9aedb68`.

The profiles reject the initial slow-kernel hypothesis. Across five Unit 140
processes, rvp9 creates 478–524 sampled threads per encode. Only the four
named loop-filter workers are persistent; the inter picker and final tile
writer each use a fresh `std::thread::scope` on every inter frame. The same
libvpx command creates exactly four threads per process. Rvp9 spends 2,920
binary self samples in short-lived tile workers, 1,452 on the main thread,
and 282 in the persistent loop-filter pool. Pinned libvpx records 5,236
binary self samples across ten processes, while keeping its tile workers
alive for the stream. The active work is therefore much closer than the
loaded-host wall-time ratio suggests; repeated thread construction and
per-frame join machinery are an architectural speed difference.

The exact next experiment is to replace both per-frame scoped-thread sites
with one stream-owned `WorkerPool`, reusing it sequentially for inter tile
picking, loop filtering, and final tile writing. Keep the configured
four-thread/tile cap, deterministic tile-order merge, panic propagation,
single-thread path, and byte output unchanged. Add a focused lifetime test
which proves the same worker thread IDs serve multiple scopes. Keep the unit
only if the standard stream and all three decoder outputs are byte-exact and
repeated four-thread child CPU or wall time improves without shifting work to
an unmeasured background thread.

## Unit 141 — accept persistent frame workers

`StreamEncoder` now creates one long-lived worker pool, capped by the
configured thread and tile-column counts. Inter tile picking, split-edge loop
filtering, and final tile arithmetic writing borrow it sequentially. Per-tile
result slots preserve submission-independent tile-order merge and return
writer errors on the caller thread. The one-thread and explicit serial-test
paths still execute inline.

The focused worker-pool test records `ThreadId` across two independent scopes
and proves the same non-caller OS worker serves both. A fresh five-encode
profile at `/tmp/rvp9-unit141-profile-v1.json.gz` contains exactly five
threads per process: the main thread plus four named workers. Unit 140
contained 478–524 sampled threads per process. The candidate removes roughly
four short-lived worker creations per inter frame without adding unsafe code.

Three alternating four-thread series on the still-saturated host report
paired wall changes of `-0.452%`, `-3.019%`, and `-1.555%`. The third series
also measured both child user and system CPU: total child CPU improves
`-3.094%` with 17/20 wins, and system CPU improves `-18.947%` with 19/20
wins. This is reproducible work elimination despite the load-average 22
noise; the clean-host corpus matrix remains the acceptance timing authority.

The 120-frame product stream remains byte-identical to Unit 140 with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.
Rvp9-decoder, pinned vpxdec, and ffmpeg each emit the same 162,201,600-byte
I420 SHA-256
`c938181f215f71b246a8b10bf2ba9477c6f6e04b49c0c0ab6917aa692e79102e`.
The strict Stage 4a quality curve is therefore unchanged at
`-8.585847331540%` BD-rate.

## Unit 144 — select dispatched source-SAD and variance kernels

The all-superblock source-SAD pass is still one of the largest mapped
main-thread costs. `pre_analysis.rs::sb_sad_and_var` walks every pixel in
scalar Rust to compute SAD, sum, and SSE. Its own header says SIMD should land
once the encoder hot path consumes it. That condition is now measured.

Rvp9 already has the required byte-exact dispatch slots:
`SimdDispatch::sad(Block64x64)` selects NEON/AVX2/scalar SAD, and
`SimdDispatch::variance(Block64x64)` selects an integer-exact `(sum, sse)`
reduction. Pinned libvpx uses the corresponding `cpi->fn_ptr[bsize].sdf` and
`.vf` kernels in `vp9_avg_source_sad`.

The exact next experiment is to resolve both Block64x64 function pointers
once per source-SAD frame and replace only the scalar reduction inside the
existing superblock loop. Preserve the scalar helper as the independent test
oracle. Add random and extreme-block differential coverage for SAD, signed
sum, SSE, content-state, and cached scene outputs. Require identical product
bytes and decoder output plus repeated speed improvement.

`compute_avg_source_sad` now resolves the existing Block64x64 SAD and

A focused oracle compares scalar and dispatched SAD, signed-sum magnitude,
and SSE over random data, maximum-difference blocks, alternating zero-sum
