## Unit 145 — select current-field exact VBP architecture screen

A twenty-pair matched run on the current Unit 144 binary measures rvp9 at
`2.06965x` pinned vpxenc total child CPU and `2.67738x` wall time on the
standard 1280x704, 120-frame, cpu-used 7, realtime VBR, AQ-off, four-thread
command. Pair wins are 0/20 in every timing category, so host contention
cannot explain the remaining order-one gap.

The Unit 144 address map rejects another blind coefficient-kernel edit.
Most `reconstruct_via_round_trip` samples land in the already-fused AArch64
NEON Hadamard quantize-metrics body. Prior units removed its duplicate
prediction/model/filter work, pixel SSE scan, scratch over-sizing,
coefficient widening, and dequant instruction pair.

The architectural mismatch is now the higher-leverage hypothesis. Pinned
vpxenc spends 40.03% inclusive in `nonrd_use_partition`: it first builds the
cpu-used 7 variance tree, then runs `nonrd_pick_sb_modes` on the chosen
leaves. Rvp9 spends 59.73% inclusive in `nonrd_partition::descend`, where
the complete picker recursively helps choose the tree before the final leaf
pass. The repository already contains an opt-in pinned-source-exact VBP tree
and copy-history adapter. Unit 63 found that adapter behind the then-current
quality field, but the picker and strict curve have since changed
materially: the accepted baseline moved from a positive deficit to
`-8.585847331540%` BD-rate after the libvpx-exact two-stage filter search.

The exact next experiment is configuration-only. On the current committed
binary, compare default partitioning with `RVP9_EXACT_VBP_TREE=1` using
alternating child CPU/wall measurements, the standard matched-bitrate
diagnostic, and fresh fixed-Q q80/q120/q160 points. If the adapter does not
remove an order-one share of CPU or gives back the current quality margin,
reject it without a source change. If it is Pareto-promising, run the strict
seven-point Stage 4a curve before making the adapter default.

## Unit 145 — reject current-field exact VBP architecture

The configuration-only screen rejects the exact VBP adapter before a quality
run. Across twenty alternating four-thread pairs it regresses total child CPU
by `+50.928%` and wall time by `+41.153%`, with 0/20 wins in both measures.
User CPU regresses `+53.669%`; system CPU regresses `+19.629%`.

The result explains why deleting recursive descent is not sufficient. The
pinned VBP tree selects substantially finer leaves on this large-frame
standard clip; the final picker and emitter then process enough additional
leaves to cost more than the removed tree search. Unit 63's fixed-Q evidence
already showed the same tree spending more bits for slightly lower quality.
No source changed, and a strict curve cannot rescue a candidate which fails
the speed premise by an order-one margin.

## Unit 146 — select tile-local reconstruction seeding

The retained four-thread architecture exposes a separate byte-exact
duplication. Each of four persistent tile jobs allocates full-frame Y/U/V
reconstruction buffers. `pick_and_simulate_inter_encode_partitions` then
copies the complete LAST Y/U/V frame into every job even though an active
tile job reads and returns only its own full-height column range. The caller
merges exactly those column ranges and discards all pixels outside them.
Pinned libvpx's tile workers operate inside their tile bounds.

The Unit 144 profile records 91 self samples in platform `memmove`, while
each inter frame currently seeds four full reconstructions. The exact next
experiment is to move tile-bound resolution before reconstruction seeding
and copy only the active tile's Y and subsampled U/V row spans when
`active_tile_col` is set. Preserve the current full-frame copy for the serial
path. Add a focused sentinel oracle proving full and tile-local bounds,
require byte-identical product and decoder output, then keep only on repeated
child-CPU or wall improvement.

## Unit 146 — reject tile-local reconstruction seeding

The tile-local copy is correct but does not produce a repeatable speed gain.
The focused sentinel oracle passed, and the release stream stayed
byte-identical with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.

Two twenty-pair four-thread series disagree within noise. The first changes
paired wall time by `-0.277%` with 11/20 wins and total child CPU by `+0.574%`
with 6/20 wins. The second changes wall time by `+0.343%` with 7/20 wins and
total child CPU by `+0.289%` with 8/20 wins. Platform `memmove` handles the
contiguous row spans efficiently enough that narrowing them does not repay
the extra per-row control work.

No source change is retained. The next architecture experiment should fix
the measured thread-budget mismatch. With `--threads=4`, rvp9 currently owns
the caller plus four persistent workers, while pinned vpxenc owns four
threads total. Change the pool to three persistent workers and make the
caller execute one scoped job, preserving four active execution lanes and
deterministic result ordering. Require a focused caller-participation oracle,
byte-identical output, and repeated paired speed evidence before retaining
the change.

## Unit 147 — accept caller-participating encoder workers

The encoder now treats `--threads=N` as a total execution budget. Its
stream-owned pool uses `N - 1` persistent OS workers and reserves tile zero
for the scope caller. Existing decoder pools retain their prior
worker-only semantics through the unchanged `WorkerPool::new` constructor.
The separate `WorkerPool::new_with_caller` API keeps the architecture choice
explicit.

The focused core oracle proves that a four-thread budget owns three workers,
executes exactly one of four jobs on the caller, and keeps tile zero on that
caller. A panic-path oracle proves the reserved job still completes before a
scope-closure panic propagates, preserving the lifetime-erasure safety
contract. The adjacent safety document records the new unconditional-join
edge.

Scheduling the final tile on the caller was rejected: two twenty-pair series
changed total child CPU by `-0.840%` and `-0.130%`, but regressed wall time by
`+0.339%` and `+1.303%`. Keeping tile zero on the caller matches libvpx's
main-worker lane and produces a repeatable Pareto improvement. Two fresh
twenty-pair series improve paired wall time by `-1.377%` and `-1.010%`, with
20/20 and 16/20 wins. Total child CPU improves by `-1.442%` and `-1.107%`,
with 18/20 and 16/20 wins. System CPU improves by `-11.338%` and `-11.184%`,
with 20/20 wins in both series.

A macOS `ps -M` sample reports six rows for Unit 144, five for Unit 147, and
five for pinned vpxenc; the extra process row means Unit 147 and vpxenc each
use four execution threads while Unit 144 used five. The product stream
remains byte-identical with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.
Rvp9-decoder, pinned vpxdec, and ffmpeg each emit the same 162,201,600-byte
I420 SHA-256
`c938181f215f71b246a8b10bf2ba9477c6f6e04b49c0c0ab6917aa692e79102e`.
The strict Stage 4a curve is therefore unchanged at `-8.585847331540%`
BD-rate.

All 1,579 applicable core tests pass after filtering the two absent captured

## Unit 154 — reject compact Hadamard metric accumulators

The Unit 153 five-encode profile is
`/tmp/rvp9-unit153-profile-v1.json.gz`; its analysis is

product work is the fused Hadamard quantizer inside

vectors into one and four squared-error vectors into two. Horizontal totals
remain mathematically identical, and expanded all-minimum/all-maximum
coefficient differential cases passed for 8x8, 16x16, and 32x32 transforms.
The product stream also remained byte-identical.

The smaller live-vector set does not help this Apple Silicon compiler
schedule. Two twenty-pair series regress wall time by `+0.292%` and
`+0.442%`, with 9/20 wins in each. Total child CPU changes by `+0.312%` and

The next measured hypothesis remains in the same dominant function, but
must remove work rather than reshuffle accumulators. In the normal Stage 4a
product mode, coefficient-domain distortion is computed for every candidate
even though selection uses pixel-domain distortion; only the opt-in S3
domain and F.0.2 diagnostics consume `coef_dist`. Add an explicit
rate-only Hadamard metrics path for normal product selection while preserving
the full metric path whenever S3 or candidate tracing is enabled. Require
focused full-vs-rate metric equivalence for EOB/SATD, exact product bytes,
and repeated speed evidence.

## Unit 155 — accept rate-only Hadamard metrics

Normal Stage 4a selection now uses dedicated NEON Hadamard quantizers that
return only EOB and coefficient SATD. They omit dequantization and four
widened squared-error accumulations per eight coefficients. The full
coefficient-distortion path remains active whenever the opt-in S3 domain or
F.0.2 candidate tracing needs `coef_dist`; intra fallback also keeps full
metrics. The non-AArch64 path preserves the same visible rate-only contract.

The focused differential compares rate-only EOB/SATD against the full
quantizer for 8x8, 16x16, and 32x32 transforms, six q-indices, randomized
coefficients, zeros, DC-only and final-scan nonzeros, and all-`i16::MIN` /
all-`i16::MAX` saturation cases. The round-trip integration oracle proves
identical coefficient rate, skippability, and DCT EOB while rate-only mode
returns the documented zero coefficient distortion. Candidate tracing still
uses full metrics.

prebuilt grid to the packet builder, covering the revised contract.

Three twenty-pair series improve total child CPU by `-0.360%`, `-0.716%`,
and `-0.656%`, with 15/20, 14/20, and 16/20 wins. Paired wall time improves
by `-1.108%`, `-1.227%`, and `-1.356%`, with 16/20, 14/20, and 17/20 wins.
The second series ran under elevated host system load, but all three paired
CPU and wall series independently favor the candidate.

The product stream remains byte-identical with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`,
so the strict Stage 4a curve remains `-8.585847331540%` BD-rate. All 521
active encoder library tests pass with one ignored. Encoder all-target
clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and diff checks pass. Retain the
shared canonical grid.

The accepted-head profile is `/tmp/rvp9-unit158-profile-v1.json.gz`; its
analysis is `/tmp/rvp9-unit158-profile-analysis.txt`. Do not spend the next
unit on the analogous keyframe grid duplication: it occurs once per
120-frame Stage 4a sequence and cannot close the remaining speed ratio.
Profile-guided work should return to the per-candidate round-trip,
prediction/filter evaluation, or the record/replay coefficient pipeline.

## Unit 159 — reject separate chroma prediction TLS

Each candidate currently declares two 32x32 chroma prediction arrays before
motion compensation overwrites their active rectangles. Replacing them with
a second per-worker thread-local scratch preserved exact output and extended
the existing narrow-after-wide candidate oracle to exercise real U/V model
inputs. It did not improve speed: three twenty-pair series change total
child CPU by `+0.869%`, `-0.424%`, and `-0.007%`, while wall time changes by
`+1.119%`, `-0.372%`, and `+0.576%`. Two of three wall series regress and
total CPU has no repeatable direction. The compiler likely removes most
dead initialization, leaving the extra TLS lookup as comparable or greater
work. No source or test change is retained.

Do not add another independent TLS lookup for candidate scratch. The next
exact memory-lifetime experiment may extend the already-borrowed luma
candidate scratch with the 32x32 Hadamard residual slab and pass it down
explicitly. That targets the dominant round-trip without another thread-
local access; require narrow-after-wide exactness and repeated CPU evidence.

## Unit 160 — accept reusable candidate residual scratch

The existing per-worker luma prediction scratch now also owns the 32x32
signed residual slab used by the Hadamard/DCT round-trip. Candidate
evaluation passes both disjoint buffers down through a typed
`RoundTripBuffers` bundle. Each subtract kernel overwrites the complete
transform-sized prefix before any transform reads it, so stale suffix data
is unreachable. Intra fallback retains a local residual because it does not
hold the candidate scratch. The narrow-after-wide candidate oracle and the
8x8/16x16/32x32 repeated round-trip oracle cover reuse across block and
transform sizes.

Five twenty-pair series (four before the argument bundle needed by clippy,
one after the final source shape) change total child CPU by `-1.050%`,
`-0.772%`, `+0.130%`, `-1.135%`, and `-0.291%`. Paired wall time changes by
`-1.608%`, `-0.487%`, `-0.151%`, `-1.191%`, and `+0.353%`. Four of five CPU
and wall series favor the candidate; each reversal is below the measurement
floor. The candidate removes a real per-evaluation 2 KiB initialization
without another TLS lookup, unlike rejected Unit 159.

The product stream remains byte-identical with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`,
so the strict Stage 4a curve remains `-8.585847331540%` BD-rate. All 1,581
applicable core tests pass after filtering the two absent captured fixtures.
Core all-target clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and diff checks
pass. Retain the residual scratch.

The accepted-head profile is `/tmp/rvp9-unit160-profile-v1.json.gz`; its
analysis is `/tmp/rvp9-unit160-profile-analysis.txt`. The `std::env::_var`
samples are one-time process initialization (four cached policy reads across
five profiled processes), not a recurring candidate bottleneck. Continue
from the round-trip's Hadamard/quantize body or the coefficient
record/replay pipeline; do not optimize those one-time environment reads.

## Unit 161 — reject reusable Hadamard coefficient scratch

Extending the existing per-worker candidate scratch with a second 32x32
signed slab let the Hadamard transforms overwrite reusable coefficient
storage instead of declaring size-specific stack arrays. The narrow-after-wide
round-trip oracle poisoned the slab between calls and still produced identical
metrics for Tx8x8, Tx16x16, and Tx32x32. The product stream also remained
byte-identical with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.

Four twenty-pair series changed total child CPU by `+1.127%`, `+0.167%`,
`-0.278%`, and `+0.994%`. Paired wall time changed by `+0.864%`, `-0.224%`,
`-1.486%`, and `+0.263%`. The host carried unrelated load, but the paired
direction is not a reproducible improvement: two CPU series regress by about
one percent and only one favors the candidate below the measurement floor.
The compiler likely already removes most dead initialization around the
fully-overwriting transform, while the larger thread-local object increases
cache pressure. No source or test change is retained.

Do not move the Hadamard output into another persistent slab without removing
other live storage. Continue with a measured operation reduction in the
coefficient replay or candidate evaluation path.

## Unit 162 — reject retained-coefficient replay rewrites

A temporary output-only diagnostic measured 10,572 replayed transform blocks
over the first 20 product frames. EOB-only blocks account for 4,371
(`41.35%`), while the average block carries 106.53 tokens and the maximum is
1,024. This justified testing an explicit one-token EOB fast path, then a
single-pass state machine that removes the generic loop's first-token reload.
Both variants preserved the canonical product stream SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`
and passed direct-versus-replay focused oracles.

The EOB-only path changed total child CPU across four twenty-pair series by
`-1.506%`, `-0.079%`, `+1.016%`, and `-0.077%`. Its sampled replay share was
unchanged at `4.11%` of binary self samples (167/4,065 versus control
172/4,183). The single-pass rewrite changed total child CPU by `-1.376%` and
`+1.542%`; paired wall time moved `-0.964%` and `+1.361%`. More importantly,
its immediately paired profile raised replay self samples to 207/4,029
(`5.14%`). The per-token run-state branch costs more than the original nested
zero-run loop saves.

No source, test, or diagnostic printing is retained. Do not rewrite the replay
state machine again without a new operation-level hypothesis. The remaining
replay cost is dominated by required boolean coding and non-zero value-tree
emission rather than the outer control shape.

## Unit 163 — reject coarse partition shortcuts and reset the speed target

An exact matched-command diagnostic compared the accepted Unit 160 binary
against pinned libvpx v1.14.1 `vpxenc` on the 120-frame 1280x704 Stage 4a
input. Across twelve warmed paired runs, rvp9 used `1.972x` vpxenc's median
total child CPU and `2.469x` its median wall time. The shared host was
saturated, so this is not acceptance evidence, but it agrees with Unit 145's
independent `2.06965x` child-CPU result. The remaining gap is architectural;
sub-percent scratch and control-flow changes cannot close it.

A five-encode sample attributes rvp9's inter-frame work to the mode-aware
partition descent and selected-leaf pipeline:

- descent: `276.82 ms` of `544.92 ms` summed tile-job phase time;
- selected-leaf coefficient emission: `187.68 ms`;
- other tile work: `80.32 ms`;
- selected leaves: `60,664`.

Three coarse-tree screens failed:

- restoring the exact variance-based partition tree raised total child CPU by
  `72.49%` and wall time by `53.94%`;
- disabling rectangular partitions saved only about `0.72%` child CPU while
  preserving the standard stream;
- forcing a 64x64 minimum more than halved the leaf count
  (`60,664 -> 25,960`) but changed child CPU by only `-0.049%` and regressed
  wall time by `+0.942%`, because each remaining 64x64 candidate and emitted
  leaf became proportionally more expensive.

No source change is retained. Do not pursue leaf-count reduction alone:
rvp9's cost scales strongly with block area. The next experiment must remove
work per processed pixel from the picker or final leaf path. The libvpx-style
color-sensitivity UV gate is also not a new lever: Units 32, 54, 58, and 69
already rejected both approximate and source-faithful wiring on the fixed-Q
quality frontier.

## Unit 164 — accept the AArch64 horizontal i8mm tier

The existing cross-codec DSP benchmark claimed that rvp9 interpolation was
`8.16x..24.27x` slower than pinned libvpx. That result was invalid on
AArch64: despite its dispatch-table documentation, the rvp9 benchmark selected
the scalar implementation on every non-x86 host. The benchmark now calls the
production `SimdDispatch` slots on every architecture. All eight convolve
cases retain matching input and output hashes against the pinned C harness.

The corrected audit found a much narrower gap. The existing signed-dot
horizontal kernel was already close, while the reload-based vertical kernel
was about `1.6x` slower. The retained horizontal tier mirrors libvpx's i8mm
arithmetic: it processes four rows together with unsigned-by-signed dot
products, keeps the arbitrary-height tail, and falls back to the signed-dot
or widened-i16 tiers according to runtime capabilities and filter range.

The i8mm horizontal microkernel matches pinned libvpx:

- 16x16: `19.08 ns` versus `19.28 ns`;
- 64x64: `303.12 ns` versus `304.62 ns`.

Three twenty-pair four-thread product trials improve total child CPU by
`-0.455%`, `-0.610%`, and `-0.822%`; user CPU favors the candidate in
15/20, 12/20, and 16/20 pairs. Paired wall changes are `-0.438%`,
`-0.909%`, and `-0.494%`. The standard stream remains byte-identical with
SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.

The exhaustive single-axis and representative separable differentials pass
for every VP9 filter family and supported block shape. Core/benchmark
all-target clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and diff checks pass. The
benchmark also surfaced pre-existing AArch64 inverse-transform hash
differences outside this unit; do not treat the whole decoder-kernel audit as
green until those are diagnosed separately.

Retain the i8mm horizontal tier. The next measured interpolation unit is a
rolling-window vertical i8mm kernel: the corrected audit reports 16x16 at
`32.52 ns` versus libvpx's `20.18 ns`, and 64x64 at `493.35 ns` versus
`300.10 ns`.

## Unit 165 — accept rolling vertical i8mm as a kernel-parity prerequisite

The vertical i8mm tier now mirrors libvpx's rolling four-output structure. It
keeps seven transposed rows live, loads four new rows, forms the three
overlapping windows with table lookups, and advances the state. The older
signed-dot tier retains the smaller reload-based implementation; the widened
i16 fallback still covers the full-pel coefficient `128`.

The corrected cross-codec microbenchmark closes almost all of the measured
vertical gap:

- 16x16: `32.52 -> 21.19 ns`, versus libvpx `20.18 ns`;
- 64x64: `493.35 -> 311.68 ns`, versus libvpx `300.10 ns`.

All eight interpolation audit hashes continue to match pinned libvpx. The
standard 120-frame cpu-used 7 stream remains exactly 716,330 bytes with
SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.

The host remained unsuitable for an acceptance wall benchmark: load average
was above 20, with unrelated jobs using several cores. Three twenty-pair
four-thread comparisons changed total child CPU by `-0.680%`, `+0.140%`, and
`-0.077%`; a one-thread comparison changed it by `+0.047%`. Those results are
neutral within noise. The direct kernel improvement is reproducible and
removes a known libvpx parity gap, so retain this exact-output unit as a
measured prerequisite. Recheck its product contribution in the clean-host
corpus matrix rather than treating the contaminated wall readings as final
speed evidence.

The focused scalar differentials pass all three `convolve8_neon` tests.
Core/benchmark all-target clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and diff
checks also pass.

## Unit 166 — widen the 32x32 DCT at the audited overflow boundary

The fresh Unit 165 profile selected the full-precision 32x32 forward DCT:
`fdct32x32` held 217 self samples, or `4.80%` of resolved rvp9 self work.
The cross-codec DSP audit did not cover forward transforms, so both harness
sides now include a deterministic valid-residual 32x32 case. Input and output
hashes match pinned libvpx:

- input FNV-1a: `7ff2a73daf767cfc`;
- output FNV-1a: `e3d9e87fbeeef2a4`.

The baseline measured rvp9 at `747.10 ns` and pinned libvpx at `501.31 ns`.
Inspection confirmed the remaining structural difference recorded after
Unit 126: rvp9 widened all eight second-pass lanes before stage 1, while
libvpx keeps eight i16 lanes through stages 1 and 2 and widens at stage 3,
where its extreme-input audit first found overflow.

The retained implementation evaluates those first two stages once in eight
i16 lanes, then widens the low and high halves into the existing audited i32
schedule from stage 3 onward. The focused randomized, constant, alternating,
and stride-jitter scalar differential remains green. Repeated cross-codec
measurements put the new kernel at `717.04..725.94 ns`, a `2.83..4.02%`
reduction.

Despite high host load, repeated child-CPU product evidence is consistently
positive:

- one thread, twenty pairs: `-0.934%`, 15/20 wins;
- one thread, twenty pairs: `-0.250%`, 12/20 wins;
- four threads, twenty pairs: `-0.500%`, 14/20 wins.

A fourth one-thread trial ran after load worsened enough to stretch the
control median from about `0.74 s` to `0.87 s`; it was neutral at `+0.009%`
total child CPU. Treat the direct kernel result as the reproducible evidence
and rerun product timing on the clean pinned host.

The standard 120-frame cpu-used 7 stream remains exactly 716,330 bytes with
SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.
Retain the unit. The remaining 32x32 transform gap is in the later-stage
schedule and transpose/storage layout; profile again before changing it.

All 40 forward-transform tests pass. Core/benchmark all-target clippy with
warnings denied, the C harness build with warnings treated as errors,
formatting, and diff checks also pass.

## Unit 167 — reject pre-search NEWMV thresholding

Frame-phase instrumentation measured `209,829` fresh NEWMV searches across
the 120-frame standard run, versus 60,664 selected leaves. A selected-mode
diagnostic was dominated by ZEROMV, with NEWMV only about 2% of reported
winners. The tested hypothesis applied the existing adaptive RD threshold to
NEWMV before its diamond/sub-pixel search, instead of after the resulting MV
was known.

The candidate preserved the exact 716,330-byte standard stream and SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.
It did not remove enough work: fresh searches fell only `209,829 -> 203,788`
(`-2.88%`). One twenty-pair one-thread comparison was neutral at `-0.054%`
total child CPU with 10/20 wins; user CPU was `+0.061%`.

The source change is fully removed. Do not broaden the threshold merely to
make it fire more often; that changes search coverage without evidence that
the skipped NEWMV candidates preserve the quality curve. The next measured
kernel hypothesis is the 16x16 forward DCT's second pass: pinned libvpx keeps
all eight low-bit-depth lanes in i16, while rvp9 still widens them into two
four-lane groups.

## Unit 168 — accept bounded eight-lane 16x16 forward DCT

The Unit 166 profile attributed 99 resolved self samples (`2.26%`) to the
16x16 forward DCT. A new cross-codec audit case uses deterministic valid
residuals and proves matching input/output FNV-1a hashes:

- input: `4f6ad662e804b7aa`;
- output: `ed878a7063a67e69`.

The starting rvp9 kernel measured `127.17 ns`, versus pinned libvpx at
`92.48..95.64 ns`. A literal all-i16 second pass was not correct for rvp9's
existing oracle: constant residual `-255` produced DC `13701` instead of
scalar `-32639`. That candidate was rejected immediately.

The retained hybrid uses a coefficient-bound audit: no intermediate in the
16-point schedule exceeds 16 times its input. A row group whose second-pass
inputs are at most 2047 therefore stays in eight i16 lanes throughout
(`16 * 2047 = 32752`). Larger groups perform only the bounded cross-input
stage in eight lanes, then widen into the established i32 remainder. The
final kernel measures `84.95 ns`, faster than the pinned reference on the
same harness, with identical hashes.

The standard cpu-used 7 stream remains exactly 716,330 bytes with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.
Two twenty-pair four-thread runs improved total child CPU by `-0.720%` and
`-1.029%`; paired wall time improved `-1.021%` and `-0.985%`. Direct
one-thread trials changed child CPU by `-0.554%`, `+0.614%`, and `-1.029%`
under load averages above 18. The intermediate 1023-bound build independently
improved twice, and the 2047 build beat it by another `-0.908%`, so the
kernel and product evidence support retention despite the noisy outlier.

Run the complete forward-transform tests, core/benchmark all-target clippy,
the C harness warning gate, formatting, and diff checks before committing.
Then take a fresh profile; do not optimize another transform from the stale
Unit 166 sample.

The green gate completed: all 40 forward-transform tests pass; core and

bytes with I420 SHA-256
`c938181f215f71b246a8b10bf2ba9477c6f6e04b49c0c0ab6917aa692e79102e`.

The next isolated arithmetic difference was libvpx's two-coefficient

- four threads, twenty pairs: `-0.152%`, 13/20 wins;
- four threads, repeat: `-0.885%`, 12/20 wins.

The standard cpu-used 7 stream remains exactly 716,330 bytes with SHA-256

The fresh Unit 171 profile is `/tmp/rvp9-unit171-profile-v1.json.gz`; its

## Unit 173 — accept pairwise Hadamard SATD accumulation

The normal product picker uses the fused rate-only Hadamard quantizer on
every candidate. Its SATD accumulator widened the low and high qcoeff halves
to i32 separately, took two absolute values, and maintained two u32 vectors.
NEON can take the exact absolute i16 lanes and pairwise-widen-add them into
one u32 accumulator. Unsigned reinterpretation preserves `abs(i16::MIN) =
32768`, and the 1,024-coefficient maximum sum fits u32.

The existing narrowed-versus-widened differential covers all realtime
transform sizes, six qindices, random coefficients, signed extremes,
all-zero, DC-only, and final-scan blocks. It passes unchanged. The complete
seven-test AArch64 quantizer module, core all-target clippy, formatting, and
diff checks pass. The standard stream remains exactly 716,330 bytes with
SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.

Four independent twenty-pair comparisons favor the candidate:

- one thread: `-0.162%` and `-0.215%` total child CPU;
- four threads: `-0.292%` and `-0.163%` total child CPU.

The gain is small but reproducible, exact, and directly removes SIMD work in
the fresh profile's largest leaf. Retain it. Take a new accepted-head profile
before changing another round-trip operation.

## Unit 174 — reject two-way rate-quantizer unrolling

The accepted Unit 173 profile is `/tmp/rvp9-unit173-profile-v1.json.gz`;
its analysis is `/tmp/rvp9-unit173-profile-analysis.txt`. The round-trip
remains the largest resolved leaf at 353 self samples (`9.61%`), and most of
its sampled instruction cluster maps to the inlined rate-only Hadamard
quantizer.

Unrolling that quantizer's eight-lane loop two ways preserved the extreme
coefficient differential and the exact standard stream. It did not produce
a stable product win. Three twenty-pair one-thread trials changed total
child CPU by `-0.258%`, `-0.059%`, and `-0.099%`; three four-thread trials
changed it by `-0.075%`, `-0.075%`, and `+0.327%`. The final regression had
only 5/20 wins. The accumulator dependency limits scheduling freedom, while
the duplicate loop body increases code size.

The source candidate is fully removed. Do not retry control-flow unrolling
without a new direct-kernel benchmark showing an architecture-specific win.
Continue with a distinct measured leaf rather than further reshaping this
quantizer loop.

## Unit 175 — accept fused Hadamard source subtraction

The accepted Unit 173 profile leaves the Hadamard-only round-trip as the
largest resolved rvp9 leaf. Its product path first wrote each source-minus-
prediction residual into a signed slab, then immediately reread the slab in
the NEON Hadamard transform. The new AArch64 8x8 kernel widens and subtracts
the source and prediction bytes in its first load. The 16x16 and 32x32
kernels compose the same exact 8x8 primitive and keep libvpx's established
combination arithmetic. The compatibility path still stages the residual
because its DCT reconstruction consumes it after the Hadamard metrics.

A focused differential covers 8x8, 16x16, and 32x32 transforms, unequal
source/prediction strides, and both signed byte-difference extremes. It
matches the existing staged transform coefficient for coefficient. All 12
Hadamard tests, both focused Hadamard-only picker tests, core all-target
clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and diff checks pass.

Two twenty-pair comparisons against committed Unit 173 favor the candidate:

- one thread: `-0.572%` total child CPU, 14/20 wins, `-0.427%` wall;
- four threads: `-0.331%` total child CPU, 14/20 wins, `-0.077%` wall.

The standard cpu-used 7 stream remains exactly 716,330 bytes with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.
Retain the fusion. Take a fresh profile from the committed candidate and
choose the next operation-level leaf; do not further reshape the Hadamard
quantizer loop without a direct-kernel cause.

The committed Unit 175 profile is `/tmp/rvp9-unit175-profile-v1.json.gz`,

## Unit 177 — accept AArch64 dot-product variance dispatch

Pinned libvpx selects `variance_neon_dotprod.c` on the Apple M5 Max, while
rvp9's process-wide AArch64 capability table exposed only base NEON. The new
`dotprod` capability is detected once and pre-resolves every 8/16/32/64-wide
variance slot plus the dedicated 8x8-cell slot. The kernel follows libvpx's
three-accumulator structure: source bytes, prediction bytes, and squared
absolute differences each use one unsigned dot product per 16 pixels. Base
NEON remains the fallback and 4-wide blocks remain scalar.

The scalar, base-NEON, and dotprod dispatch tiers agree exactly. Random
differentials cover all 11 promoted shapes, stride jitter `0..16`, and
equality plus worst-case `0 <-> 255` inputs. All 1,581 applicable core tests
pass after filtering the two absent captured-vector fixtures and the
release-build form of a debug-only should-panic test; that should-panic test
passes separately in the debug profile. Core all-target clippy passes with
warnings denied, as do formatting and diff checks.

Two twenty-pair comparisons against Unit 175 strongly favor the candidate:

- one thread: `-2.198%` total child CPU and `-2.219%` wall, 20/20 wins;
- four threads: `-2.049%` total child CPU, 19/20 wins, and `-2.730%` wall,
  20/20 wins.

The standard cpu-used 7 stream remains exactly 716,330 bytes with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.
Retain the tier. Take a fresh committed profile and confirm that the 64x64
variance leaf collapses before selecting another exact kernel or duplicate
operation.

## Unit 179 — reject zero-offset sub-pixel dot-product reduction

The committed Unit 178 profile is `/tmp/rvp9-unit178-profile-v1.json.gz`,
with symbols in `/tmp/rvp9-unit178-profile-v1.json.syms.json` and analysis
in `/tmp/rvp9-unit178-profile-analysis.txt`. It contains 3,408 resolved rvp9
self samples. The vectorized loop-filter store reduced the vertical 16-tap
leaf from 119 samples to 89. Sub-pixel variance was the next independent
kernel at 128 samples.

Pinned libvpx routes the `(xoffset, yoffset) == (0, 0)` specialization to
ordinary variance. A candidate added pre-resolved dot-product-aware sub-pixel
slots and reused Unit 177's exact dot-product variance reduction for that
case. All eight supported shapes and every offset matched the scalar oracle;
the standard stream remained 716,330 bytes with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.

The product result did not clear the measurement floor. Twenty one-thread
pairs changed total child CPU by `-0.050%` with 10/20 wins and regressed wall
time by `+0.122%`. Twenty four-thread pairs changed child CPU by `-0.317%`
and wall by `-0.051%`, both with 12/20 wins. The complete source candidate is
removed. The likely reason is that zero/zero calls are too sparse to repay
the added specialization boundary, even though the isolated reduction is
faster.

The exact next experiment is to quantify a narrower operation-level target
before editing. The vertical 16-tap filter still has 89 self samples, but only
40 occur below its dual wrapper; a full 16-lane arithmetic duplicate has a
limited ceiling. Prefer an exact hot loop in `dec_find_mv_refs`, coefficient
encoding/replay, or another profile leaf unless static attribution reveals a
small way to widen only the dual loop-filter path.

## Unit 180 — reject cached reference-MV neighbour lookups

The hot `dec_find_mv_refs` monomorph has 85 of the profile's 88 self samples.
Its leading offsets map to the first eight-neighbour walk, and the sign-bias
fallback repeats the same tile/frame bounds and grid-index calculation for
each position. An exact candidate cached the eight immutable `ModeInfo`
references from the first walk and reused them in the fallback. A focused
counting oracle proved the different-reference path performed eight lookups
instead of sixteen, while all 54 reference-MV tests passed. The standard
stream remained byte-identical.

The added eight-reference stack array costs more across all calls than the
saved fallback lookups return. Twenty one-thread pairs regressed total child
CPU by `+0.145%` with 9/20 wins and wall by `+0.205%`. Twenty four-thread
pairs regressed child CPU by `+0.107%` with 10/20 wins and wall by `+0.374%`
with 8/20 wins. The source and focused cache test are fully removed. Do not
retry whole-walk caching without a representation that has zero setup cost
on the common early-return path.

The exact next experiment returns to a profile leaf with a narrower local
operation. Attribute `encode_coefs_impl` or another coefficient-path loop at
the instruction level; Unit 162 already rejected broad replay traversal
rewrites, so do not revisit the outer replay loop without a new operation-
level cause.

## Unit 181 — reject compile-time coefficient bit-emission specialization

Instruction attribution of `encode_coefs_impl` found that its hottest offsets

selected the recording specialization once per block. This removed repeated
tests from both the count-only recorder and direct writer, at the cost of a
second specialized coefficient loop. The two retained-token replay oracles
passed, and the standard cpu-used 7 stream remained exactly 716,330 bytes with
SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.

The isolated branch tests were not a useful product bottleneck. Twenty
one-thread pairs improved total child CPU by `-0.336%` with 13/20 wins and
wall by `-0.322%` with 12/20 wins. Twenty four-thread pairs regressed total
child CPU by `+0.202%` with 7/20 wins and wall by `+0.024%` with 10/20 wins.
The release binary also grew by 48 bytes. The complete source candidate is
removed. Do not retry coefficient bit-emission specialization without an
independent profile proving that the branch share increased materially.

The exact next experiment is instruction-level attribution of
`reconstruct_via_round_trip`, the largest independent self-time leaf at 332
of 3,408 resolved rvp9 samples. Identify one repeated copy, transform,
quantize, or reconstruction operation inside it before editing; earlier broad
coefficient-lifetime and generic reconstruction specialization attempts do

## Unit 182 — reject explicit Hadamard-to-quantizer register handoff

Temporary counters on the standard 120-frame cpu-used 7 workload found that
Tx8x8 accounts for more than 99% of round trips: the first 120,000 calls
comprised 119,514 Tx8x8, 486 Tx16x16, and zero Tx32x32 transforms. This made
the apparent 8x8 coefficient scratch boundary the only plausible structural
target inside the dominant round-trip leaf.

An exact candidate returned the eight final Hadamard vectors directly to new
full- and rate-metric quantizer entries. Focused transform and quantizer
oracles passed across mismatched strides, all six quantizers, random input,
zeros, signed extremes, DC-only, and final-scan-only coefficients. The
standard stream remained exactly 716,330 bytes with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.

Machine-code comparison invalidated the premise: LLVM had already scalar-
replaced the original coefficient array and fed live Hadamard registers into
the inlined quantizer. The explicit split instead duplicated the product and
compatibility paths, growing `reconstruct_via_round_trip` by 1,596 bytes.
Twenty one-thread pairs regressed total child CPU by `+0.840%` and wall by
`+0.923%`, both with only 4/20 wins. The counters, source candidate, and
focused tests are fully removed. Do not add a transform/quantizer register
API unless generated code first demonstrates an actual store/reload boundary.

The exact next experiment leaves the exhausted round-trip body. Attribute the
general fractional-offset work in `sub_pixel_variance_neon` at instruction
level and compare it with pinned libvpx's AArch64 kernels. Unit 179 rejected
only the sparse zero/zero dispatch; require a distinct repeated operation in
the horizontal, vertical, or two-dimensional filter before editing.

## Unit 183 — reject hoisted sub-pixel filter specialization

Temporary counters classified 455,000 sub-pixel variance calls by each
offset's zero / half-pixel / other class. The final counts in row-major
`(x-class, y-class)` order were
`[68074, 109848, 20339, 112800, 63863, 17481, 20235, 17877, 24483]`.
Thus 76.82% of calls use only zero and half-pixel filters, with the two
one-dimensional half-pixel cases alone accounting for 48.93%.

Pinned libvpx dispatches those cases outside its vector loops. An exact
candidate likewise hoisted the zero/half/general decision to row helpers and
gave half-pixel averaging a dedicated `vrhaddq_u8` loop. All three AArch64
sub-pixel differentials passed across every supported shape and offset,
varied strides, edge values, and dispatch. The standard stream remained
exactly 716,330 bytes with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.

Loop duplication outweighed the predictable inner branch. Twenty one-thread
pairs regressed total child CPU by `+0.286%` with 8/20 wins and wall by
`+0.225%` with 7/20 wins. The counters and source candidate are fully
removed. Do not retry filter-control specialization without isolated kernel
evidence that covers the final emitted machine text.

The exact next experiment stays in the 128-sample sub-pixel leaf but targets
its arithmetic reduction. Every filtered 16-byte vector currently uses two
widening differences, two pairwise sum accumulations, and four multiply-adds.
The accepted Unit 177 dot-product reduction computes the same `(sum, sse)`
with one absolute difference and three `udot` instructions on this host.
Add a pre-resolved dot-product sub-pixel tier for all offsets, not Unit 179's
sparse zero/zero shortcut, and require full offset/shape differential plus
paired product timing.

## Unit 184 — accept dot-product reduction for every sub-pixel offset

The accepted kernel adds a process-pre-resolved Armv8.2-A dot-product tier
for all eight 16/32/64-wide sub-pixel variance shapes. Filtering remains
unchanged. Each filtered 16-byte vector now uses one absolute difference and
three `udot` operations to accumulate prediction sum, reference sum, and
squared error. Hosts without dot-product support retain the established base
NEON tier, and 4/8-wide shapes remain on the scalar dispatch slots.

The focused all-offset/all-shape differential exercises 512 shape/offset
combinations for both SIMD tiers. Edge-value and forced scalar/base-NEON/

SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.

Two independent twenty-pair one-thread comparisons against committed Unit

with 13/20 wins. Three twenty-pair four-thread trials report total-child-CPU

`-0.114%`. The repeatable one-thread gain and neutral-to-favorable
four-thread result justify retaining the exact 32-byte binary increase.

All 1,582 applicable release core tests pass after filtering the two absent
captured-vector fixtures and the release form of a debug-only should-panic

## Unit 187 — accept all-nonflat loop-filter early exit

The Unit 185 profile grouped 179 self samples in the horizontal and vertical
16-tap loop-filter kernels. Unlike pinned libvpx, rvp9 always computed the
7-tap and 15-tap candidates before selecting per lane, even when every lane's
wide mask was zero. The retained change horizontally reduces the combined
mask once and returns the already-computed narrow result when no lane is
flat. Mixed lane groups retain the exact vector selections.

An initial candidate also reduced the 15-tap mask and skipped that arithmetic
when no lane was very flat. It improved one-thread child CPU by `-0.338%` and
`-0.167%`, but four-thread trials were neutral at `-0.012%` and regressed by
`+0.247%`. The second reduction is removed; once any flat lane exists, its
cost does not repay the narrower skip on this workload.

The retained single-exit candidate has four twenty-pair comparisons against
committed Unit 185:

- one thread: total child CPU `-0.165%` and `-0.096%`, both with 12/20 wins;
  paired wall `-0.213%` with 13/20 wins and `-0.426%` with 12/20 wins;
- four threads: total child CPU `-0.423%` with 13/20 wins and `+0.012%` with
  10/20 wins; paired wall `-0.246%` with 12/20 wins and `+0.226%` with 9/20
  wins.

The binary size is unchanged. Randomized single/dual horizontal/vertical
differentials, flat/step edge cases, and all 1,582 applicable release core
tests pass. The standard stream remains exactly 716,330 bytes with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.
Core all-target clippy passes with warnings denied, the debug should-panic
case passes separately, and formatting and diff checks pass.

Retain the single aggregate exit as a small exact Pareto improvement. Commit
it atomically, then profile the committed binary before selecting another
operation.

## Unit 188 — accept direct ONE-token coefficient replay

The committed Unit 187 profile contains 237 self samples across retained
coefficient replay and its coefficient writers. Instruction-level attribution
put 22 replay samples on the nonzero call path into the large general writer.
Temporary counters over the standard 120-frame cpu-used 7 workload classified
804,738 replayed nonzero coefficients: 680,337 (`84.54%`) were `+1` or `-1`.

Pinned libvpx's `pack_mb_tokens` handles `ONE_TOKEN` directly, writing the
one-context decision and sign without entering its coefficient tree. Rvp9's
retained records store raw signed values, so the accepted replay path detects
`+1` and `-1`, emits those same two boolean decisions inline, and calls the
shared general writer for every other magnitude. The temporary counters are
fully removed.

The retained-versus-direct oracle covers both signs, zero runs, every
coefficient category, and different pre/post probability tables. The standard
product stream remains exactly 716,330 bytes with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.

Four twenty-pair comparisons against committed Unit 187 favor the candidate:

- one thread: total child CPU `-0.554%` with 15/20 wins, then `-0.116%`
  with 12/20 wins; paired wall `-0.595%` and `-0.275%`;
- four threads: total child CPU `-0.559%` with 16/20 wins, then `-0.401%`
  with 13/20 wins; paired wall `-0.269%` and `-0.301%`.

Retain the exact common-token path. Run the focused and full core gates,
commit it atomically, then profile the committed binary before choosing the
next operation.

The release binary size is unchanged at 1,549,992 bytes. All 1,582 applicable

and the release form of the debug-only should-panic case. That should-panic
case passes separately in debug mode. Core all-target clippy passes with

## Unit 189 — reject paired arithmetic-coder state

The committed Unit 188 profile is `/tmp/rvp9-unit188-profile-v1.json.gz`,
with symbols in `/tmp/rvp9-unit188-profile-v1.json.syms.json` and analysis in
`/tmp/rvp9-unit188-profile-analysis.txt`. It contains 3,481 resolved rvp9 self
samples. The out-of-line general coefficient writer fell from Unit 187's 37
samples to 18, confirming Unit 188's intended mechanism. The inline ONE-token
boolean writes are now attributed to replay itself.

An exact candidate factored the arithmetic coder's state transition into an
always-inlined helper and added a two-symbol entry that kept `count`, `range`,
and `low_value` live across the ONE-context decision and sign bit. A dedicated
512-pair oracle covered both bit values and probabilities 1 through 255; it
matched two independent writes byte for byte. The standard product stream
also remained exactly 716,330 bytes with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.
Generated replay text shrank by 32 bytes, and total binary size was unchanged.

The whole-product effect did not justify refactoring the central arithmetic
coder. Two valid one-thread trials changed total child CPU by `-0.145%` and
`-0.635%`, but wall changed `+0.276%` and `-0.658%`. Two four-thread trials
changed child CPU by `-0.233%` and `-0.240%`, while wall split `-0.200%` and
`+0.170%`. A later single-thread run occurred under severe shared load (wall
rose from about 0.7 seconds to 1.48 seconds) and is discarded. The complete
source refactor and its temporary test are removed.

Do not batch arithmetic-coder state again without a materially larger symbol
burst and a direct wall-time result. The next experiment should stop spending
the remaining budget on sub-percent kernels: the strict Stage 4a curve still
beats libvpx by `-8.585847331540%`, while the last matched speed check was far
outside the final gate. Attribute one search-breadth difference inside
`pick_and_simulate_inter_encode_partitions` against libvpx's cpu-used 7
realtime pruning, then trade only measured excess quality for a large speed
gain under the unchanged corpus BD-rate gate.

## Unit 190 — reject replacing A4 with the variance partition tree

The first architecture-level A/B used the existing
`RVP9_A4_RD_PARTITION_DISABLE=1` diagnostic to restore the source-faithful
cpu-used 7 variance tree and skip rvp9's speculative picker descent. This was
decisively slower on the 1280x704 120-frame product workload: twenty
four-thread pairs regressed wall by `+58.175%` with 0/20 wins and total child
CPU by `+79.707%` with 0/20 wins. The variance tree creates enough smaller
final leaves that their emit work costs far more than the avoided descent.

`RVP9_A4_PROFILE=1` explains the accepted path. Large-frame recovery already
clamps descent to 32x32/64x64, and every committed leaf reuses the identical
descent pick (`same == committed`); the separately timed final picker is zero.
Per-tile descent is roughly 0.4--0.8 ms and emit roughly 0.2--0.4 ms on the
profiled frames. A4 is therefore both a partition decision and the only mode
pick for committed leaves, not a simple duplicate pass.

Keep A4. Do not retest the pure variance tree unless its leaf-size distribution
changes first. The next bounded search-breadth hypothesis is the existing
cpu-used 7 large-frame ML search breakout: it is default-on only when
`base_qindex >= 100`, leaving the high-bitrate speed workload unpruned. Add a
diagnostic all-q override, measure its fire rate and large-frame speed, and run
a large-frame quality comparison before considering any default change.

## Unit 191 — reject wider ML-breakout activation

The standard 2000 kbps workload actually runs at `base_qindex = 255`, so the
source-faithful `base_qindex >= 100` ML gate is already active. An all-q
diagnostic produced the identical 716,330-byte stream and neutral twenty-pair
timing. Threshold sweeps from the current `-20` through `-1000` also left the
stream and partition-call distribution unchanged.

An instrumented two-frame run at the deliberately extreme `-3e38` threshold
proved the model receives finite real inputs and returns `true`. The apparent
inertia comes from `variance_hint_bounds`: many superblocks have
`max_partition_size = 32x32`. Their 64x64 NONE arm is disallowed, so breakout
cannot fire until the four 32x32 floor nodes, where there is no recursion left
to suppress. On 64x64-eligible blocks the existing `-20` policy has already
captured the useful breakouts. The extreme policy regressed a twenty-pair
four-thread comparison by `+0.086%` wall and `+0.515%` total child CPU.

The all-q gate, temporary input logging, and forced activation are fully
removed. Do not retune the ML threshold without first changing which parent
sizes are eligible; the model is not the active limit on this workload.

## Unit 192 — reject expanding large-frame maximum partition size

The next candidate let large-frame recovery raise every variance-derived
maximum to 64x64. This gave A4 one 64x64 NONE choice before considering four
32x32 children. The two-frame picker profile changed from 162 64x64 plus 232
32x32 square calls to 220 64x64 plus 24 32x32 calls, but 64x64 mode evaluation
is sufficiently more expensive that the candidate lost overall.

With rectangular search enabled, the 120-frame stream shrank from 716,330 to
708,575 bytes, but twenty four-thread pairs regressed wall by `+10.210%` with
0/20 wins and total child CPU by `+15.098%` with 0/20 wins. Disabling
rectangular candidates reduced that cost and produced a 710,324-byte stream,
but still regressed wall by `+1.257%` with 3/20 wins and child CPU by `+3.009%`
with 1/20 wins. Both forms are removed.

Keep the variance-derived maximum and the large-frame 32x32 floor. The next
architecture experiment should preserve this proven partition shape and prune
one expensive mode/reference family inside its picker. Start with the existing
`RVP9_RT_DISABLE_GOLDEN` diagnostic, because 32x32+ already excludes NEARMV
while LAST/GOLDEN reference duplication remains reachable.

## Unit 193 — reject disabling the GOLDEN reference

The existing `RVP9_RT_DISABLE_GOLDEN=1` diagnostic removed GOLDEN-reference
mode evaluation without changing source. On the standard 1280x704, 120-frame,
cpu-used 7 VBR workload it reduced the IVF from 716,330 to 714,464 bytes. Two
twenty-pair four-thread comparisons improved wall by `-1.232%` and `-1.561%`,
and total child CPU by `-1.807%` with 18/20 wins and `-2.319%` with 16/20
wins. This confirms duplicate reference evaluation is a material cost.

The rate-distortion result rejects the shortcut. At the 2,000 kbps VBR point,
average PSNR fell from 31.935117 dB to 31.893268 dB while bitrate fell only
0.2605%. More importantly, the repository's six-point CBR oracle at target
rates 500, 750, 1,000, 1,500, 2,000, and 3,000 kbps reports `+20.008%`
BD-rate for GOLDEN-off versus the committed encoder. The curve is stored at
`/tmp/rvp9-unit193-golden-off-cbr.csv`; representative losses include 0.26 dB
at 500 kbps and 0.24 dB at 1,500 kbps.

Keep GOLDEN enabled. Do not trade an entire reference family for speed. The
next experiment should retain GOLDEN but avoid redundant work between LAST and
GOLDEN when their predictors or reference regions are demonstrably identical,
or prune only a measured block-size/mode combination and gate it with a full
curve before changing the default.

## Unit 194 — reject skipping physically aliased GOLDEN

A narrower diagnostic masked GOLDEN only when LAST and GOLDEN resolved to the
same physical ref-pool slot. This occurs after a keyframe and on the frame
following each eight-frame GOLDEN refresh. Unlike Unit 193, the candidate was

716,330-byte IVF with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.

The saved candidate work is too sparse to matter. Five twenty-pair,
four-thread comparisons reported paired total-child-CPU changes of `-0.504%`,

neutral. Paired wall changes were mixed (`-0.262%`, `+0.091%`, `+0.037%`,
`+0.246%`, `-0.014%`). The diagnostic and its per-frame pointer check are
removed.

Do not revisit frame-level alias masking. The next reference-search experiment
must target a candidate class that runs on most frames. Measure GOLDEN winner
and candidate counts by block size and mode first, then prune only a class that
is both expensive and rarely decisive; use the full CBR curve before any
default change.

## Units 195--199 — reject GOLDEN NEWMV and motion-search shortcuts

An environment-gated picker profile over all 120 standard-product frames
recorded 60,664 picker calls. Block32x32 accounted for 46,272 calls and 37,086
GOLDEN candidate evaluations; GOLDEN won 6,260 of them (3,937 NEAREST and 2,323
NEW). Block64x64 accounted for 14,392 calls but only 64 GOLDEN evaluations and
13 GOLDEN winners. GOLDEN's active cost and value are therefore concentrated
in 32x32 leaves. The raw profile is
`/tmp/rvp9-unit195-golden-profile-full.txt`.

Unit 195 removed only GOLDEN NEWMV. It improved a twenty-pair four-thread run
by `-2.058%` wall and `-3.481%` total child CPU, but average PSNR collapsed from
31.935117 dB to 31.082530 dB at the matched 2,000 kbps VBR point. The IVF
shrinking from 716,330 to 713,823 bytes does not compensate for the 0.852587 dB
loss. Reject without spending a full curve on a clearly invalid operating
point.

The existing predicted-MV SAD mask cannot safely separate these winners. Of
50,817 profile calls with a finite nonzero LAST SAD, tightening the current
2.0x GOLDEN/LAST cutoff to 1.875x would newly mask only 382 calls while
rejecting 15 observed GOLDEN winners. A 1.5x cutoff would mask 1,918 calls and
reject 164 winners. Do not retune this threshold from this corpus.

Unit 196 reused LAST's already-computed NEWMV as GOLDEN's NEWMV while retaining
the independent GOLDEN RD evaluation. It improved wall by `-1.196%` and child
CPU by `-1.346%` with 20/20 user-time wins, but lost 0.450240 dB average PSNR;
the IVF was 711,566 bytes. Independent GOLDEN motion is required.

Unit 197 reduced the existing `RVP9_RT_NEWMV_SUBPEL_ITERS` diagnostic from two
checks per level to one. It lost 0.265160 dB while timing was neutral
(`-0.190%` wall, `-0.115%` child CPU). Unit 198 forced the existing fast-diamond
variant; it was byte-exact and timing-neutral (`-0.073%` wall, `+0.025%` child
CPU). Unit 199 capped full-pixel diamond depth at 8, 6, 4, 2, and 0; every form
was byte-exact. Two default-versus-zero comparisons were neutral to slower
(`+0.200%`/`+0.130%` wall and `+0.041%`/`+0.023%` child CPU). These controls do
not remove active work on this workload.

All temporary profiling, pruning, and motion-reuse code is removed. Keep the
independent LAST/GOLDEN searches and the two-level sub-pixel refinement. The
next experiment should return to the committed profile and target an actually
hot implementation kernel rather than removing search breadth whose quality
value is now measured.

## Unit 200 — reject transform-sized DCT scratch arrays

`reconstruct_via_round_trip` allocated 1,024-element coefficient, quantized,
and dequantized arrays even for Tx8x8 and Tx16x16. A byte-exact rewrite used
64-, 256-, or 1,024-element arrays selected with the transform, removed one
copy, and reduced the release binary by 16 bytes. The standard stream remained
exactly 716,330 bytes with the committed SHA-256.

Whole-product timing rejected the apparent cleanup. Two twenty-pair,
four-thread comparisons regressed child CPU by `+0.642%` and `+0.319%`, with
only 5/20 and 8/20 wins. Wall regressed `+0.617%` and `+0.242%`. LLVM's
existing stack/lifetime optimization is more effective than the specialized
control flow on the product workload. The rewrite is fully removed.

Do not infer stack-zeroing cost from source array extents. The next
implementation candidate should remove a measured operation or route an
existing scalar kernel through proven SIMD, then verify at whole-product level.

## Unit 201 — reject picker EOB-bucketed inverse dispatch

The picker reconstruction was routed through the same EOB buckets and inverse
dispatch used by the final encoder/decoder (`1`, `10`/`12`, `34`/`38`, `135`,
then dense). The standard stream remained byte-exact, showing the candidate did
not perturb decisions on this workload.

Timing was consistently worse. Two twenty-pair four-thread comparisons
regressed total child CPU by `+0.901%` and `+0.232%`, and wall by `+1.053%` and
`+0.488%`. The candidate profile at
`/tmp/rvp9-unit201-profile-analysis.txt` still attributes samples to dense
`idct32`/`idct32x32_1024_add` and shows no picker sparse-kernel symbol. This
path's nonzero blocks are not entering useful sparse buckets often enough to
pay for dispatch.

The inverse-dispatch rewrite is fully removed. Do not add per-round-trip EOB
dispatch without a measured sparse-bucket population on the target corpus.

## Unit 202 — reject fixed-stack emitted dqcoeff return

`inter_forward_quant_one_tx` normally widens its 1,024-element i16 dqcoeff
scratch into a short-lived `Vec<i32>` for the final inverse transform. A
byte-exact candidate returned the fixed i16 array directly and widened only
the active transform prefix into stack scratch at the inverse call, removing
one heap allocation per nonzero transform. Binary size and the standard stream
were unchanged.

Two twenty-pair four-thread comparisons regressed child CPU by `+0.731%` and
`+0.655%`, with only 3/20 and 4/20 wins. Wall changed `+0.366%` and `+0.205%`.
The fixed 2 KB return/move and 4 KB inverse scratch cost more than the
short-lived vector allocation. The candidate is fully removed.

Keep the owned dqcoeff vector until profiling identifies allocator cost. Avoid
large fixed-array returns as an allocation substitute on this ABI.

## Units 203--204 — reject remaining partition/filter breadth switches

The refreshed matched standard-product timing is still far outside parity:
twenty pairs put rvp9 at `2.3407x` libvpx wall and `1.8421x` total child CPU
(rvp9 0.5970 s wall / 1.0451 s CPU; libvpx 0.2552 s / 0.5695 s). This is an
architectural CPU-work gap, not measurement noise.

Unit 203 disabled rectangular A4 partition candidates on the existing
large-frame 32x32 floor. The stream was byte-exact, and timing was neutral to
slower (`+0.194%` wall, `+0.368%` child CPU). The active ML rect pruner already
removes those arms. A two-frame `RVP9_A4_PROFILE=1 RVP9_RT_PROFILE=1` trace
also shows every large-frame A4 pick becomes a committed leaf: final picker
time is zero, and the per-size call counts exactly equal committed counts.
There is no duplicate final mode pick left to remove.

The full 120-frame picker profile records 86,581 LAST and 37,150 GOLDEN
candidate evaluations across 60,664 leaf picks, only 2.04 candidates per pick
on average. Mode-count pruning is already tight.

Unit 204 forced every candidate to use EightTap without interpolation-filter
search. It improved wall by `-0.485%` and child CPU by `-0.794%`, but average
PSNR fell from 31.935117 dB to 31.495740 dB and the IVF shrank from 716,330 to
710,986 bytes. The 0.439377 dB loss decisively rejects the switch.

The temporary filter-search override is removed. Do not expect another search
breadth toggle to close the measured 1.84x CPU gap: the remaining candidates,
references, and filters have all shown material quality value. Investigate
whole-program code generation or a more faithful lower-cost non-RD model next.

## Unit 205 — reject single-workload profile-guided optimization

An external release build used Rust/LLVM profile generation, trained on the
standard 120-frame cpu-used 7 VBR workload, merged the raw counters with Apple
LLVM 21 `llvm-profdata`, and rebuilt with `-C profile-use`. No repository or
release-profile setting changed. The optimized binary produced the exact
716,330-byte stream with the committed SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.

Two twenty-pair comparisons found a repeatable child-CPU improvement of
`-1.639%` and `-1.902%`, but wall time regressed `+1.368%` and `+0.742%`.
The training run also reported exhausted static counter storage, and the use
build identified broad missing-function coverage outside the one trained
configuration. This is neither an end-to-end speed win nor representative
training for the required feature matrix, so a production PGO pipeline would
add build complexity without a measured acceptance benefit.

The external candidate and profile remain under `/tmp`; the worktree is
unchanged. Do not adopt PGO from this single workload. The next experiment
should isolate the unusually high allocation/system-CPU share or remove a hot
per-transform allocation without returning large fixed arrays.

## Unit 206 — retain coefficient-domain picker distortion

Stage 4a VBR temporarily disabled its S3 coefficient-domain distortion, which
routes the AArch64 Hadamard picker through the rate-only quantization kernel.
The candidate improved wall by `-4.328%` and child CPU by `-4.680%`, winning
all 20 paired samples. The saving is real, but the quality cost is prohibitive:
the standard stream fell from 716,330 to 708,362 bytes and average PSNR fell
from 31.935117 to 29.523095 dB (`-2.412022 dB`).

The one-line policy change is fully removed. S3's quantization-error metric is
a measured quality requirement, not optional diagnostic work. A lower-cost
model must approximate that metric closely enough to preserve its candidate
ranking; simply reverting to pixel-domain prediction SSE spends far more than
the available quality margin.

## Unit 207 — reject sparse Hadamard quantization-error correction

An AArch64 candidate rewrote coefficient error as unquantized Hadamard energy
plus `dq * (dq - 2*c)` correction. The unquantized term came from the already
available prediction SSE using the transform's energy gain; all-zero groups
could then bypass the 64-bit correction accumulator. Differential quantizer
tests passed. The standard product stream also stayed byte-exact at 716,330
bytes, confirming that the small 16x16/32x32 integer-shift approximation did
not change this workload's decisions.

The extra per-group nonzero reduction erased the intended saving. Two
twenty-pair comparisons regressed child CPU by `+0.936%` and `+0.105%`, with
8/20 and 9/20 wins; wall changed `+0.704%` and `+0.407%`. The complete
candidate is removed. The existing four independent 64-bit accumulators are
already the faster implementation on this Apple AArch64 core.

## Unit 208 — retain per-tile reconstruction and MV scratch

Matched thread-count diagnostics showed the gap grows with tile parallelism:
the one-thread cpu-used 7 comparison was `1.673x` total CPU and `1.670x` wall,
while two threads reached `1.793x` CPU and `2.594x` wall. The parallel rvp9
path allocated and zeroed a full-frame YUV reconstruction plus MV grid for
every column tile on every inter frame.

`StreamEncoder` now owns one frame-shaped scratch set per configured column
tile and reuses it across frames. Jobs still own disjoint active rectangles;
the merge reads only those rectangles, so stale pixels outside a job's tile
are unobservable. The four-tile deterministic regression now checks both
thread-count byte identity and stable scratch allocation addresses across
consecutive inter frames.

The standard stream remains byte-exact at 716,330 bytes with SHA-256
`8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.
Two twenty-pair comparisons reduced system CPU by `-15.760%` and `-15.797%`,
total child CPU by `-1.377%` and `-0.947%`, and wall by `-0.281%` and
`-0.535%`. Focused test and encoder-library clippy both pass. Keep the change.

The next tile-overhead target is the four full-frame nested partition/history
clones per frame. Any replacement must preserve independent per-column RD and
partition history state and the existing thread-count byte identity.

## Unit 209 — reject active-tile-only MV-grid clearing

With retained tile scratch in place, each tile job still reset every cell in
its frame-shaped MV grid before writing only its active columns. Restricting
the reset to those columns was byte-exact and passed the four-tile worker-count
regression. One twenty-pair run improved wall/CPU by `-0.653%`/`-0.816%`, but
the repeat under higher host load was neutral (`-0.036%` wall, `+0.115%` CPU;
8/20 CPU wins). The extra row-slice loop does not provide a reproducible
whole-product improvement over the contiguous full-grid fill.

The change is removed. Keep the contiguous clear; target allocation and deep
clone costs rather than subdividing this small linear write.

## Unit 210 — retain active-tile-only partition and history clones

Every column-tile job deep-cloned the leaf vectors for every superblock in the
frame, although it only mutates its own columns. It also cloned exact-VBP
partition history even though that experimental path is disabled by default.
Jobs now allocate empty inactive partition slots, clone only their active
superblock columns, and copy VBP history only when `RVP9_EXACT_VBP_TREE` is
enabled. The deterministic merge still copies only each job's active columns.

The standard cpu-used 7 VBR stream remains byte-exact at 716,330 bytes with
SHA-256 `8144dcf9d104119634855cf2ed0554f14e206137ad33689f5b0f871b95c2acb3`.
Two twenty-pair ABBA comparisons improved total child CPU by `-0.650%` and
`-0.716%`, with 18/20 and 16/20 wins; wall improved `-1.411%` and `-0.795%`,
with 19/20 and 18/20 wins. The four-tile regression passes for the default and
exact-VBP paths across one, two, and four workers, and encoder-library clippy
passes with warnings denied. Keep the change.

The remaining result merge clones each active partition vector a second time.
Move those vectors out of the completed tile result before attempting a larger
coefficient-source reuse change.

## Unit 211 — reject moving completed tile state during merge

Moving active partition vectors, exact-VBP history entries, and the active RD
table out of each completed tile result preserved the exact standard stream and
passed both four-tile worker-count regressions. It did not produce a stable
end-to-end speed win. Two twenty-pair comparisons measured wall at `-0.098%`
then `+0.271%`, and total child CPU at `+0.027%` then `-0.539%`; wins were
12/20 and 10/20 for wall, 10/20 and 11/20 for CPU. System CPU also changed
direction between runs.

The transfer is below measurement noise because the destination retains the
same allocations either way. The change is removed. Proceed to the larger
four-full-frame coefficient-source allocation and merge cost.
